<page-num>4</page-num>

<header>Henry Cornelius Agrippa</header>

Now we proceed to explain which planets are assigned to these figures; for upon this, all the properties and nature of the figures, and the judgment of the whole art, depend. Therefore, *Greater Fortune* and *Lesser Fortune* are ascribed to the Sun. The first, or *Greater Fortune*, occurs when the Sun is diurnal <note>original: "diurnall"; active during the day</note> and positioned in his dignities <note>original: "dignities"; astrological positions where a planet's influence is strengthened</note>. The other, or *Lesser Fortune*, occurs when the Sun is nocturnal <note>original: "nocturnall"; active during the night</note> or placed in lesser dignities. *Via* and *Populus* (that is, the Way and the People) are referred to the Moon; the first from her beginning and waxing <note>original: "encreasing"</note>, and the second from her full light and waning <note>original: "quarter decreasing"</note>. *Acquisitio* and *Laetitia* (which mean Gain or Profit, and Joy or Gladness) belong to **Jupiter**. However, the first has Jupiter as a greater fortune, while the second has him as a lesser fortune, but without any negative effect <note>original: "detriment"</note>. *Puella* and *Amissio* belong to **Venus**; the first is fortunate, while the other is (as it were) retrograde <note>appearing to move backward</note> or combust <note>so close to the Sun that its influence is weakened</note>. *Conjunctio* and *Albus* are both figures of **Mercury** and are both good, but the first is the more fortunate. *Puer* and *Rubeus* are figures ascribed to **Mars**; in the first, Mars is benevolent <note>kind or favorable</note>, while in the second, he is malevolent <note>harmful or unfavorable</note>. *Carcer* and *Tristitia* are both figures of **Saturn** and are both evil, though the first causes the greater harm <note>original: "detriment"</note>. The Dragon’s Head and Dragon’s Tail follow their own specific natures.

And these are the reliable comparisons of the figures, and from these, we may easily discern the correspondences <note>original: "equality"</note> of their signs. Therefore, *Greater* and *Lesser Fortune* have the sign of **Leo**, which is the House of the Sun. *Via* and *Populus* have the sign of **Cancer**, which is the House of the Moon. *Acquisitio* has the sign of **Pisces**, and *Laetitia* has **Sagittarius**, both of which are the Houses of Jupiter. *Puella* has the sign of **Taurus**, and *Amissio* has **Libra**, which are the Houses of Venus. *Conjunctio* has the sign of **Virgo**, and *Albus* has the sign of **Gemini**, which are the Houses of Mercury. *Puer* and *Rubeus* have the sign of **Scorpio**, the House of Mars. *Carcer* has the sign of **Capricorn**, and *Tristitia* has **Aquarius**, which are the Houses of Saturn. The Dragon’s Head and Tail are divided thus: the Head belongs to **Capricorn**, and the Dragon’s Tail is attached to **Scorpio**. From this, you may easily determine the triplicities <note>original: "triplicities"; groups of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element</note> of these signs, following the pattern of the triplicities of the signs of the Zodiac. Therefore, *Puer*, both *Fortunes*, and *Laetitia* govern the fiery triplicity. *Puella*, *Conjunctio*, *Carcer*, and the Dragon’s Head govern the
->earthly<-

<summary>Agrippa details the astrological foundations of geomancy by assigning each of the sixteen figures to a specific planet, zodiac sign, and elemental triplicity. These correspondences are essential for interpreting the "judgment" or meaning of a geomantic reading.</summary>

<keywords>geomancy, astrology, planetary dignities, zodiac signs, triplicities, Greater Fortune, Lesser Fortune, Dragon's Head, Dragon's Tail, Henry Cornelius Agrippa</keywords>

--- Page 43 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical categorization of geomantic figures, specifically how they are grouped into the four elements (the triplicities) and assigned to the twelve signs of the zodiac, before introducing the method for generating a geomantic chart.</meta>

<page-num>5</page-num>

## of Geomancy.

...earthly triplicity <note>original: "triplicity"; a group of three (or sometimes four in geomancy) astrological signs or figures belonging to the same element</note>. *Albus* (White), *Amitia* <note>original: "Amitia"; likely a variation of *Amissio* (Loss) or *Amicitia* (Friendship)</note>, and *Tristitia* (Sorrow) make the airy triplicity. *Via* (The Way), *Populus* (The People), and *Rubeus* (Red), along with the Dragon’s Tail and *Acquisitio* (Gain), rule the watery triplicity. This order is determined according to the sequence and nature of the zodiac signs.

But if anyone wishes to organize these triplicities according to the natures of the planets and the figures themselves, let them observe this rule: *Fortuna Major* (Greater Fortune), *Rubeus* (Red), *Puer* (The Boy), and *Amissio* (Loss) make the fiery triplicity. *Fortuna Minor* (Lesser Fortune), *Puella* (The Girl), *Laetitia* (Joy), and *Conjunctio* (Conjunction) make the triplicity of the Air. *Acquisitio* (Gain), the Dragon’s Tail, *Via* (The Way), and *Populus* (The People) govern the watery triplicity. Finally, the earthly triplicity is ruled by *Carcer* (The Prison), *Tristitia* (Sorrow), *Albus* (White), and the Dragon’s Head. This method is to be preferred over the first one we presented because it is organized according to the rules and patterns of the zodiac signs.

This order is also far more accurate and logical than the one commonly <note>original: "vulgarly"</note> used, which is described in this way: the fiery triplicity consists of the Dragon's Tail (*Cauda*), *Fortuna Minor*, *Amissio*, and *Rubeus*. The airy triplicity consists of *Acquisitio*, *Laetitia*, *Puer*, and *Conjunctio*. The watery triplicity consists of *Populus*, *Via*, *Albus*, and *Puella*. And the Dragon's Head (*Caput*), *Fortuna Major*, *Carcer*, and *Tristitia* belong to the earthly triplicity.

They likewise distribute these figures to the twelve signs of the Zodiac in this manner: *Acquisitio* (Gain) is given to **Aries**; *Fortuna* (Fortune), both *Major* (Greater) and *Minor* (Lesser), to **Taurus**; *Laetitia* (Joy) to the sign **Gemini**; *Puella* (The Girl) and *Rubeus* (Red) to **Cancer**; *Albus* (White) is assigned to **Leo**; *Via* (The Way) to **Virgo**; the Dragon’s Head and *Conjunctio* (Conjunction) to **Libra**; *Puer* (The Boy) is assigned to **Scorpio**; *Tristitia* (Sorrow) and *Amissio* (Loss) are assigned to **Sagittarius**; the Dragon’s Tail to **Capricorn**; *Populus* (The People) to **Aquarius**; and *Carcer* (The Prison) is assigned the sign **Pisces**.

And now we come to speak of the method of "projecting" or setting down these figures. It is done like this: we set down points in four lines, moving from the right hand toward the left, and we do this in four sequences <note>original: "courses"</note>. This will result in four figures made in four separate lines, based on whether the marks in each line are even or odd. These four figures are usually called the *Matres* (Mothers), which give birth to the rest, filling up and completing the entire "Figure of Judgment" <note>the final chart used for divination</note>.

From these four Mothers <note>original: *Matres*</note> are also produced four other secondary figures, which they call Daughters <note>original: *Filiæ*</note>, or Succedents <note>In astrology and geomancy, "succedent" refers to things that follow or result from the primary positions</note>. These are assembled in this way: the four Mothers are arranged in their proper order, placing them side-by-side in a row. Then, the figures of the Daughters are formed from the horizontal lines of the Mothers. The order is determined by reading across the rows, starting from the top points, moving through both middle sections, and ending at the lowest points, as shown in this example.

<image-desc>A rectangular diagram divided horizontally into two sections. The top section is labeled "Mothers." <note>original: *Matres*</note> and contains four geomantic figures side-by-side. The third consists of one star, two stars, two stars, and one star. The fourth consists of one star, one star, one star, and one star. The bottom section is labeled "Daughters produced." <note>original: *Filiæ produced*</note> and contains four geomantic figures: the first consists of a triangle of three stars, one star, one star, and two stars; the second consists of two stars, two stars, two stars, and one star; the third consists of one star, one star, two stars, and one star; and the fourth consists of one star, one star, one star, and one star.</image-desc>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for constructing a geomantic chart. Agrippa describes how to generate the twelve "Houses" of the chart, along with the "Witnesses" and the final "Index" (the Judge), by combining the initial eight figures.</meta>

<page-num>7</page-num>
<header>of Geomancy.</header>

These eight figures create the eight Houses of Heaven <note>In geomancy and astrology, these "houses" represent different areas of life, such as wealth, health, or relationships</note> in the following way: by placing the figures from the left hand towards the right. Just as the four Mothers <note>original: *Matres*</note> make up the first four Houses, the four Daughters <note>original: *Filiæ*</note> make up the following four Houses (the fifth, sixth, seventh, and eighth). 

The remaining Houses are found in this manner: the ninth is derived from the first and second; the tenth from the third and fourth; the eleventh from the fifth and sixth; and the twelfth from the seventh and eighth. This is done by combining or joining together two figures according to the rule of even or odd numbers in the corresponding points of each figure <note>To "join" figures, you count the points in the matching rows of two figures; if the sum is even, the new figure gets two points (a bar) in that row; if the sum is odd, it gets one point (a dot)</note>. 

In the same way, two more figures are produced from the last four (the ninth, tenth, eleventh, and twelfth). These are called the Witnesses <note>original: *Coadjutrices* or *Testes*</note>. From these two witnesses, a final figure is formed, which is called the Index <note>Also known as the "Judge" in other geomantic traditions</note> of the entire chart, or the answer to the matter being asked about <note>original: *quesited*</note>, as shown in the following example.

### A Theme of Geomancy.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for constructing a geomantic chart. Agrippa describes how to generate the twelve "Houses" of the chart, along with the "Witnesses" and the final "Index" (the Judge), by combining the initial eight figures.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>
<header>Henry Cornelius Agrippa,</header>

The method we have just described is the common way used by geomancers <note>Practitioners of geomancy, a method of divination using patterns of dots</note>. We neither completely reject nor praise this method; therefore, it should also be considered in our interpretations. However, I will now give you the true figure of geomancy, based on the correct principles of astrological reasoning, which is as follows.

Just as the first four figures—the Mothers <note>original: *Matres*</note>—make up the four "Angles" of a house <note>In astrology, the "Angles" or "Angular Houses" are the most powerful points of a chart: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>, the first Mother makes the first Angle, the second Mother makes the second Angle, the third makes the third Angle, and the fourth makes the fourth Angle. In the same way, the four Daughters <note>original: *Filiæ*</note> that arise from the Mothers make up the four "succedent" houses <note>The houses following the Angles: the 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses</note>. The first Daughter makes the second house, the second Daughter makes the eleventh, the third makes the eighth, and the fourth makes the fifth house <note>original OCR: "first House," likely a transcription error for "fifth," as the 5th is the remaining succedent house</note>. 

The rest of the houses, which are "cadent" <note>The remaining houses: the 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th</note>, are to be calculated according to the rule of their "triplicity" <note>A group of three; here referring to how houses are combined in groups</note>. That is to say, you create the ninth house out of the fourth and fifth; the sixth house out of the tenth and second; the third house out of the seventh and eleventh; and the twelfth house out of the fourth and eighth.

Now you have the entire figure for a true judgment, built according to valid and effective reasons. I will now show you how to complete it. The figure located in the first house will give you the "ascending sign" <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note>, which that first figure represents. Once this is done, you shall assign the appropriate zodiac signs to the rest of the houses according to their standard order. 

Next, in every house, you should note the planets according to the nature of the figure found there. From all of this, you shall build your interpretation based on the meaning of the planets in the signs and houses where they are found, as well as their "aspects" <note>The geometric angles between planets that indicate how they influence one another</note> to each other, and their relationship to the person asking the question <note>original: *querent*</note> and the matter being asked about <note>original: *quesited*</note>. You shall judge according to the nature of the signs rising in their houses, the qualities and properties of the figures placed in the various houses, and the mixture of other figures influencing them through aspects. This explains how the "Index" <note>The final summary figure or "Judge" of the reading</note> of the chart—which geomancers almost always use—is found in the previous method.

But here we shall give you the secret of the entire art: how to find the Index in the following figure. The method is this: you must count all the points contained in the lines of the projections <note>The marks or dots made during the initial divination process</note>,

# Of Geomancy

Divide this total by twelve, and take the remainder and count it out from the Ascendant <note>The 1st House, or the point where the zodiac signs begin in the chart</note> through the various houses. The house where the final count lands gives you a reliable judgment of the matter sought <term>quesited</term>; this should be used along with the meanings of the judgments mentioned before. But if the results on either side are equal or ambiguous, then the <term>Index</term> <note>The final figure or "Judge" of the geomantic chart</note> alone shall inform you about the matter sought. An example of this figure is placed here.

Agrippa explains how each house governs specific aspects of human life and the natural world, which allows the practitioner to interpret the geomantic figures based on their location in the chart.</meta>

...whenever a question is proposed concerning himself, his own affairs, or anything belonging to him. And this house provides judgment regarding life, appearance <note>original: "form"</note>, social standing, condition, physical habits, personality <note>original: "disposition"</note>, physique <note>original: "form and figure"</note>, and the complexion <note>original: "colour"</note> of people. 

The second house contains judgments about wealth <note>original: "substance"</note>, riches, poverty, gain and loss, good and bad fortune, and accidents involving property, such as theft, loss, or negligence. 

The third house signifies brothers, sisters, and blood relatives. It judges short journeys and the faithfulness of people. 

The fourth house signifies fathers and grandfathers, inheritance <note>original: "patrimony and inheritance"</note>, possessions, buildings, fields, treasure, and hidden things. It also gives descriptions of those who lack anything due to theft, loss, or negligence. 

The fifth house gives judgment regarding legacies <note>original: "Legats"</note>, messengers, rumors, and news; honors and events occurring after death; and questions that may be asked concerning pregnant women or animals. 

The sixth house gives judgment regarding illnesses <note>original: "infirmities"</note> and medicines; household members <note>original: "familiars"</note> and servants; and livestock and domestic animals. 

The seventh house signifies marriage, adultery <note>original: "whoredom"</note>, and sexual behavior <note>original: "fornication"</note>; it provides judgment on friends, conflicts, and controversies; and matters handled in court before judges. 

The eighth house signifies death and the things that come through death, such as legacies <note>original: "Legats"</note> and inheritances <note>original: "hereditaments"</note>; and the dowry or property brought by a wife. 

The ninth house shows long journeys, faith, and steadfastness; dreams, the sacred sciences <note>such as theology or alchemy</note>, and religion. 

The tenth house signifies honors and high-ranking public offices <note>original: "Magisterial Offices"</note>. 

The eleventh house signifies friends and the wealth <note>original: "substance"</note> of rulers or princes. 

The twelfth house signifies enemies, servants, imprisonment, and misfortune—essentially whatever evil can happen besides death and sickness (judgments for which are found in the sixth and eighth houses).

It now remains for us to show you what every figure mentioned previously signifies within these places, which we will now explain.

### Of Geomancy
<sig>C 2</sig>

<margin>The Greater Fortune.</margin>
The Greater Fortune <note>original: *Fortuna major*</note> being found in the first House, gives long life and frees one from the trouble <note>original: molestation</note> of diseases. It shows a man to be noble, generous <note>original: magnanimous</note>, well-mannered, of average height <note>original: mean of stature</note>, with a reddish <note>original: ruddy</note> complexion, curly hair, and his upper body <note>original: superiour members</note> larger than his lower body. In the second House, he signifies clear <note>original: manifest</note> riches and clear gain, good fortune, and the recovery of anything lost or mislaid; the capture of a thief, and the recovery of stolen goods. In the third House, he signifies brothers and kinsmen, nobles, and people of good character <note>original: conversation</note>; journeys will be prosperous and profitable with honor. It shows men to be faithful and their friendship to be sincere <note>original: unfeigned</note>. In the fourth House, he represents a father who is noble, reputable, and known by many people. He expands possessions in cities, increases inheritances <note>original: patrimonies</note>, and uncovers hidden treasures. In this place, he likewise signifies theft and the recovery of everything lost. In the fifth House, he gives joy through children and causes them to achieve great honors. He makes diplomatic missions <note>original: embassages</note> successful, though they are achieved with effort and petitions <note>original: pains and prayers</note>. He indicates that rumors are true. He bestows public honors and causes a man to be very famous after death. He predicts that a pregnant woman will give birth to a boy <note>original: man-childe</note>. In the sixth House, he frees one from diseases and shows that those who are sick <note>original: have infirmities</note> shall recover in a short time. He signifies a physician to be faithful and honest in administering good medicine <note>original: physick</note>, which should be trusted without suspicion. He signifies that household servants and attendants <note>original: ministers</note> will be faithful; and among animals, he chiefly signifies horses. In the seventh House, he gives a wife who is wealthy, honest, and well-mannered, loving and pleasant. He overcomes strife and conflict. But if the inquiry <note>original: Question</note> concerns those things, he signifies that the adversaries are very powerful and have influential supporters <note>original: great favourites</note>. In the eighth House, if a question is asked about the death of anyone, it signifies they shall live. He shows the kind of death to be good and natural, with a respectable burial and honorable funeral <note>original: funerals</note>. ...prosperous journeys. By land on horseback rather than on foot, he signifies the trip will be long and not quickly finished. He predicts dreams to be true and signifies true and perfect sciences. In the tenth House, he foretells great honors, bestowing public offices, magistrate positions <note>original: Magistracie</note>, and legal judgments, as well as honors in the courts of princes. He signifies judges to be just and not corrupted by bribes <note>original: gifts</note>. He brings a legal case <note>original: Cause</note> to be easily and quickly resolved <note>original: expedited</note>. He shows kings to be powerful, fortunate, and victorious, and denotes that victory is certain. He signifies a mother to be noble and long-lived. In the eleventh House, he signifies true and profitable friends and a prince who is rich and generous <note>original: liberal</note>. He makes a man fortunate and beloved by his prince. In the twelfth House, if a question is asked about the quality of enemies, it demonstrates them to be powerful and noble, and hard to resist. But if a question concerns any other condition or respect regarding enemies, he will deliver the seeker from their treacheries. It signifies faithful servants and the return of runaways <note>original: fugitives</note>. It represents animals such as horses, lions, and bulls. He frees people from imprisonment, and he either reduces or removes eminent dangers.

<margin>The Lesser Fortune.</margin>
The Lesser Fortune <note>original: *Fortuna minor*</note> in the first House gives long life, but one burdened <note>original: incumbred</note> with various troubles and sicknesses. It signifies a person of short stature and a lean body, having a mole <note>original: mold</note> or mark on his forehead or right eye. In the second House, he signifies wealth <note>original: substance</note> that will be consumed by excessive overspending <note>original: prodigality</note>. He hides a thief, and a stolen item is unlikely to be recovered except with great effort. In the third House, he causes discord among brothers and relatives. He threatens danger during a journey, though it will be escaped. He makes men faithful but of a secretive and hidden mind. In the fourth House, he harms <note>original: prejudiceth</note> patrimonies <note>original: inherited property</note> and inheritances. He conceals treasures, and lost things cannot be regained except with great difficulty. He signifies a father to be honest but a spender of his estate through overspending, leaving small portions...

# Of Geomancy

The Lesser Fortune <note>original: Fortuna minor</note> in the fifth House gives few children. If a woman is pregnant, it signifies she shall have a girl. It indicates that diplomatic missions <note>original: Embassages</note> will be honorable but bring little profit. It raises a person to modest honors and gives a good reputation after death, though it will not be widely known or of lasting memory. 

In the sixth House, it signifies diseases related to both the blood and yellow bile <note>original: Sanguine and Cholerick</note>. It shows that the sick person is in great danger but will eventually recover. It signifies faithful servants who are nonetheless lazy and unprofitable, and it relates to the reputation of other animals. 

In the seventh House, it provides a wife descended from a good family <note>original: progenie</note>, but you shall be burdened with many troubles because of her. It causes love to be anxious and inconsistent. It prolongs legal disputes and causes one's adversary to outmaneuver him with many legal quibbles <note>original: cavillations</note>; however, in the course of time, it brings victory. 

In the eighth House, it shows the manner of death to be good and honest, but obscure, or occurring in a strange place or during a pilgrimage. It reveals legacies and possessions, but they are to be obtained only through lawsuits and difficulty. It indicates that funerals and burials will be obscure. A wife’s dowry <note>original: portion</note> will be hard to get but easily spent. 

In the ninth House, it makes journeys dangerous and indicates that an absent person will return slowly. It causes men to be occupied in religious offices. It shows that sciences or studies will be incomplete <note>original: unaccomplished</note>, but it maintains consistency in faith and religion. 

In the tenth House, it signifies that kings and princes will be powerful but will gain their power through war and violence. It shows that banished men shall soon return. It likewise reveals honors, great offices, and benefits, but these are things for which you shall continually labor and strive, and in which you shall have no lasting stability. A judge will not favor you. It prolongs lawsuits and contentions. It shows that a father and mother shall die soon and will always be afflicted with many diseases. 

In the eleventh House, it provides many friends, but they are poor and unprofitable, unable to help you in your needs. It brings you into the favor of princes and gives great hopes but small gains; you will not continue for long in any benefit or office bestowed by a prince.

<margin>The Way <note>original: Via</note></margin>
*The Way* in the first House bestows a long and prosperous life. It signifies a stranger or foreigner: thin of body and tall of stature; fair-skinned, having a small beard. It represents a person who is generous and pleasant, but slow and not very inclined to hard work <note>original: addicted to labour</note>.

In the second House, it increases wealth and riches. It recovers anything that is stolen or lost, but indicates that the thief has already fled the city. 

In the third House, it multiplies brothers and relatives. It signifies continual and prosperous journeys, and men who are publicly known, honest, and of good behavior <note>original: conversation</note>. 

*The Way* in the fourth House signifies that the father is honest. It increases the family estate <note>original: Patrimony</note> and inheritance. It produces fertile fields and shows that treasure is in the place being asked about. It recovers anything that is lost. 

In the fifth House, it increases the number of male children; it shows a pregnant woman will bring forth a boy. It sends diplomatic missions <note>original: Embassages</note> to strange and remote parts. It increases public honors and signifies an honest kind of death that will be known throughout many provinces. 

In the sixth House, *The Way* preserves one from sickness and signifies that the diseased will recover quickly. It gives profitable servants, as well as productive and profitable animals. 

In the seventh House, it bestows a wife who is fair and pleasant, with whom you shall enjoy perpetual happiness. It causes disputes and legal battles to be resolved very quickly. It allows adversaries to be easily overcome, as they will willingly submit their disputes to the judgment <note>original: Arbitration</note> of honest men. 

In the eighth House, it shows the manner of death to proceed from phlegmatic diseases <note>original: Phlegmatick diseases; in the medical theory of the time, an excess of the "phlegm" humor was thought to cause sluggishness or respiratory issues</note>. It signifies a death that is honest and of good reputation. It reveals that great legacies and rich inheritances will be obtained from the dead. And if anyone has been reported as dead, it shows him to be alive.

In the second House, *Populus* <note>The People</note> shows a modest <note>original: mean</note> wealth, and one that is acquired with great difficulty. In the third House, *Populus* gains few friends, whether among siblings or relatives. It foretells journeys, but they will be filled with labor and trouble; nevertheless, some profit may come from them. It indicates a man who is unstable in his faith and causes a person to be often deceived by his companions. 

In the fourth House, it signifies a father to be sickly and of a life of hard work, and shows his earthly possessions and inheritances <note>original: patrimony</note> being taken away. It indicates profit to be gained by water. It shows treasure is not hidden; or if there is any hidden, it shall not be found. It suggests a family inheritance is to be preserved only with great labor. 

In the fifth House, he shows no honorable messages, but instead makes the messengers act as laborers <note>original: Porters</note> or public couriers <note>original: Carryers</note>. He spreads false rumors, which nevertheless have the appearance of some truth and seem to originate from truth, though they are not reported as they actually happened. It signifies a woman to be barren and causes those who are pregnant to miscarry <note>original: be abortives</note>. It ordains an inglorious funeral and a bad reputation after death. 

In the sixth House, *Populus* shows "cold" illnesses <note>According to the medical theory of the four humors, diseases were classified by temperature and moisture</note>, and chiefly affects the lower parts of the body. It notes the deceitfulness of servants and the loss of livestock <note>original: cattel</note>. 

In the seventh House, it shows a wife to be beautiful and pleasant, but one who shall be courted <note>original: solicited</note> by the love of many suitors. It signifies her feelings of love to be faked and deceptive. It makes weak and powerless adversaries soon give up their prosecution.

### *Acquisitio*
->Gain<-

*Acquisitio* <note>Latin: "Acquisition" or "Gain"</note> found in the first House, gives a long life and a prosperous old age. It signifies a man of average height <note>original: middle stature</note> and a large head, with a very distinctive face, a long nose, a full beard, curling hair, and beautiful eyes. He is generous with his food and drink, but in all other things, he is sparing and not liberal. 

In the second House, it signifies very great riches, catches all thieves, and causes whatever is lost to be recovered. 

In the third House, it signifies many siblings, and that they shall be wealthy; it indicates many profitable journeys and represents a man of good faith. 

In the fourth House, it signifies an inheritance <note>original: Patrimony</note> of great wealth and many possessions with abundant harvests <note>original: copious fruits</note>. It signifies that treasure hidden in any place shall be found, and shows a father to be rich but greedy <note>original: covetous</note>.

[In the fifth House, *Acquisitio*] signifies many children of both sexes, but more males than females; it shows a woman to be with child and that she shall be delivered without danger. In the seventh House, it signifies a wife will be rich, but either a widow or a woman of mature age; it signifies that lawsuits and contentions will be great and long-lasting, and that love and marriage shall be determined by fate <note>original: "lot"</note>. It signifies the kind of death will be quick and the sickness will last only a few days; it discovers very profitable legacies and inheritances and signifies a wife will have a rich dowry. In the ninth House, it signifies long and profitable journeys; it shows that if anyone is absent, they shall soon return. It causes gain to be obtained from religious and ecclesiastical persons or scholars and signifies a man of true and perfect knowledge <note>original: "Science"</note>. In the tenth House, it makes princes enlarge their dominions; it shows a judge to be favorable, but one who must be continually presented with gifts. It causes official positions and government roles <note>term: magistracy; the office or power of a government administrator or judge</note> to be very profitable; it signifies a mother who is rich and happy. In the eleventh House, *Acquisitio* <note>original: Acquisitio; Latin for "Gain" or "Acquisition"</note> multiplies friends, brings profit from them, and increases favor with princes. In the twelfth House, it signifies a man shall have many powerful or potent enemies; it recovers and brings home runaway servants and strayed cattle; and it signifies that he who is in prison shall not be released.

<margin>Maid.</margin>
*Puella* <note>original: "The Maid"</note> in the first House signifies a person of a short life, weak constitution of body, middle stature, slightly fat <note>original: "little fat"</note>, but fair, effeminate, and luxurious; it describes one who will incur many troubles and dangers in his lifetime for the love of women. In the third House, *Puella* signifies more sisters than brothers <note>original: "brethren"</note>, and increases and continues good friendship and harmony <note>original: "amity"</note> among them; it denotes journeys to be pleasant and joyous, and men of good character <note>original: "conversations"</note>. In the seventh House, *Puella* gives a wife who is fair, beautiful, and pleasant, who maintains a peaceful and quiet conduct <note>original: "conversation"</note> with her husband—notwithstanding she is one who shall burn much with lust and be coveted and lusted after by many men. It denotes no lawsuits <note>original: "suits"</note> or controversies that shall depend before a judge, but some jars and bickering <note>original: "wranglings"</note> among the common people, which shall be easily resolved and ended. In the ninth House, *Puella* <note>original: "The Maid"</note> signifies very few journeys; it shows a person of good religion and average <note>original: "indifferent"</note> skill or knowledge in the sciences, unless perhaps <note>original: "happily"</note> in music, both vocal and instrumental. In the tenth House, *Puella* signifies that princes will not be very powerful <note>original: "potent"</note>, but they shall nevertheless govern peaceably within their dominions and shall be beloved by their neighbors and subjects. It causes them to be affable, mild, and courteous, and ensures they will always occupy themselves with continual mirth, plays, and hunting. It makes judges good, godly, and merciful, and grants offices related to women, especially those granted by noblewomen. In the eleventh House, *Puella* gives many friends and increases favor with women. In the twelfth House, *Puella* signifies few enemies, but conflict <note>original: "contention"</note> with women; it delivers prisoners out of prison through the intercession of friends.

<margin>Loss.</margin>
*Amissio* <note>Latin: "Loss"</note> in the first House signifies that the sick will not live long and shows a short life. It signifies a person with disproportionate body parts <note>original: "members"</note> and one of a wicked life and behavior <note>original: "conversation"</note>, who is marked with some notorious and remarkable defect in some part of their body, such as being lame, maimed, or the like. *Amissio* in the second House consumes all wealth <note>original: "substance"</note> and makes one suffer and undergo the burden of miserable poverty. Neither the thief nor the stolen thing shall be found. It signifies that treasure is not in the place being searched, and searching for it will lead to loss and damage. In the third House, *Amissio* signifies the death of brothers <note>original: "brethren"</note>, or the lack of them, as well as of kindred and friends; it signifies no journeys and causes one to be deceived by many. In the fourth House, *Amissio* signifies the utter destruction of one's inheritance <note>original: "Patrimony"</note>, shows the father to be poor, and the son to die. *Amissio* in the fifth House shows the death of children and afflicts a person with various <note>original: "divers"</note> sorrows. It signifies a woman not to be pregnant <note>original: "with child"</note>, or else to have miscarried. It raises no fame or honors and spreads <note>original: "disperseth"</note> false rumors. In the sixth House, *Amissio* signifies that the sick person will recover <note>original: "be recovered"</note>, or shall soon recover, but it causes loss and damage through servants and livestock <note>original: "cattels"</note>. In the se-

<summary>This page concludes the interpretations for the geomantic figure Puella (The Maid), emphasizing social harmony and mild governance, before introducing Amissio (Loss), a figure largely associated with poverty, physical defects, and misfortune.</summary>

In the seventh House, *Amissio* <note>Latin: "Loss"</note> gives an unfaithful <note>original: "adulterous"</note> wife who opposes <note>original: "contrarying"</note> her husband with constant arguing; nevertheless, she shall not live long, and it causes disputes to be ended. In the eighth House, *Amissio* signifies that a person is dead, consumes a wife’s dowry, and brings no inheritances or legacies. In the ninth House, *Amissio* causes no journeys, except those that shall be completed with very great loss; it signifies people who are inconsistent in religion, often changing their opinion from one sect to another, and who are completely uneducated. In the tenth House, *Amissio* makes Princes most unfortunate and shows that they shall be compelled to end their lives in exile and banishment; it shows judges to be wicked and signifies that offices and magistracies will be harmful <note>original: "damageable"</note>, and it shows the death of a mother. In the eleventh House, *Amissio* signifies few friends and causes them to be easily lost and turned into enemies; it causes a man to have no favor with his Prince, unless that favor is harmful to him. In the twelfth House, *Amissio* destroys all enemies and keeps a person long in prison, but preserves them from dangers.

<margin>Union <note>Latin: "Conjunctio"</note></margin>
*Conjunctio* <note>Latin: "Conjunction" or "Union"</note> in the first House makes for a prosperous life and signifies a man of middle stature, neither thin nor fat, with a long face, straight <note>original: "plain"</note> hair, a small beard, and long fingers and thighs; he is generous <note>original: "liberal"</note>, friendly <note>original: "amiable"</note>, and a friend to many people. In the second House, *Conjunctio* does not signify that any riches will be gained, but it keeps a man secure and free from the hardships <note>original: "calamities"</note> of poverty; it detects both the thief and the thing stolen and helps acquire hidden treasure. In the third House, it gives various journeys with varying success and signifies honesty <note>original: "good faith"</note> and constancy. In the fourth House, *Conjunctio* shows an average <note>original: "mean"</note> inheritance <note>original: "Patrimony"</note>; it causes a father to be honest, of good reputation, and of good understanding. In the fifth House, it gives children of sharp <note>original: "subtile"</note> intelligence and wit, shows a pregnant woman will have a male child, and raises men to honors by their own natural <note>original: "meer proper"</note> wit and intelligence, spreading their fame and reputation far and wide; it also signifies news and rumors to be true. In the sixth House, *Conjunctio* signifies sicknesses to be long-lasting <note>original: "tedious"</note>...

In the twelfth House, *Conjunctio* signifies wary and quick-witted enemies. <margin>White <note>Latin: "*Albus*"</note></margin>
*Albus* <note>Latin: "White"</note> in the first House signifies a life troubled with continual sickness and grievous diseases. It signifies a man of short stature, a broad chest, and thick <note>original: "gross"</note> arms, having curly or frizzled hair. He has a broad, full mouth and is a great talker and babbler, much given to vain and unprofitable talk; however, he is merry, joyful, and cheerful <note>original: "jocund"</note>, and very pleasing to others. In the third House <note>The house of siblings, short journeys, and communication.</note>, *Albus* <note>Latin: "White"</note> signifies having very few siblings; it gives not many journeys, and those that occur are tedious and wearying; it also signifies all manner of deceivers. In the fourth House <note>The house of the home, land, and the father.</note>, he shows very little or no inheritance <note>original: "Patrimony"</note>, and the father to be a well-known man; however, it declares him to be a man of some base and inferior office and employment. In the fifth House <note>The house of children, pleasure, and news.</note>, *Albus* gives no children, or if there are any, they shall soon die. It denotes all rumors to be false and raises no one to honor. In the sixth House <note>The house of health, servants, and domestic animals.</note>, *Albus* causes very tedious sicknesses and diseases. It discovers the fraud, deceit, and wickedness of servants, and signifies that diseases and infirmities of livestock <note>original: "cattel"</note> will be fatal; it also makes the physician be suspected by the sick patient.

*Albus* in the seventh House <note>The house of marriage and partnerships.</note> gives a barren wife, though one who is fair and beautiful; it signifies few lawsuits or controversies, but those that do happen shall be of very long duration. In the eighth House <note>The house of death and the spouse's wealth.</note>, if a question is asked about any person, *Albus* shows that the party is dead. It gives little portion or dowry with a wife and causes that amount to be much fought and contended for. In the ninth House <note>The house of long journeys, religion, and philosophy.</note>, *Albus* denotes that some journeys will be accomplished, but with meager <note>original: "mean"</note> profit. It hinders the person who is away and signifies he shall not return; it also declares a man to be superstitious in religion and given to false and deceitful sciences. In the tenth House <note>The house of career, authority, and the mother.</note>, *Albus* causes princes and judges to be malevolent. It shows vile and base offices and positions of authority; it signifies a mother to be a whore, or one much suspected of adultery. In the eleventh House <note>The house of friendships and hopes.</note>, *Albus* creates dissembling and false friends; it causes love and favor to be inconstant. *Albus* in the twelfth House <note>The house of hidden enemies and imprisonment.</note> denotes vile, powerless <note>original: "impotent"</note>, and unrefined <note>original: "rustical"</note> enemies. It shows that those who are in prison shall not escape, and it signifies a great many and various troubles and inconveniences <note>original: "discommodities"</note> in one's life.

<margin>The Red One <note>original: "Red"</note></margin>
*Rubeus* <note>Latin: "The Red One"</note> in the first House <note>The house of self, appearance, and vitality.</note> signifies a short life and an evil end. It indicates a person who is filthy, useless, and has a cruel and malicious face, often with a notable scar or mark on some part of their body. In the second House <note>The house of wealth and finances.</note>, *Rubeus* signifies poverty. It produces thieves and robbers, and people who seek their livelihood through false, wicked, and illegal methods. It protects thieves and hides theft, and indicates that no treasure is hidden or will be found. In the third House <note>The house of siblings and short travels.</note>, *Rubeus* makes brothers and kinsmen full of hatred and spiteful toward one another; it shows them to have bad manners and an evil disposition. It makes journeys very dangerous and predicts betrayal and treachery. In the fourth House <note>The house of family and land.</note>, it destroys and consumes family estates <note>original: "Patrimonies"</note> and wastes inheritances, causing them to come to nothing. It destroys the crops in the field through stormy weather and the poor quality of the soil, and it brings the father to a quick and sudden death. *Rubeus* in the fifth House <note>The house of children and pleasure.</note> gives many children, but they will either be wicked and disobedient or will cause their parents grief, disgrace, and shame <note>original: "infamy"</note>. In the sixth House <note>The house of health and service.</note>, *Rubeus* causes fatal wounds and illnesses. The sick person will die; the physician will make a mistake; servants will prove false and treacherous; and livestock will cause harm and danger. <note>This section concludes the description of Rubeus in the Seventh House, which concerns marriage and partnerships.</note> ...a wife to be infamous, publicly unfaithful <note>original: "adulterate"</note>, and argumentative. It indicates deceitful and treacherous adversaries who will try to defeat you through sneaky tricks and legal maneuvers <note>original: "subtle wiles and circumventions of the Law"</note>. In the eighth House <note>The house of death and inheritance.</note>, *Rubeus* <note>Latin: "The Red One"</note> signifies a violent death inflicted by the execution of public justice. It also signifies that if you are asking about someone who is missing, they are certainly dead; it further indicates that a wife will have no inheritance or dowry. 

*Rubeus* in the ninth House <note>The house of travel, religion, and philosophy.</note> shows journeys to be evil and dangerous, and that a person will be in danger of being robbed by thieves or captured by plunderers. It declares people to be of the most wicked religious opinions and poor faith—people who will easily be persuaded to abandon their faith for any small reason. It denotes that the sciences are false and deceptive, and their professors are ignorant. 

In the tenth House <note>The house of authority, career, and social standing.</note>, *Rubeus* signifies rulers <note>original: "Princes"</note> who are cruel and tyrannical, and that their power will come to a bad end. They will either be cruelly murdered and destroyed by their own subjects, or they will be captured by their conquerors and put to a shameful <note>original: "ignominious"</note> and cruel death, or end their lives miserably in harsh imprisonment. It signifies that judges and officials are dishonest, thieving, and prone to charging excessive interest <note>original: "addicted to usury"</note>. It shows that a mother will die soon and indicates she will be stained with a bad reputation. 

In the eleventh House <note>The house of friends and social circles.</note>, *Rubeus* provides no true or faithful friends. It shows people to be of wicked lives and behavior <note>original: "conversations"</note>, and causes a person to be rejected and cast out from all society and interaction with good and noble people. 

*Rubeus* in the twelfth House <note>The house of hidden enemies, sorrow, and confinement.</note> makes enemies cruel and treacherous, of whom we should be very cautious. It signifies that those in prison will come to a bad end and shows that many troubles and misfortunes will happen in a person's life.

...miserable poverty; it signifies that both the thief and the stolen item will be captured and recovered, and it shows that no treasure remains hidden. In the third House <note>The house of siblings, short journeys, and communication.</note>, *Carcer* <note>Latin: "The Prison"</note> signifies hatred and disagreement among brothers; it indicates unlucky journeys and a most wicked faith and conduct <note>original: "conversation"</note>. *Carcer* in the fourth House <note>The house of the home, land, and the father.</note> signifies that a man will have no possessions or inheritances, that a father is most wicked, and will die a sudden and evil death. In the fifth House <note>The house of children, pleasure, and creativity.</note>, *Carcer* gives many children, yet shows a woman not to be pregnant; it causes those who are pregnant to miscarry by their own choice, or kills the child. It signifies no honors and spreads most false rumors. In the sixth House <note>The house of health, illness, and service.</note>, *Carcer* causes the diseased to suffer through long sickness; it signifies that servants are wicked or, rather, unprofitable, and that physicians are ignorant. In the seventh House <note>The house of marriage and partnerships.</note>, *Carcer* shows that a wife shall be hated by her husband, and signifies that lawsuits and disputes will be poorly ended and decided. In the eighth House <note>The house of death and inheritance.</note>, *Carcer* declares the type of death to be by some fall, accident, or false accusation—or that men shall be condemned in prison or in public judgment—and shows them to be put to death, or that they shall often take their own lives <note>original: "lay violent and deadly hands upon themselves"</note>. It denies a wife any inheritance or legacies. *Carcer* in the ninth House <note>The house of travel and religion.</note> shows that he who is away shall not return, and signifies some evil shall happen to him on his journey; it denotes people of no religion, a wicked conscience, and those ignorant of learning. In the tenth House <note>The house of career and authority.</note>, *Carcer* causes rulers <note>original: "Princes"</note> to be very wicked and to perish wretchedly because, once they are established in their power, they will wholly devote themselves to every sensual lust, pleasure, and tyranny. It causes judges to be unjust and false; it declares the mother to be cruel and infamous, branded with the mark of adultery. It gives no public offices or positions of authority <note>original: "Magistracies"</note> except those obtained either by lying, or through theft and base, cruel robbery. In the eleventh House <note>The house of friends and hopes.</note>, *Carcer* causes a person to have no friends, nor love, nor favor among men. In the twelfth House <note>The house of hidden enemies and imprisonment.</note>, it raises enemies, keeps one in prison, and inflicts many evils.

<meta>This page continues the interpretation of the geomantic figure Tristitia (Sorrow), specifically its effects when placed in the first House and continuing through the tenth House. It follows the previous page's discussion of Carcer (The Prison).</meta>

<page-num>29</page-num>

<header>of Geomancy.</header>

...afflicts it with many troubles <note>original: "molestations"</note>; it signifies a person of good manners and conduct, but one who is solitary and slow in all his business and affairs. He is solitary, melancholy, seldom laughing, but most greedy <note>original: "covetous"</note> for all things. 

In the second House <note>The house of wealth and personal finances.</note>, it gives much substance and riches, but those who have them shall not enjoy them. Instead, they will hide them and will scarcely afford to buy themselves food or sustenance from them. Treasure shall not be found, nor shall the thief or the stolen goods be recovered. 

*Tristitia* <note>Latin: "Sorrow"</note> in the third House <note>The house of siblings, short journeys, and communication.</note> signifies a man will have few brothers, but shows that he shall outlive them all. It causes unhappy journeys but grants good faith. 

In the fourth House <note>The house of the home, land, and the father.</note>, *Tristitia* consumes and destroys fields, possessions, and inheritances. It causes a father to be old and long-lived, and a very greedy hoarder of money. 

In the fifth House <note>The house of children, pleasure, and creativity.</note>, it signifies no children, or that they shall soon die. It shows a pregnant woman will give birth to a girl and brings no fame or honors. 

In the sixth House <note>The house of health, illness, and service.</note>, *Tristitia* shows that the sick shall die. Servants shall be good but lazy <note>original: "slothful"</note>, and it signifies that cattle shall be of a low price or value. 

In the seventh House <note>The house of marriage and partnerships.</note>, *Tristitia* shows that the wife shall soon die. It declares lawsuits and disputes <note>original: "suits and contentions"</note> to be very harmful and decided against you. 

In the eighth House <note>The house of death and inheritance.</note>, it signifies the type of death to be by a long and grievous sickness with much grief <note>original: "dolour"</note> and pain. It grants legacies and an inheritance and provides a wife with a dowry <note>original: "portion"</note>. 

*Tristitia* in the ninth House <note>The house of foreign travel, religion, and philosophy.</note> shows that he who is absent shall perish on his journey, or signifies that some evil misfortune shall happen to him. It causes journeys to be very unfortunate but declares men to be of good religion, devout, and profound scholars. 

In the tenth House <note>The house of career, reputation, and authority.</note>, *Tristitia* signifies princes to be severe but very good lovers of justice. It causes just judges, but those who are tedious and slow in deciding cases. It brings a mother to a good old age with integrity and honesty of life, but mixed with various hardships <note>original: "discommodities"</note> and misfortunes. It raises one to great offices, but they shall not be long enjoyed nor maintained. It signifies such offices as pertain to water, or the tilling and fertilizing <note>original: "manuring"</note> of land.

...of the Earth, or those who are to be employed in matters of religion and wisdom. In the eleventh House <note>The house of friends, hopes, and ambitions.</note>, *Tristitia* <note>original: "Tristitia"; Latin: "Sorrow"</note> signifies a scarcity of friends and the death of friends; it also signifies little love or favor. In the twelfth House <note>The house of secrets, hidden enemies, and prisons.</note>, it shows no enemies; it wretchedly condemns the imprisoned; and it causes many inconveniences <note>original: "discommodities"</note> and losses <note>original: "disprofits"</note> to happen in one's life.

<margin>Dragon's Head</margin>
*Caput Draconis* <note>Latin: "The Dragon's Head," representing the North Node of the Moon, usually considered a point of growth and positive increase.</note> in the first House increases life and fortune. In the second House, it increases riches and substance; it saves and conceals a thief; and it signifies that treasure is hidden. In the third House, *Caput Draconis* gives many brothers; it causes journeys and brings relatives <note>original: "kinsmen"</note>, good faith, and credit. In the fourth House, it gives wealthy inheritances and causes the father to reach old age. In the fifth House, *Caput Draconis* gives many children; it signifies that pregnant women will give birth to girls <note>original: "women-children"</note> and often to twins. It shows great honors and fame, and signifies that news and rumors are true. *Caput Draconis* in the sixth House increases sickness and diseases; it signifies that the physician is learned; and it gives very many servants and livestock <note>original: "chattel"</note>. In the seventh House, it signifies that a man shall have many wives; it multiplies and stirs up many adversaries and lawsuits. In the eighth House, it shows death to be certain, increases legacies and inheritances, and gives a good dowry <note>original: "portion"</note> with a wife. In the ninth House, *Caput Draconis* signifies many journeys, many sciences, and good religion; it shows that those who are absent shall soon return. In the tenth House, it signifies glorious princes, great and magnificent judges, great offices, and profitable <note>original: "gainful"</note> positions in government <note>original: "magistracy"</note>. In the eleventh House, it causes one to have many friends and to be beloved by all men. In the twelfth House, *Caput Draconis* signifies that men will have many enemies and many women; it detains those who are imprisoned and punishes them severely.

<margin>Dragon's Tail</margin>
*Cauda Draconis* <note>Latin: "The Dragon's Tail," representing the South Node of the Moon, usually considered a point of decrease or release, often having the opposite effect of the Head.</note>, in each and every one of the respective Houses mentioned above, gives the opposite <note>original: "contrary"</note> judgment to the Dragon's Head. And these are the natures of the figures of Geomancy and their judgments in each and every one of their Houses, upon all manner of Questions...

<header>Of Geomancy</header>

## Questions to be Asked Concerning Any Matter or Thing Whatsoever

Now, regarding the method of arriving at a judgment, you must especially observe this: whenever any question is proposed to you that is related to any of the Houses <note>In geomancy, as in astrology, the "Houses" are twelve sections of a chart that represent different areas of life, such as wealth, health, or travel.</note>, you should not only answer based on the symbol <note>original: "figure"</note> found in that specific house. Instead, by observing and carefully considering all the symbols, including the Index <note>In a geomantic chart, the "Index" refers to specific points of calculation used to summarize the reading.</note> itself within the two houses, you shall base your final judgment. 

You should therefore consider the symbol representing the subject of the question <note>original: "quesited"; referring to the person or thing asked about</note>. If that symbol appears again <note>original: "multiply himself"</note> in other places in the chart, you should allow those other positions to be part of your judgment as well. For example, if a question is asked concerning the second House <note>The house of personal wealth and possessions.</note> regarding a thief, and the symbol from the second House is also found in the sixth House <note>The house of servants, laborers, and small animals.</note>, it indicates that the thief is a member of your own household or one of your servants. 

You should judge and consider the rest of the symbols in this same manner; for this entire art consists of the blending <note>original: "commixtures"</note> of the symbols and their specific natures. Whoever practices this correctly will always provide the most accurate and reliable judgments on every specific matter whatsoever.

You must also observe that the names of the evil spirits are extracted from the names of the good spirits, as well as from the evil. However, if we enter the table with the name of a good spirit of the second order, the name of the evil spirit shall be extracted from the order of the Princes and Governors. But if we enter the table with the name of a good spirit of the third order, or with the name of an evil spirit who is a Governor—regardless of how they are extracted, whether by this table or from a celestial figure <note>A "celestial figure" refers to an astrological chart or horoscope.</note>—the names that proceed from this shall be the names of the evil spirits who are the Ministers of the inferior order.

It is further to be noted that as often as we enter this table with the good spirits of the second order, the names extracted are also of the second order; and if under them we extract the name of an evil spirit, he belongs to the superior order of the Governors. The same rule applies if we enter with the name of an evil spirit of the superior order. Therefore, if we enter this table with the names of the spirits of the third order, or with the names of the ministering spirits (both good and evil), the names extracted shall be the names of the ministering spirits of the inferior order.

But many magicians, men of no small authority, insist that tables of this kind should be expanded using Latin letters. In this way, using these same tables, the name of any spirit—whether good or evil—could be found based on the name of any office or desired effect. This is done in the same manner described above: by taking the name of the office or the effect in the column of letters, on its own line, under its own star. *Trismegistus* <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary author of the Hermetic Corpus and a foundational figure in Western occultism.</note> is a primary authority on this practice; he provided this kind of calculation using Egyptian letters. These may also be appropriately applied to the letters of other languages, for the reasons assigned to the signs. Truly, he is the only man among all those who have written on the subject of obtaining the names of spirits whose work still survives <note>original: "extant"</note>.

### The Fourth Book

Therefore, the force, secrecy, and power of how the sacred names of spirits are truly and correctly discovered consists in the arrangement of vowels. These vowels form the name of a spirit, through which the true name and the right word are established. 

Now, this art is perfected and achieved in this way: first, we must pay attention to the placement of the vowels belonging to the letters found through the calculation of the celestial figure <note>A "celestial figure" is an astrological chart or horoscope.</note> to find the names of the spirits of the second order—the Presidents and Governors. 

For good spirits, this is accomplished by considering the stars that constitute the letters and placing them in their proper order. First, subtract the degree of the eleventh House <note>The "eleventh House" in astrology is traditionally associated with friends, hopes, and "good" spirits.</note> from the degree of the star that comes first in order. Take the remainder and project it from the degree of the ascendant <note>The "ascendant" is the sign or degree rising on the eastern horizon at the time for which the chart is cast.</note>. Where that number ends is the portion of the vowel for the first letter. Begin, therefore, to calculate the vowels of these letters according to their number and order; the vowel that falls in the place of the first star is the vowel attributed to the first letter.

Afterward, you shall find the part of the second letter by subtracting the degree of the star that is second in order from the first star, and projecting the remainder from the ascendant. This is the point from which you shall begin the calculation of the vowels; the vowel that falls upon the second star is the vowel of the second letter. 

You may then continue to search out the vowels of the following letters in this way: always subtract the degree of the next star from the degree of the star immediately preceding it. 

Similarly, all calculations and numbering for the names of good spirits should be made following the succession of the signs <note>original: "succession of the signes"; this refers to the standard order of the zodiac signs (Aries, Taurus, etc.).</note>. When calculating the names of evil spirits, whereas the degree of the eleventh House is used for good spirits, for these you must use the degree of the twelfth House <note>The "twelfth House" is traditionally associated with hidden enemies, sorrow, and "evil" spirits.</note>. All numbering and calculations may be made following the order of the signs, beginning from the...

# Of Occult Philosophy,

...degree of the tenth House <note>In astrology, the "tenth House" (the Medium Coeli or Midheaven) represents one's career, reputation, and social standing.</note>.

But in all extractions using tables, the vowels are arranged in a different manner. First, take the specific number of letters that make up the name itself. Count this number from the beginning of the column of the first letter (the column from which the name is extracted). The letter where this count ends is then compared to the first letter of the name by calculating the distance between them according to the order of the alphabet. 

The number representing that distance is then projected from the beginning of its column. Where this count ends, you find the position of the first vowel. From that point, calculate the vowels themselves according to their number and order within that same column. The vowel that falls upon the first letter of the name is the one assigned to that name. 

You will find the subsequent vowels by measuring the distance from the preceding vowel to the next one, following the order of the alphabet. Count that distance from the beginning of its own column; where the count stops is the location of the vowel you are looking for. Calculate the vowels from that point as described above. The vowels that fall upon their respective letters are assigned to them. If a vowel happens to fall on another vowel, the first one must give way to the second. 

Understand that this process applies only to good spirits. For evil spirits, you may proceed in the same way, with one exception: you must perform the calculations in reverse and backward order—contrary to the succession of the alphabet and contrary to the order of the columns (that is to say, in ascending order).

The method for finding the names of good Angels and of every person, which we taught in our third book of *Occult Philosophy* <note>Henry Cornelius Agrippa's *Three Books of Occult Philosophy* (1531) is a foundational text of Western esotericism.</note>, is of significant authority and is built on a solid foundation. However, we will now provide you with other methods, supported by sound reasoning. One of these methods involves identifying five specific places in the figure of the nativity <note>A "figure of the nativity" is a birth chart or horoscope cast for the moment of a person's birth.</note>...

### The fourth book.

...of the *Hylech* <note>original: Hylech, or Hileg; in astrology, the planet or point in a birth chart that acts as the "giver of life."</note>. Once this is noted, the characters of the letters are projected in their order and number from the beginning of *Aries*. Those letters that fall upon the degrees of the specified places—arranged and aspected according to their order and dignity—form the name of an Angel. 

There is also another method where they use the *Almutel* <note>original: Almutel; the planet that has the most essential dignity and influence over a specific house or point in a horoscope.</note>, which is the ruling and governing star over the five places mentioned previously. In this case, the projection is to be made from the degree of the ascendant. This is done by collecting the letters that fall upon the *Almutel*. When these letters are placed in order according to their dignity, they form the name of an Angel. 

Furthermore, there is another way used and closely observed by the *Egyptians*. They make their calculation from the degree of the ascendant and gather the letters according to the *Almutel* of the eleventh House—which they call a good *Daemon* <note>original: Dæmon; here used in the classical Greek sense of a guardian spirit or divinity, rather than a "demon" in the modern sense.</note>. When these letters are arranged according to their dignities, the names of the Angels are established. 

Now, the names of the evil Angels are determined in a similar manner, except that the projections must be performed in reverse—contrary to the course and order of the signs of the zodiac. Thus, while we calculate from the beginning of *Aries* to find the names of good spirits, we must count from the beginning of *Libra* to find the names of the evil ones. And while we count from the degree of the ascendant for good spirits, for evil spirits, we must calculate from the degree of the seventh House. According to the *Egyptians*, the name of an [evil] Angel is collected according to the *Almutel* of the twelfth House, which they call an evil spirit. 

All these rites, which we have already described elsewhere in our third book of *Occult Philosophy*, may be performed using the characters of any language. In all of these (as we said above), there is a mystical and divine number, order, and figure. Because of this, it happens that the same spirit may be called by various names. Other names, however, are discovered from the name of the spirit itself—whether good or evil—using tables created for this purpose.

# Of Occult Philosophy

Now, these celestial characters consist of lines and heads. There are six types of heads, corresponding to the six magnitudes <note>magnitude: a measurement of the brightness of a star as seen from Earth; first magnitude stars are the brightest.</note> of the stars, to which the planets are also assigned. 

1. The **first magnitude** (associated with the **Sun**) uses a Star or a Cross. 
2. The **second** (associated with **Jupiter**) uses a circular point. 
3. The **third** (associated with **Saturn**) uses a semicircle or a triangle—which may be curved, round, or acute. 
4. The **fourth** (associated with **Mars**) uses a small stroke that cuts through a line, whether it is square, straight, or oblique. 
5. The **fifth** (associated with **Venus** and **Mercury**) uses a small stroke or a point with a tail, either ascending or descending. 
6. The **sixth** (associated with the **Moon**) uses a point that has been blackened. 

You may see all of these in the following table. Once the heads are positioned according to the location of the stars in the astrological chart <note>original: "figure of Heaven"</note>, the lines are then drawn out according to the agreement <note>original: "congruency"</note> of their natures. You should understand this as applying to the fixed stars. However, when creating characters for the planets, the lines are drawn by positioning the heads according to their specific orbits and natures relative to one another.

->Stars. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Heads. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Lines joined to the Heads.<-

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for constructing magical "characters" or sigils. Having discussed the visual appearance of the "heads" and "lines" on the previous page, the author now explains the mathematical and astronomical methods for determining their arrangement and deriving symbols from the names of spirits.</meta>

# Book Four

If an image is ascending in any degree or face <note>face: an astrological subdivision of a zodiac sign, usually consisting of ten degrees.</note> of a sign which consists of stars of the same magnitude and nature, then, with the number of these stars positioned according to their place and order, the lines are drawn to resemble the signified image as fully as can be done.

However, the characters <note>characters: in this context, magical symbols or sigils used to represent specific entities or powers.</note> that are extracted according to the name of a spirit are composed using the following table, by assigning to every letter the name from the table that corresponds to it. While this may appear easy to those who understand it, there is actually significant difficulty involved—specifically, knowing which figure or letter to choose when a letter of a name falls upon the line of letters or figures. 

This may be determined as follows: if a letter falls upon the line of letters, consider what position this letter holds in the order of the name (such as the second or the third letter). Then, consider how many letters the name contains in total (such as five or seven). Multiply these two numbers together and triple the product. Then, count that total number from the beginning of the alphabet; the letter upon which that number falls should be used as the character for that spirit.

But if any letter of a name falls on the line of figures, it is to be worked out this way: take the position of the letter in the name and multiply it by the letter’s position in the alphabet. Divide the total by nine, and the remainder indicates the figure or number to be placed in the character. This may be expressed as either a geometric shape or an arithmetic number; however, it should not exceed the number nine or have more than nine angles.

# Of Occult Philosophy

->A scepter. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; A sword. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; A scourge.<-

<image-desc>Three woodcut illustrations arranged horizontally. From left to right: a decorative scepter with an ornate head and pommel; a straight-bladed sword with a cross-guard and round pommel; and a scourge with a short handle and three knotted lashes.</image-desc>

However, the symbols <note>original: "Characters"</note> revealed by spirits derive their power <note>original: "vertue"</note> from that revelation. They act as hidden seals that create harmony with a specific divinity. They are either signs of a covenant that has been established—representing promised and pledged faith—or signs of obedience. These symbols cannot be discovered by any other means.

Furthermore, besides these symbols, there are specific recognizable figures and images of evil spirits, which are the forms they usually take when appearing or obeying those who summon <note>original: "invoke"</note> them. All of these symbols or images can be determined using the table following the sequence of letters that make up the spirits' names. 

If a letter represents more than one spirit's name, that spirit's image takes priority, while the others contribute their own rank <note>original: "order"</note>. For example, the first letters of a name are used to determine the head and upper body according to their specific figure. The middle letters determine the middle parts of the body, and the final letters determine the thighs and feet. If there is a conflict between letters, the letter with the higher numerical value <note>referring to Gematria, where letters have number values</note> prevails; if they are equal, they contribute equally to the image. Additionally, if a name is associated with a specific notable symbol or tool from the table, that item will also appear in the spirit's image.

We can also determine the rank <note>original: "dignities"</note> of these evil spirits using the same tables of symbols and images. Whichever spirit is assigned a specific noble sign or tool from the table holds that corresponding rank. For instance, a crown indicates kingly status; a crest or plume signifies a dukedom; and a horn represents the rank of a count <note>original: "County"; the domain or rank of a Count or Earl</note>; if with—

# The Fourth Book

If among these there is a scepter, a sword, or a forked instrument, it shows rule and authority. Similarly, from the Table of Images, you shall find those who hold the highest kingly status <note>original: "dignity"</note>. Determine their rank from the crown, and their rule and authority from the instruments they hold. Finally, those who take a human shape and figure have greater status than those who appear in the forms and images of beasts; likewise, those who ride surpass those who appear on foot.

In this way, by considering all these combinations, you may judge the rank and excellence of spirits relative to one another. Furthermore, you must understand that spirits of a lower order, regardless of their status, are always subject to spirits of a higher order. It is also not inconsistent <note>original: "incongruent"</note> for their kings and dukes to be subject to and serve the presiding spirits <note>original: "presidents"</note> of a higher order.

Their sign <note>a physical manifestation indicating their arrival</note> is white earth, whiter than any snow.

### The familiar forms of the Spirits of Jupiter

<image-desc>The astrological symbol for Jupiter (♃).</image-desc>

The spirits of Jupiter appear with a body that is sanguine <note>associated with the "humor" of blood: ruddy, active, and sturdy</note> and choleric <note>associated with yellow bile: fiery or irritable</note>. They are of average height and move in a terrifying and awe-inspiring way, yet they have a mild appearance and speak gently. Their color is like that of iron. Their movement is accompanied by flashes of lightning and thunder. Their sign is that men will appear around the magic circle who seem to be devoured by lions.

### Their particular forms are:

A king with a drawn sword, riding on a stag.

A maid with a laurel crown adorned with flowers.
A bull.
A stag.
A peacock.
<margin>An azure garment <note>original: "Azurino vestis"</note>.</margin>
A sword.
<margin>A box-tree <note>original: "Buxus"</note>.</margin>

### The familiar forms of the Spirits of Mars

<image-desc>The astrological symbol for Mars (♂).</image-desc>

They appear with a tall body and a choleric <note>angry or aggressive</note> temperament. They have an ugly appearance and are brown, swarthy, or red in color. They have horns like a deer's antlers and griffin's claws, and they bellow like wild bulls. Their movement is like a burning fire, and their sign is thunder and lightning appearing around the magic circle.

### Their particular shapes are:

A king in armor riding upon a wolf.
A man in armor.

But we now come to speak of the holy and sacred Pentacles and Sigils <note>original: "Sigils." These are inscribed symbols used in magic, often representing a specific spirit or intention.</note>. Now these pentacles are like certain holy signs that preserve us from evil accidents and events, and help and assist us to bind, banish <note>original: "exterminate"</note>, and drive away evil spirits, while attracting good spirits and reconciling them to us. And these pentacles consist of either the symbols <note>original: "Characters"</note> of the good spirits of the higher order, or of sacred images made of holy letters or revelations, with appropriate verses <note>original: "apt and fit versicles"</note>. These are composed of either geometrical figures and holy names of God (according to the standard practice of many of them), or they are made by mixing all or many of these elements together. 

The symbols <note>original: "Characters"</note> that are useful for us to design and create pentacles are those of the good spirits—especially and chiefly the good spirits of the first and second orders, and sometimes also of the third order. These kinds of symbols are especially to be called holy; as are those symbols which we have previously called holy. <margin>The bronze serpent set up in the wilderness.</margin> 
Such as the image of the serpent hanging on the cross and similar figures, many of which can be found in the visions of the prophets—such as *Isaiah, Daniel, Ezra*, and others—and also in the Book of *Revelation*.
