<margin>Receive the holy sword, a gift from God, with which you shall strike down the enemies of my people Israel. <note>original: "Accipe gladium sanctum, munus a Deo, in quo concides adversarios populi mei Israel."</note></margin> 
Therefore, when an image of any of these holy symbols is placed, a circle should be drawn all around it. Within this circle, write a divine name that is appropriate and aligned with the intended effect of that figure. Alternatively, a short verse taken from the Holy Scriptures may be written around it, which serves to confirm or call forth the desired result. 

For example, if a pentacle were being made to gain victory or revenge against one's enemies—both visible and invisible—the image might be taken from the second book of the *Maccabees*: specifically, a hand holding a drawn golden sword. Around this, write the verse contained there: *"Receive this holy sword as a gift from God, with which you shall strike down the adversaries of my people Israel."* Or, one could write a verse from the fifth *Psalm*: *"In this is the strength of your arm; before your face there is death,"* or some other similar verse. 

But if you choose to write a divine name around the figure, choose one that signifies fear, a sword, wrath, the vengeance of God, or some other name that is consistent and in agreement with the desired effect. And if a geometric figure is to be used, let it be chosen according to the logic and rules of numbers, as we taught in our second book of *Occult Philosophy*, where we discussed numbers and similar operations. Oftentimes we also construct <term>Pentacles</term> <note>original: "Pentacles"; magical diagrams or talismans, often circular, used in ritual magic to command spirits or provide protection</note> by making the revolution of some kind of name in a square table, and by drawing a single or double circle around it, and by writing within it some holy verse <note>original: "Versicle"</note> that is suitable and fits this name, or from which that name is extracted. And this is the way of making the Pentacles, according to their several distinct forms and fashions, which we may as we please either multiply or combine together in sequence among themselves, to work the greater efficacy, and the extension and enlargement of force and virtue.

As, if a <term>deprecation</term> <note>original: "deprecation"; a prayer specifically intended to avert evil or invoke judgment against enemies</note> should be made for the overthrow and destruction of one's enemies, then we are to mind and call to remembrance how God destroyed the whole face of the earth in the Great Flood <note>original: "deluge of waters"</note>; and the destruction of Sodom and Gomorrah, by raining down fire and brimstone; likewise, how God overthrew Pharaoh and his host in the Red Sea: and to call to mind if any other curse <note>original: "malediction"</note> be found in Holy Scripture. And thus in things of a similar sort. So likewise in praying against the perils and dangers of water, we ought to call to remembrance the saving of Noah in the Great Flood, the passing of the children of Israel through the Red Sea; and also we are to mind how Christ walked upon the waters, and saved the ship that was in danger of being wrecked by the storm; and how he commanded the winds and the waves, and they obeyed—

...him; and also, that he drew *Peter* out of the water when he was in danger of drowning; and the like. And lastly, with these we invoke and call upon certain holy names of God; namely, those that are significant for accomplishing our desire and suited to the desired effect. For example, if the goal is to overthrow enemies, we should invoke and call upon the names of God's wrath, revenge, fear, justice, and fortitude. If we wish to avoid and escape any evil or danger, we then call upon the names of mercy, defense, salvation, fortitude, goodness, and similar names of God. When we pray to God to grant us our desires, we should also intermix the name of some good spirit—whether only one or more—whose office it is to carry out our desires. Sometimes we also require an evil spirit to be restrained or compelled, whose name we likewise intermingle; this is done correctly and especially if the goal is to execute some harmful work, such as revenge, punishment, or destruction.

Furthermore, if there is any verse <note>original: "Verſicle"; a short sentence from the Bible, often used in a liturgical or ritual response</note> in the Psalms, or in any other part of Holy Scripture, that seems congruent and agreeable to our desire, it should be mingled with our prayers. After prayer has been made to God, it is then appropriate to make an <term>oration</term> <note>original: "Oration"; here meaning a formal ritual address or command</note> to that agent <note>original: "executioner"; in this context, the spirit or entity that "executes" or performs the requested task</note> whom we requested in our preceding prayer to God to assist us—whether one or more, and whether they are an angel, a star, a soul, or any of the noble angels. This kind of oration should be composed according to the rules we provided in the second book of *Occult Philosophy*, where we discussed the manner of composing incantations <note>original: "Inchantments"</note>.

<margin>*Fires; Envy and Malice <note>original Latin: "Incendia"; meaning fires or conflagrations</note></margin>
You should also know that these kinds of bonds have a threefold difference: the first bond is when we conjure by natural things; the second is compounded of religious mysteries, such as sacraments, miracles, and things of this sort; and the third is constituted by divine names and holy <term>sigils</term> <note>original: "Sigils"; magical signs or symbols used to represent and summon specific spirits</note>. By these kinds of bonds, we may bind not only spirits, but also all other creatures whatsoever—such as animals, tempests, fires, floods of water, and the force and power...

Often we also use these aforementioned bonds, not only through conjuration <note>original: "Conjuration"; the formal act of calling upon spirits</note>, but sometimes also using the methods of prayer <note>original: "Deprecation"; a prayer intended to turn away evil or seek mercy</note> and blessing <note>original: "Benediction"</note>. Moreover, it is very helpful for this purpose to add some passage from Holy Scripture, if any is found to be suitable. For example, in the conjuration of serpents, one might commemorate the curse of the serpent in the Garden of Eden and the raising of the bronze serpent in the wilderness <note>References to Genesis 3:14 and Numbers 21:9</note>. To this, one might add that verse <note>original: "Versicle"</note>: <margin>You shall walk upon the asp and the basilisk, and so on. <note>original Latin: *Super aspidem & basiliscum ambulabis, &c.*</note></margin> *You shall walk upon the asp <note>a venomous snake</note> and the basilisk <note>a legendary serpent king whose gaze was lethal</note>, etc.* Ritual practice <note>original: "Superstition"; in this context, it refers to the use of specific religious ceremonies or external rites</note> is also very effective here by adapting <note>original: "translation"</note> certain sacramental rites to bind what we intend to hinder. These include the rites of excommunication, of tombs, funerals, burials, and similar ceremonies.

And now we come to discuss the consecrations that a person should perform upon all instruments and necessary items used in this art. The power <note>original: "vertue"</note> of this consecration consists mainly of two things: specifically, the power of the person performing the consecration, and the power of the prayer by which the consecration is made. For the person consecrating, holiness of life and the power of sanctifying are required. Both of these are acquired through spiritual elevation <note>original: "Dignification"; the process of making oneself worthy or "dignified" to perform magic</note> and initiation. Furthermore, the person must believe in its power and effectiveness with a firm and certain faith. 

As for the prayer itself used for consecration, a similar holiness is required. This either resides in the prayer itself—as if it were ordained for this purpose by divine inspiration, such as passages found in many places in the Holy Bible—or it is established for this purpose through the power of the Holy Spirit within the formal traditions <note>original: "ordination"</note> of the Church. Alternatively, the prayer contains a holiness <note>original: "Sanctimony"</note> that comes not only from the words themselves, but from the mention <note>original: "commemoration"</note> of holy things. This includes referencing Holy Scripture, sacred histories, divine works, miracles, spiritual effects, graces, promises, sacraments, and sacramental objects. These things, through a certain resemblance, are seen to relate (either directly or indirectly) to the item being consecrated.

This, therefore, is to be known and firmly observed: that if any consecration is to be made of things that are non-sacred <note>original: "profane"</note>, in which there is any pollution or defilement, then an exorcism and purification <note>original: "expiation"</note> of those things ought to precede the consecration. Once these things are made pure, they are more capable of receiving the influences of the divine powers <note>original: "vertues"</note>. We are also to observe that at the end of every consecration, after the prayer is properly performed, the person consecrating ought to bless the thing consecrated by breathing out some words with divine power <note>original: "vertue"</note> and the authority of the present consecration, while recounting <note>original: "commemoration"</note> his power and authority, so that the rite may be more duly performed with an earnest and focused <note>original: "intentive"</note> mind. The blessing of the lights, wax, and lamps is derived from the fire and the altar which contains the substance of the flame. It also draws on other symbolic comparisons used in the mysteries, such as the seven candlesticks and lamps burning before the face of God.

These, therefore, are the consecrations which are necessary to be used first in every kind of devotion; they ought to precede the ritual, for without them, nothing in holy rites can be performed correctly.

In the next section, we shall show you the consecration of places, instruments, and similar things.

<margin>The Holy of Holies <note>original: "Sanctum sanctorum"</note></margin>
Therefore, when you wish to consecrate any place or circle, you ought to use the prayer of Solomon used in the dedication of the Temple. Moreover, you must bless the place by sprinkling holy water and using fumigations <note>original: "fumigations"; the burning of incense or aromatic herbs for purification</note>. During the blessing, you must commemorate holy mysteries such as these: the sanctification of the throne of God, of Mount Sinai, of the Tabernacle of the Covenant, of the Holy of Holies, and of the Temple of Jerusalem. Also, mention the sanctification of Mount Golgotha by the crucifixion of Christ; the sanctification of the Temple of Christ; and of Mount Tabor, by the transfiguration and ascension of Christ, and other such events. You must also invoke divine names that are significant to this purpose, such as: the Place of God, the Throne of God, the Chair of God, the Tabernacle of God, the Altar of God, and the Habitation of God. These and similar divine names should be written around the circle or the place to be consecrated.

In the consecration of instruments and of all other things whatsoever that are used in this art, you shall proceed in the same manner: by sprinkling them with holy water, perfuming them with holy fumigations, anointing them with holy oil, sealing them with some holy sigil <term>a symbolic seal or sign representing a specific spiritual force</term>, and blessing them with prayer. You must also commemorate holy things from the Sacred Scriptures, religious tradition, and Divine names.

Furthermore, besides these, there is another rite of consecration of wonderful power and great efficacy. This is derived from the categories of "superstitions" <note>In the context of Renaissance magic, "superstition" often referred to the practice of adapting established religious sacraments or liturgical rites for magical use.</note>: that is to say, when the rite of consecration or the gathering of any Sacrament in the Church is transferred to the object which we wish to consecrate.

It should also be known that vows, offerings, and sacrifices have the power of consecration, both material and personal. These act as certain covenants and agreements between those divine names with which they are made and us who make them, binding themselves strongly to our desire and the desired effect. This occurs when we dedicate, offer, and sacrifice using certain names or objects, such as: fumigations <note>incense or ritual smoke</note>, unctions <note>ritual oils</note>, rings, images, and looking-glasses. It also applies to less material things, such as divine figures <note>original: "Deities"</note>, sigils <note>magical symbols or seals</note>, pentacles, enchantments, orations <note>formal prayers or speeches</note>, and pictures.

<margin>The Book of Spirits <note>original: "Liber Spirituum"</note></margin>
Among those magicians who mostly employ the service <note>original: "ministery"</note> of evil spirits, there exists a certain rite for summoning <note>original: "invocating"</note> spirits using a book previously consecrated for that purpose. This is properly called *A Book of Spirits*, about which we shall now say a few words. This book is to be consecrated as a book of evil spirits, ceremoniously composed in their names and hierarchy <note>original: "order"</note>. To this book, the magician binds the spirits written within it to ready and present obedience by means of a specific holy oath.

This book must be made of the purest and cleanest paper that has never been used before, which many call **virgin paper**. It must be inscribed in this way: on the left side, place the image of the spirit, and on the right side, its character <note>original: "character"; in magic, this refers to a unique sigil or symbolic mark associated with a specific entity</note>. Above these, write the oath containing the spirit's name, its rank <note>original: "dignity"</note>, place, office, and power. Although many people compose this book differently by omitting the characters or images, it is more effective not to neglect anything that contributes to its success.

Furthermore, one must observe the specific circumstances of locations, dates, and hours that correspond to the stars under which these spirits are governed. The spirit's location, ritual, and hierarchy must also be applied accordingly.

Once the book is written and well-bound, it should be decorated, embellished, and kept secure with fasteners <note>original: "Registers"; here referring to book clasps or ties</note> and seals. This prevents it from accidentally opening after its consecration, which could endanger the practitioner <note>original: "operator"</note>. Furthermore, this book ought to be kept with as much reverence as possible, for an irreverent mind causes it to lose its power <note>original: "vertue"</note> through spiritual pollution and profanation.

Now that this sacred book has been composed in the manner described, we must then proceed to its consecration in one of two ways. The first way is to call each and every spirit written in the book to the ritual circle, according to the proper rites and hierarchy...

Then you shall uncover the holy plate <note>original: "Lamen"</note> and pray on your knees before the altar as described above. After an invocation is made to the angels, the ones you desire will appear to you. You shall receive them with kind and virtuous <note>original: "chaste"</note> conversation, and then give them permission to depart.

Within the **Circle** itself, one should write the general divine names and those things which provide us with defense. <meta>This page continues the discussion of necromancy, specifically the methods for summoning the souls of the dead by using material offerings and selecting specific locations based on the nature of the soul's death.</meta>

Because of this, the souls of the dead are not to be called up without blood, or by applying some part of their remaining <note>original: "relict"</note> body.

In the raising of these spirits <note>original: "shadows"</note>, we must burn incense <note>original: "perfume"</note> with new blood, with the bones of the dead, and with flesh, eggs, milk, honey, and oil, and similar things. These provide the souls with a means suitable for receiving their bodies.

It should also be understood that those who wish to raise any souls of the dead ought to do it in places where these kinds of souls are most known to linger. This might be due to some connection drawing those souls back to their forsaken bodies, or some past affection from their lives drawing the soul to certain places, things, or persons. It could also be due to the powerful nature of a place designed to purge or punish these souls. These locations are mostly identified through the experience of visions, powerful hauntings <note>original: "incursions"</note>, apparitions, and similar signs <note>original: "prodigies"</note> that have been seen.

Therefore, the most suitable places for these activities are churchyards. Better than those are places where criminal judgments are executed. Even better than these are places where, in recent years, there have been public slaughters of men. Furthermore, a place is even more effective if a dead carcass from a violent death remains there, not yet ritually cleansed <note>original: "expiated"</note> or properly buried, especially if it was buried recently. The ritual cleansing of those places is a holy rite that should be properly applied <note>original: "adhibited"</note> at the burial of bodies; it often prevents souls from returning to their bodies and drives them far away to places of judgment.

<page-num>71</page-num>

For this reason, the souls of the dead are not easily raised up, except for the souls of those whom we know to be evil, or those who perished by a violent death and whose bodies lack a proper and formal burial.

Now, although we have discussed these kinds of places, it is often not safe or convenient to travel to them. Therefore, it is necessary for us to take a principal part of the body that remains <note>original: "relict"</note> to whatever place we have chosen. With that part, we must create a ritual incense <note>original: "perfume"</note> in the proper manner and perform other appropriate rites.

It should also be known that because souls are a type of spiritual light, artificial lights—especially if they are crafted from specific, suitable materials and compounded according to true occult rules, featuring matching inscriptions of Names and Seals—are very helpful in raising the souls of the departed.

Furthermore, the methods spoken of so far are not always sufficient to raise souls because of a supernatural <note>original: "extranatural"</note> portion of understanding and reason which exists above them, known only to Heaven, the Destinies, and their power.

We must, therefore, entice these souls using supernatural and celestial powers duly administered. We do this through things that move the very harmony of the soul—including its imaginative, rational, and intellectual faculties. Examples of these include voices, songs, sounds, and enchantments, as well as religious elements such as prayers, conjurations, exorcisms, and other holy rites which may be very suitably <note>original: "commodiously"</note> used for this purpose.

---

# Heptameron: <note>From the Greek *heptameros*, meaning "of seven days," referring to planetary rituals for each day of the week.</note>

## MAGICAL ELEMENTS

->OF<-

## PETER of ABANO <note>original: "de Abano"; Pietro d'Abano (c. 1250–1316) was an Italian physician and philosopher often associated with the occult.</note>

### PHILOSOPHER.

<image-desc>An ornate square woodcut initial letter 'I' decorated with scrolling foliage and floral motifs.</image-desc>
In the previous book, which is the fourth book of *Agrippa* <note>Referring to the *Fourth Book of Occult Philosophy*, a manual of ceremonial magic attributed to Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa (1486–1535).</note>, magical ceremonies and initiations <term>initiations</term> <note>The formal rites of admission into a secret society or magical practice.</note> have been sufficiently discussed. However, because he seems to have written for the learned and those highly experienced in this art—and because he does not treat the ceremonies specifically, but rather speaks of them in general terms—it was therefore decided to add the *Magical Elements* of *Peter of Abano* to this volume. This is so that those who, until now,

<meta>This page continues the introduction to Peter of Abano’s "Heptameron," moving from the general purpose of the text to the specific practical instructions for creating a magical circle.</meta>

<page-num>74</page-num>
<header>Magical Elements,</header>

are too ignorant and have not yet experienced <note>original: "tasted of"</note> magical superstitions, may have these instructions ready so that they might practice them. In this book, we see a kind of introduction to "magical vanity" <note>Magical vanity: A common period term for the study of the occult, often used with a mix of fascination and moral caution by contemporary authors.</note>. As if the reader were currently practicing, they can observe the distinct roles of spirits and how they may be drawn into conversation and communication. It details what must be done every day and every hour, and how things should be recited <note>original: "read"</note>, as if they were described syllable by syllable.

In short, this book contains the principles of magical methods <note>original: "conveyances"</note>. However, because the greatest power is attributed to the circles (which serve as fortresses to keep the practitioners safe from evil spirits), we will first discuss the construction of a circle.

## Concerning the Circle and Its Construction

The form of these circles is not always the same; rather, it changes according to the order of the spirits being summoned, as well as their locations, times, days, and hours. When making a circle, you must consider the time of year, the day, and the hour of its creation; which spirits you intend to call, which star and region they belong to, and what their specific roles are.

Therefore, let there be drawn three circles with a width <note>original: "latitude"</note> of nine feet, spaced a hand’s breadth apart from each other. 

1. In the middle circle, first write the name of the hour in which you are working. 
2. In the second place, write the name of the Angel of that hour. 
3. In the third place, write the Sigil <term>sigil</term> <note>A magical symbol or seal representing a specific spirit or angel.</note> of the Angel of the hour. 
4. Fourthly, write the name of the Angel that rules the day in which you are working, along with the names of his ministers <note>Subordinate spirits or celestial assistants.</note>. 
5. In the fifth place, write the name of the current season <note>original: "present time"</note>. 
6. Sixthly, write the name of the spirits ruling that period of time and their presiding officers <note>original: "Presidents"</note>. 
7. Seventhly, write the name of the head of the zodiac sign <note>original: "Signe"</note> ruling in that part of time.

<summary>This page explains the purpose of the Heptameron as a practical manual for summoning spirits and provides the initial geometric and inscribed requirements for constructing a protective magical circle.</summary>

time in which you are working. Eighth, the name of the Earth, according to the period of time in which you are working. Ninth, to complete the middle circle, write the names of the Sun and the Moon according to the same rule of time; for as the time changes, the names must also be changed. In the outermost circle, draw in the four angles the names of the presiding <note>original: "presidential"</note> angels of the air for the day you are performing this work—namely, the name of the King and his three ministers. Outside the circle, at the four angles, let pentagons <note>original: "Pentagones"</note> be drawn. In the inner circle, write four divine names with crosses placed between them in the middle of the circle. Specifically, write *Alpha* <note>Alpha (Α): the first letter of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing the beginning.</note> toward the east and *Omega* <note>Omega (Ω): the last letter of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing the end.</note> toward the west, and let a cross divide the center of the circle. Once the circle is finished according to these rules, you shall proceed.

## Concerning the Names of the Hours and the Angels Who Rule Them

It should also be known that the angels rule the hours in a sequential order, according to the movement of the heavens and the planets to which they are subject. Thus, the spirit that governs the day also rules the first hour of that day. The second spirit in order governs the second hour, the third governs the third hour, and so on. When the seven planets and their hours have completed their cycle <note>original: "revolution"</note>, the order returns to the first spirit who rules the day. Therefore, we will first discuss the names of the hours.

| 2. *Janor.* | 2. *Barol.* |
| 3. *Nasnia.* | 3. *Thami.* |
| 4. *Salla.* | 4. *Athar.* |

## The Consecrations and Blessings <note>original: "Benedictions"</note>: and first, the Blessing of the Circle.

When the Circle has been properly completed <note>original: "ritely perfected"</note>, sprinkle it with holy or purifying water and say:
> *You shall cleanse me with hyssop <note>original: "hysop"</note>, O Lord, and I shall be clean; You shall wash me, and I shall be whiter than snow.* <note>This is a ritual recitation of Psalm 51:7, used historically in the "Asperges" rite to purify a space or person before a religious or magical ceremony.</note>

<page-num>79</page-num>
<header>Of Peter de Abano <note>Peter de Abano (c. 1250–1316) was an Italian philosopher and astrologer; this text, the *Heptameron*, is traditionally attributed to him.</note>.</header>

## The Blessing <note>original: "Benediction"</note> of the perfumes.

The God of Abraham, the God of Isaac, and the God of Jacob, bless these created things of various kinds, so that they may be filled with the power and virtue of their scents <note>original: "odours"</note>. Let this be so that neither the enemy nor any false imagination may be able to enter into them, through our Lord Jesus Christ, etc. Then, let them be sprinkled with holy water.

## The Exorcism of the fire upon which the perfumes are to be put.

The fire which is to be used for ritual incense burnings <term>suffumigations</term> <note>The act of burning incense or herbs to create smoke for ritual purification or to attract spirits.</note> must be in a new vessel made of clay <note>original: "earth"</note> or iron. Let it be exorcised in this manner: 

"I exorcise you, O creature of fire, by him by whom all things were made, so that you immediately cast away every phantom <note>original: "phantasme"</note> from yourself, and so that it shall not be able to do any harm in any way." 

Then say: 

"Bless, O Lord, this creature of fire, and sanctify it, so that it may be blessed to set forth the praise of your holy name, and that no harm may come to the practitioners <note>original: "Exercisers"</note> or spectators, through our Lord Jesus Christ, etc."

## Regarding the Garment and Pentacle.

Let the clothing be a priest's garment, if possible; but if it cannot be obtained, let it be made of clean linen. Then, take this pentacle <note>A magical symbol or talisman, often inscribed on parchment or metal, used for protection and command over spirits.</note> made during the day and hour of Mercury while the Moon is waxing <note>original: "increasing"; in astrology, the waxing moon is associated with growth and success.</note>, written upon parchment made of kidskin <note>parchment made from the skin of a young goat</note>. But first, let the Mass of the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Mass of the holy Ghost"</note> be said over it, and let it be sprinkled with the water of baptism.

## A Prayer <note>original: "Oration"</note> to be said when the Ritual Garment <note>original: "Vesture"</note> is put on.

Ancor, Amacor, Amides, Theodonias, Anitor <note>These are "Names of Power," divine names used in medieval and Renaissance magic to invoke spiritual authority.</note>, by the merits of your Angel, O Lord, I will put on the garments of salvation, so that I may bring to fulfillment what I desire: through you, the most holy Adonay <note>A Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord."</note>, whose kingdom endures forever and ever. Amen.

# Of the manner of working.

Let the Moon be waxing <note>original: "increasing"</note> and in an equal state <note>Likely referring to the Moon being in a balanced zodiacal position or "equal" in dignity.</note>, if possible, and let her not be <term>combust</term> <note>In astrology, a planet is "combust" when it is too close to the sun, which was believed to weaken its influence.</note>.

The practitioner <note>original: "Operator"</note> must be clean and purified for nine days before the beginning of the work; he should go to confession and receive the Holy Communion. He should have ready the incense <note>original: "perfume"</note> assigned to the specific day on which he intends to perform the work. He must also have holy water obtained from a priest, a new clay <note>original: "earthen"</note> vessel containing fire, a ritual robe <note>original: "Vesture"</note>, and a pentacle; all these things must be properly and duly consecrated and prepared.

Let one of the assistants <note>original: "servants"</note> carry the clay vessel full of fire and the incense, while another carries the book, and another the garment and pentacle. Let the master carry the sword, over which a Mass of the Holy Spirit must have been said. In the middle of the sword, the name **Agla** <note>A kabbalistic acronym for the Hebrew "Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai," meaning "You are mighty forever, O Lord."</note> † should be written, and on the other side, the name † **On** <note>A name for God often associated with divine light.</note> †. As he goes to the consecrated place, let him continually recite litanies while the assistants give the responses. When he arrives at the place where he will establish the circle, let him draw the lines of the circle as we have previously instructed. After he has made it, let him sprinkle the circle with holy water, saying:

<margin>Wash me, O Lord, etc.</margin>
"Sprinkle me, O Lord, etc." <note>original: "Asperges me Domine, &c." This refers to Psalm 51:7, traditionally recited during the ritual sprinkling of holy water.</note>

The Master, therefore, must be purified through fasting, celibacy <note>original: "chastity"</note>, and abstinence from all luxury for three full days before the day of the operation. On the day he performs the work, being dressed in pure garments and equipped with pentacles, incense, and other necessary items, let him enter the circle. He shall call the angels from the four corners <note>original: "parts"</note> of the world who govern the seven planets, the seven days of the week, the colors, and the metals; their names will be listed in their appropriate sections. Kneeling and invoking each of these angels specifically, let him say: "O aforementioned angels, be helpers to my petition." <note>original: "O Angels supradicti, estote adjutores meæ petitioni."</note>

<meta>catchword: &</meta>

*...and for my help, in my affairs and requests.* <note>original: "& in adjutorium mihi, in meis rebus & petitionibus."</note>

Then let him call the Angels from the four parts of the world who rule the Air on the same day that he performs the work or ritual <note>original: "experiment"</note>. And having specifically implored all the Names and Spirits written in the Circle, let him say: *O you all, I adjure you and call you to witness by the throne of Adonay, by the Holy, O God, the Strong, the Immortal, the Advocate <note>original: "Paracletos"</note>, Alpha and Omega, and by these three secret names: Agla, On, Tetragrammaton, that today you must fulfill what I desire.* <note>original: "O vos omnes, adjuro atque contestor per sedem Adonay, per Hagios, ò Theos, Ischyros, Athanatos, Paracletos, Alpha & Omega, & per hæc tria nomina secreta, Agla, On, Tetragrammaton, quòd hodie debeatis adimplere quod cupio."</note>

When these things are performed, let him read the Conjuration assigned for the day on which he performs the ritual, as we have spoken of before. But if they are stubborn <note>original: "partinacious"</note> and rebellious <note>original: "refractory"</note>, and will not yield themselves in obedience—neither to the Conjuration assigned to the day nor to the prayers made before—then use the Conjurations and Exorcisms that follow.

## An Exorcism of the Spirits of the Air.

We, *created in the image of God, endowed with the power of God, and created by His will, through the most powerful and strengthened name of God, El—the strong and wonderful—we exorcise you* (here he shall name the Spirits he would have appear, of whatever order they may be) *and command you through Him who spoke and it was done, and by all the names of God, and by the names Adonay, El, Elohim, Elohe, Zebaoth, Elion, Escerchie, Jah, Tetragrammaton, Sadai, the Lord God Most High: we exorcise you and powerfully command you, that you appear immediately to us here beside the Circle in a beautiful form—specifically a human one—and without any deformity or twisting <note>original: "tortuositate"</note>. Come, all you who are of such a kind, because we command you by the name Y and V <note>Likely referring to the first two letters of the Tetragrammaton, Yod and He (V/H).</note> which Adam heard and spoke; and by the name of God, Agla, which Lot heard and was saved with his family; and by the name Joth, which Jacob heard from the Angel struggling with him, and was delivered from the hand of his brother Esau:* <note>original: "Nos facti ad imaginem Dei, dotati potentia Dei, & ejus facti voluntate, per potentissimum & corroboratum nomen Dei El, forte & admirabile vos exorcizamus... & imperamus per eum qui dixit, & factum est..."</note> and by the name *Anephexeton, which Aaron heard and, by speaking it, became wise; and by the name Zebaoth, which Moses spoke, and all the rivers and marshes of the land of Egypt [turned to blood],* <note>original: "Anephexeton, quod Aaron audivit, & loquens, & sapiens factus est: & per nomen Zebaoth, quod Moses nominavit, & omnia flumina & paludes de terra Ægypti..."</note>

...were turned into blood. And by the name *Ecerchie Oriston*, which Moses named, and all the rivers bubbled forth frogs, and they crawled up into the houses of the Egyptians, destroying everything. And by the name *Elion*, which Moses named, and there was hail such as there has not been from the beginning of the world. And by the name *Adonay*, which Moses named, and there were locusts, which appeared upon the land of the Egyptians and ate what remained after the hail. And by the name *Schemes amathia*, which Joshua called upon, and the Sun stayed its course. 

And by the name *Alpha and Omega*, which Daniel named, and he destroyed Bel and killed the Dragon. <note>This refers to the "Bel and the Dragon" story from the Apocryphal additions to the Book of Daniel.</note> And in the name *Emmanuel*, which the three youths—Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego—sang in the furnace of burning fire, and they were delivered. And by the name *Hagios* <note>original: "Hagios," Greek for "Holy"</note>, and the throne of *Adonay*, and by *O Theos, Ischyros, Athanatos, Paracletus* <note>original: "o Theos, Iscytos, Athanatos, Paracletus." These are Greek titles for God: O God, the Strong, the Immortal, the Advocate/Comforter.</note>; and by these three secret names, *Agla, On, Tetragrammaton*, I adjure and call you to witness. 

And by these names, and by the other names of our Lord God Almighty, living and true—you who by your own fault were cast out of Heaven even to the place of hell—we exorcise you and powerfully <note>original: "viriliter," literally "manfully" or "with strength"</note> command you. We command you by Him who spoke and it was done, whom all creatures obey; and by that terrible judgment of God; and by the sea—uncertain to all, like glass—which moves before the sight of the divine majesty, and is powerful. 

And by the four divine living creatures † <note>The symbol "T." or "†" in the text indicates where the practitioner should make the Sign of the Cross.</note> going before the seat of divine majesty, having eyes before and behind; and by the fire surrounding His throne; and by the holy Angels of Heaven †; and by that which is named the Church of God; and by the highest wisdom of Almighty God, we powerfully exorcise you, so that you appear here before us in front of the Circle, to perform our will in all things as it shall please us. 

We command you by the seat of *Baldachia*, and by this name *Primeumaton*, which Moses named, and Dathan, Korah, and Abiram were sunk or swallowed into the caverns of the abyss. <note>A reference to the biblical story in Numbers 16, where the earth opened to swallow those who rebelled against Moses.</note> And by the power of this name *Primeumaton*, the whole host of Heaven compelling you, we curse you, we deprive you of every office, place, and joy you have, even into the depths of the abyss. And we place and banish you into eternal fire, and into the lake of fire and sulfur, until the final day of judgment, unless you immediately appear here before us, in front of the Circle, to perform our will. In all things, come by these names: *Adonay Zebaoth, Adonay Amioram*. Come, come, *Adonay* commands you...

...indeed, Shaddai <note>original: "Saday," a Hebrew name for God often translated as "Almighty"</note>, the most powerful and most dread King of kings, whose power no creature can escape. ## A Prayer to God, to be said in the four parts of the world, in the Circle.

> Amorule, Taneha, Latisten, Rabur, Taneha, Latisten, Escha, Aladia, Alpha and Omega, Leyste, Oriston, Adonay: O my most merciful heavenly Father, have mercy upon me, although I am a sinner. Make the arm of your power appear in me this day (even though I am your unworthy child) against these obstinate and pernicious spirits, so that I, by your will, may be made a contemplator of your divine works, and may be enlightened with all wisdom, and always worship and glorify your name. I humbly implore and beseech you, that these spirits which I call by your judgment, may be bound and constrained to come, and give true and perfect answers to those things which I shall ask them. Let them declare and show to us those things which shall be commanded them by me or us, without hurting any creature, nor injuring or terrifying me or my companions, nor hurting any other creature, and frightening no one; instead, let them be obedient to my requests in all these things which I command them. Then let him stand in the middle of the Circle, and hold his hand towards the Pentacle, and say: *By the Pentacle of Solomon I have called you; give me a true answer.* <note>original: "Per Pentaculum Salomonis advocavi, dent mihi responsum verum."</note>

Then let him say: *Beralanensis, Baldachiensis, Paumachia and Apologia, your seats; by the Kings and great powers, and the most powerful princes, by the spirit <note>original: "genio"</note>, Liachidae, ministers of the infernal <note>original: "tartareae," referring to Tartarus</note> regions: Primac,*

<summary>This page concludes the adjuration of spirits with a final threat of divine fire and transitions into a prayer for God's protection and authority. It also provides the specific ritual actions for using the Pentacle of Solomon to compel truthful answers from the summoned spirits.</summary>

[...I conjure you] as the prince of the seat of the Apology in the ninth cohort <note>original: "hic princeps sedis Apologiæ nona cohorte"</note>:
> I invoke you, and by invoking I conjure you; and, fortified by the power of the supreme Majesty, I powerfully command you—by him who spoke and it was done, and whom all creatures obey—and by this ineffable name, Tetragrammaton <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note> יהוה Jehovah, in which the whole world was formed; which, when heard, the elements collapse, the air is shaken, the sea flows backward, the fire is extinguished, the earth trembles, and all the armies of celestial, terrestrial, and infernal beings tremble, are troubled, and fall down. I command you to come quickly and without delay, setting aside all excuses, from all parts of the world, and respond rationally to everything I shall ask you. Come peacefully, visible, and easy to speak with <note>original: "affabiles"</note>, manifesting now and without delay what we desire. You are conjured by the name of the eternal, living, and true God, Helioren, to fulfill our commands and persist always until the end and my intended purpose, being visible and easy to speak with, using a clear and intelligible voice for us, without any ambiguity.

## Visions and Apparitions.

When these things are rightly performed, infinite visions will appear, as well as phantasms playing organs and all kinds of musical instruments. This is done by the spirits so that the companions, driven by terror, might flee from the Circle, because the spirits can do nothing against the Master. After this, you will see an infinite number of archers with an infinite multitude of horrible beasts, which carry themselves as if they intended to devour the companions; yet let them fear nothing. Then the Priest or Master, stretching his hand toward the Pentacle <note>A magical amulet or diagram, usually drawn on parchment or metal, used to constrain spirits.</note>, shall say:
> Let your iniquity flee from here by the power of the banner of God <note>original: "Fugiat hinc iniquitas vestra, virtute vexilli Dei."</note>.

And then the spirits are compelled to obey the master, and the companions shall see nothing more.

Then let the Exorcist say, stretching out his hand to the Pentacle, 
> Behold the Pentacle of Solomon, which I have brought before your presence. Behold the person of the exorcist in the middle of the Exorcism, who is perfectly fortified by God, fearless, and cautious, who, being powerful in strength, has invoked and calls you by exorcising. Come, therefore, with haste in the power of these names—Aye Saraye, Aye Saraye, Aye Saraye <note>Transliterations of "Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh," the Hebrew "I Am That I Am."</note>—do not delay your coming, by the eternal names of the living and true God, Eloy, Archima, Rabur, and by this present Pentacle, which...

<header>Magical Elements,</header>

*[...God] powerfully commands you: and by the power of the celestial spirits who are your masters: and by the person of the exorcist, I conjure you to come quickly and obey your teacher, who is called Octinomos <note>A "barbarous name" or magical name used in ceremonial magic to assert authority over spirits.</note>. When these things are done, you shall whistle in the four corners of the world. And you will immediately see great movements: and when you see them, say: Why do you delay? Why do you wait? What are you doing? Prepare yourselves and obey your teacher, in the name of the Lord Bathat, or Vachat rushing over Abrac, coming upon, Abeor over Aberer <note>original: "...Bathat, vel Vachat super Abrac ruens, superveniens, Abeor super Aberer." These are ritualistic names used to compel the spirits.</note>.*

*Then they will immediately come in their own proper form. And when you see them near the Circle, show them the Pentacle <note>A magical talisman, often a disk made of parchment or metal inscribed with symbols, used to protect the magician and control spirits.</note> covered with a sacred linen cloth; let it be uncovered, and say: "Behold your conclusion; do not become disobedient." And suddenly you will see them in a peaceful form: and they will say to you, "Ask what you want, for we are ready to fulfill all your commands, because the Lord has subjected us to these things." When the spirits have appeared, then say: "Welcome, Spirits, or most noble kings, because I have called you through him at whose name every knee bows—of those in heaven, on earth, and under the earth <note>A reference to Philippians 2:10.</note>: in whose hand are all the kingdoms of kings, nor is there anyone who can oppose his Majesty. I constrain you to remain here before the circle, visible and easy to speak with <note>original: "affabiles"</note>, as long and as constant as I require, and do not depart without my permission <note>original: "licentia"</note> until you have truthfully and without any deception performed my will, by the power of that strength which set the sea its boundary, which it cannot pass, and by the law of that power, it does not cross its limits—the power of the most high God, King, and Lord, who created all things. Amen."* Then command what you wish, and it shall be done. Afterwards, give them license to depart <note>A formal dismissal to ensure the spirits return to their own realm and do not linger.</note> in this way:

> *† In the name of the Father, † of the Son, and † of the Holy Spirit, go in peace to your places: and let there be peace between us and you; be ready to come when called <note>original: "† In nomine Patris, † Filii, & † Spiritus sancti, ite in pace ad loca vestra : & pax sit inter nos & vos, parati sitis venire vocati."</note>.*

These are the things which *Peter de Abano* <note>Pietro d'Abano (c. 1250–1316), an Italian philosopher and astrologer to whom this text, the 'Heptameron', is attributed.</note> has spoken concerning *Magical Elements.*

But so that you may better understand the method of constructing a Circle, I will provide one diagram; for example, if someone wished to make a Circle in the springtime for the first hour of the Lord’s Day <note>Sunday.</note>, it must be done in the same manner as the following figure.

## The design of a Circle for the first hour of the Lord’s Day <note>Sunday</note> in Springtime.

<image-desc>A circular magic diagram surrounded by four five-pointed stars at the corners of the composition. 

On the far left, the word "Cynabal" <note>original: "Cynabal." refers to Cinnabar, often used as a pigment or incense in rituals.</note> is written vertically. On the far right, the word "Tus" <note>original: "Tus" is Latin for frankincense.</note> is written vertically.

The central diagram consists of three concentric rings:
1. The outer ring contains a series of names and titles written clockwise starting from the top: "King Varcan" <note>original: "Varcan Rex"</note>, "Michael" (followed by astrological symbols including a Sun symbol), "Lardid.", "Hurafapel:", "Talvi.", "Caracasa.", "Core", "Amatiel.", "Commissoros.", "Spugliguel:", "Atnaday.", "Abraym", "Aguista.", and "Yayn.".
2. The middle ring contains four divine names separated by crosses: "+ Adonay <note>A Hebrew name for God, meaning "My Lord."</note> /", "++ Eloy <note>A Hebrew name for God, "Elohi."</note> /", "++ Agla <note>A kabbalistic acronym for "Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai," meaning "You, O Lord, are mighty forever."</note> /", and "+ Tetragrammaton <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note>".
3. The innermost circle is divided by a central cross into four quadrants containing the words "Alpha and Omega" <note>original: "Alpha et ω." A Greek title for God meaning "the beginning and the end."</note>. At the very bottom of this inner circle, the word "Andas" is written upside down, positioned below two equal-armed crosses.</image-desc>

It now remains for us to explain the week and its various days, beginning first with the Lord’s Day <note>Sunday</note>.

# The Conjuration of the Lord’s Day.

I conjure and confirm over you, strong and holy Angels of God, in the name of Adonay, Eye, Eye, Eya, who is he who was, is, and shall be, Eye, Abraye; and in the name Saday, Cados, Cados, Cados <note>original: "Saday, Cados," from Hebrew *Shaddai* (Almighty) and *Kadosh* (Holy)</note>, sitting on high upon the Cherubim. 

I conjure you by the great name of God himself, strong and powerful, and exalted above all the heavens, Eye, Saraye, the shaper of the ages, who created the world, the heaven, the earth, the sea, and all things that are in them on the first day, and sealed them with his holy name Phaa. 

I conjure you by the names of the holy Angels who rule in the fourth army and serve before the most powerful Salamia, a great and honored Angel. I conjure you by the name of the star, which is the Sun, and by its sign, and by the immense name of the living God, and by all the names aforesaid. 

I conjure you, Michael, great angel, who are the ruler of the Lord’s Day <note>Sunday</note>; and by the name Adonay, the God of Israel, who created the world and whatever is in it, that you work for me, and fulfill all my petition, according to my will and my desire, in my business and... <note>The text continues on the next page, likely concluding with the specific request of the practitioner.</note>

...*my cause* <note>original: "causa mea"</note>. And here you shall declare your cause and business, and for what purpose you perform this Conjuration.

The Spirits of the Air of the Lord’s Day <note>Sunday</note> are under the North Wind; their nature is to procure gold, gems, carbuncles <note>a bright red gemstone, typically a garnet or ruby</note>, and riches; to help one obtain favor and goodwill; to dissolve the enmities of men; to raise men to honors; and to carry or take away illnesses. But the manner in which they appear has already been discussed in the previous book of *Magical Ceremonies* <note>This refers to the *Fourth Book of Occult Philosophy*, often attributed to Henry Cornelius Agrippa, to which this text serves as a supplement</note>.

## The Conjuration of Tuesday

I conjure and confirm upon you, strong and holy Angels, by the names Ya, Ya, Ya; He, He, He; Va, Hy, Hy; Ha, Ha, Ha; Va, Va, Va; An, An, An; Aie, Aie, Aie; El, Ay, Elibra, Elohim, Elohim; and by the names of the high God himself, who made the dry land appear from the water, and called it Earth, and produced trees and herbs from it, and sealed it with his precious, honored, feared, and holy name. 

I also conjure you by the names of the angels ruling in the fifth army, who serve Acimoy—the great, strong, powerful, and honored Angel—and by the name of the Star which is Mars. I conjure upon you, Samael, the great Angel who is set over the day of Tuesday, by the names mentioned before and by the names of Adonai, the living and true God, that you work for me and fulfill my requests... <note>original Latin: "Conjuro & confirmo super vos, Angeli fortes & sancti, per nomen Ya... & per nomina ipsius alti Dei, qui fecit aquam aridam apparere, & vocavit terram, & produxit arbores, & herbas de ea, & sigillavit super eam cum precioso, honorato, metuendo & sancto nomine suo : & per nomen angelorum dominantium in quinto exercitu, qui serviunt Acimoy Angelo magno, forti, potenti, & honorato : & per nomen Stellæ, quæ est Mars : & per nomina prædicta conjuro super te Samael, Angele magne, qui præpositus es diei Martis : & per nomina Adonay, Dei vivi & veri, quod pro me labores, & adimpleas, &c."</note> 

(The rest of the prayer follows the same format as the **Conjuration of Sunday**).

The Spirits of the Air of Tuesday are under the East Wind. Their nature is to cause wars, mortality, death, and fires <note>original: "combustions"</note>; they can provide two thousand soldiers at a time, and bring about death, illness, or health. You may find the description of how they appear in the previous book.

# The Conjuration of Wednesday

I conjure and confirm you, strong, holy, and powerful angels, in the name of the strong, most fearsome, and blessed Yah, Adonai, Elohim, Shaddai, Shaddai, Shaddai, Ehyeh, Ehyeh, Ehyeh, Asamie, Asaraie <note>original: "Conjuro & confirmo vos angeli fortes, ſancti & potentes, in nomine fortis, metuendiſſimi & ben dicti Ja, Adonay, Eloim, Saday, Saday, Saday, Eie, Eie, Eie, Aſamie, Aſaraie." These are various Hebrew names for God common in medieval and Renaissance grimoires.</note>: and in the name of Adonai, the God of Israel, who created the great lights to distinguish day from night: and by the name of all the angels serving in the second host before Tetra, the great, strong, and powerful angel: and by the name of the star, which is Mercury: and by the name of the seal, which is sealed by the most strong and honored God: by all these things mentioned above, I conjure you, great Angel Raphael, who are set over the fourth day <note>Wednesday</note>: and by the holy name which was written on the forehead of Aaron, the priest of the Most High Creator: and by the names of the angels who are confirmed in the grace of the Savior: and by the name of the throne of the living creatures having six wings, that you may work for me, etc. <note>original: "Conjuro... per nomen ſedis Animalium, habentium ſenas alas, quòd pro me laboret, &c." The "living creatures" refers to the four celestial beings described in Ezekiel and Revelation.</note> As in the Conjuration of Sunday.

The Spirits of the Air of Wednesday are subject to the southwest wind. Their nature is to give all metals; to reveal all earthly things past, present, and to come; to pacify judges, to give victories in war, to rebuild <note>original: "re-edifie"</note> and teach experiments and all lost <note>original: "decayed"</note> sciences, and to change bodies mixed of

The manner in which they appear has been described previously.

# The Conjuration of Thursday.

I conjure and confirm upon you, holy Angels, by the names *Kadosh, Kadosh, Kadosh, Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh* <note>original: "Cados, Cados, Cados, Eschereie, Eschereie, Eschereie"; these are Latinized Hebrew names for God, meaning "Holy" and "I am that I am."</note>, the strong establisher of the ages, *Cantine, Jaym, Janic, Anic, Calbar, Sabbac, Berifay, Alnaym*: and by the name *Adonai* <note>original: "Adonay"; a Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord."</note>, who created fish and creeping things in the waters, and birds upon the face of the earth, flying toward the heavens on the fifth day: and by the names of the Angels serving in the sixth army before the shepherd, the holy Angel and great and powerful prince: and by the name of the star, which is Jupiter: and by the name of the Seal <note>original: "Sigilli"; referring to the magical signature or symbol of the spirit or planet.</note>

The Spirits of the Air of Thursday are subject to the South wind. Their manner of appearing has been mentioned already.

The Spirits of the Air of Friday are subject to the West wind; their nature is to give silver; to excite men and incline them to lust <note>original: "luxury"; in Early Modern English, this term often referred specifically to lechery or sexual desire rather than material wealth.</note>; to reconcile enemies through lust; and to make marriages; to allure men to love women; to cause or take away infirmities; and to do all things which have motion.

-># WEDNESDAY<-

| Hours of the day <note>In Renaissance magic, the "planetary hours" divide the period from sunrise to sunset into twelve equal parts, and sunset to sunrise into another twelve. <page-num>107</page-num>
<header>Concerning Peter de Abano</header>

However, it should be noted in passing that the first hour of the day—in every country and in every season—is to begin at sunrise, when the sun first appears rising on the horizon. Additionally, the first hour of the night is considered the thirteenth hour, counting from the first hour of the day. <note>In traditional planetary hours, the period from sunrise to sunset is divided into twelve equal hours, and the period from sunset to sunrise is divided into another twelve. Therefore, the "thirteenth hour" of the full cycle is the first hour of the night.</note> But enough has been said regarding these matters.

# ISAGOGE:
## <term>An introduction or introductory manual (from the Greek 'eisagoge')</term> An Introductory Discourse on the nature of such Spirits as operate within the sublunary <term>The region below the moon; in historical cosmology, this referred to the Earth and its atmosphere, the realm of change, decay, and elemental spirits</term> bounds; their origins, names, duties, deceptions, power, prophecies, and miracles; and how they may be banished and driven away.

---

### By Georgius Pictorius of Villingen <note>original: Geo. Pictorius Villinganus</note>, Doctor of Medicine <note>original: Physick</note>.

->In a conversation between<-

### CASTOR and POLLUX.

**Castor.** The Greeks report that Castor and Pollux both came from the same egg; but I hardly believe this because of the difference in your temperaments. For you are drawn to the heavens, while she <note>The text uses "she," which may refer to a feminine personification of the earth or the twins' sister, Helen; original: "she"</note> focuses on the earth and on slaughter.

**Pollux.** And perhaps that is where the argument originated: "The Greeks were always granted the freedom to lie."

**Castor.** Precisely.

<meta>Catchword: Pollux.</meta>

<summary>This page introduces an "Isagoge" or introductory treatise by Georgius Pictorius regarding the nature and behavior of spirits on Earth. It begins with a dialogue between the mythological twins Castor and Pollux, discussing Greek reputation for storytelling and their own differing natures.</summary>

<keywords>Georgius Pictorius, Isagoge, sublunary spirits, demonology, Castor and Pollux, Greek mythology, early modern medicine, spiritual hierarchy</keywords>

--- Page 148 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Castor and Pollux regarding the nature of spirits and the "Isagoge" by Georgius Pictorius.</meta>

<page-num>110</page-num>
<header>A Discourse,</header>

*Pollux*. But it is not to be supposed that the *Greeks* are dishonest in all things; rather, they are like many others when they speak from a <term>three-footed thing</term> <note>original: "three-footed thing," referring to a tripod, the traditional seat of the Oracle at Delphi</note>; of which the poet *Ovid* also speaks in verse:

---

->*The Greeks do not invent everything.* <note>original: "Nec fingunt omnia Græci"</note><-

<margin>Man is a God to man. <note>original: "Homo homini Deus"</note></margin>
*Castor*. In this proverb, I declare they are most truthful, without any exception: that is, <term>one man is a spirit to another</term> <note>original: "ἄνθρωπος ἀνθρώπῳ δαιμόνιον"</note>—meaning, one man is a devil to another.

*Pollux*. Why do you believe this to be most true, *Castor*?

<margin>Man is a devil to man. <note>original: "Homo homini diabolus"</note></margin>
*Castor*. Truly, that man is a devil and a ravening wolf to his fellow man, as daily events certainly prove. We need only look at the treacheries that one man invents daily against another: the robberies, thefts, lootings, rapes, murders, deceits, adulteries, and a hundred other vipers of this nature. Fathers persecute their sons with a serpentine and poisonous bite; one friend seeks to devour another, and even a guest is not safe with his host.

*Pollux*. I admit that you speak the truth; but from what I hear, you misunderstand the <term>etymology</term> <note>the origin and historical development of a word</note> of the word compared in this proverb. For *Daemon* <note>In the original Greek, "daemon" referred to a guiding spirit or lesser deity, not necessarily an evil one</note> here is not a horrible or hateful name, but the name of one who administers
<margin>Pliny, Book 2, Chapter 7. <note>original: "Plin. lib. 2. chap. 7."</note></margin>
help or assistance to another, and whom *Pliny* calls a God.

*Castor*. Therefore, do you claim that the word *Daemon* in this proverb signifies anything other than a cunning and malicious accuser?

*Pollux*. You have hit the mark. For everyone may easily perceive that there are more *Daemons* than just that <term>sublunary</term> <note>of the earth; literally "under the moon"</note> one which you understand, provided they have not carelessly read the opinions of the most excellent *Plato*.

*Castor*. I desire, then, that you would not conceal his writings, but that I might understand the core of his meaning.

*Pollux*. I will grant your wish, for I truly delight in discussing this subject with you. Listen, therefore, and pay attention.

<meta>catchword: Plato</meta>

<summary>In this dialogue, Pollux corrects Castor’s cynical interpretation of a proverb, explaining that the word "Daemon" originally referred to a helpful spirit or deity rather than an inherently evil devil. Pollux prepares to explain Plato’s more nuanced philosophy regarding these spirits.</summary>

<keywords>Castor and Pollux, Georgius Pictorius, Daemon, etymology, Pliny the Elder, Plato, human nature, Greek proverbs</keywords>

--- Page 149 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Castor and Pollux concerning the various classifications of spirits or "demons," specifically exploring the hierarchy attributed to Plato and other classical philosophers.</meta>

<page-num>111</page-num>
<header>Of the Nature of Spirits.</header>

<margin>Three ranks of spirits.</margin>
<margin>The first rank of spirits.</margin>
Plato divided the orders of spirits (or "demons") into three ranks. Just as they are distinct in the greatness of their dignity, they are also different in the locations they occupy. He assigns the first order to those spirits whose bodies are nourished by the purest element of air, woven together as if with shining threads. These do not contain enough of the element of fire to be visible to the eye, nor do they contain enough of the earth to be felt or touched. They inhabit the heavenly realm, attending and waiting upon their Prince—who cannot be described by any human tongue—and following the commands of the most wise God.

<margin>The second rank.</margin>
The second rank consists of those spirits that Apuleius calls "rational animals," who are subject to emotions in their minds and are eternal in their lifespan. By this, he means the fallen spirits spread throughout the regions between the Moon and the Earth, under the rule of their prince, Beelzebub. Before the fall of Lucifer, these spirits had pure, radiant bodies; now, after their sin, they wander back and forth in a form like air, similar to the first group.

*Castor.* I do not believe these are the ones referred to in the Greek proverb, for these spirits cause harm and are the accusers and betrayers of humanity. But please, continue.

<margin>The third rank.</margin>
<margin>Plato and Aristotle called "demons."</margin>
*Pollux.* The third rank of spirits is of a divine nature, which Hermes calls "a divine miracle to man," provided he does not fall away from the noble state of his original form. Therefore, the Greeks and Plato have called this kind "Demons" <note>original: *Dæmons*; in the classical sense, this refers to a guardian spirit or a being between gods and men, rather than an evil spirit</note>, meaning a god; this is so that man may be like God and be helpful and beneficial to one another. And so also (as the Syrian writer testifies) we know that Plato himself was called a "Demon" because he had explained many profound matters for the good of the state. Likewise, Aristotle was called one because he wrote extensively about <term>sublunaries</term> <note>the region of the universe below the moon, subject to change and decay</note> and all things perceived by the senses. Homer calls both God and evil spirits "Demons" without making a distinction between them.

<meta>Catchword: Castor.</meta>

<summary>Pollux describes Plato's three-fold classification of spirits, distinguishing between celestial attendants, the fallen spirits under Beelzebub, and the divine "demon" or guiding spirit within wise men like Plato and Aristotle.</summary>
<keywords>Plato, Apuleius, Beelzebub, Lucifer, Hermes Trismegistus, Aristotle, Homer, demons, celestial hierarchy, sublunary</keywords>

--- Page 150 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Castor and Pollux regarding the hierarchy of spirits, moving from the general classification of "Lunaries" to the specific nature of the devil's knowledge and the physical transformation of fallen angels.</meta>

<page-num>112</page-num>
<header>A Discourse,</header>

*Castor.* You have already set sail, *Pollux*; therefore do not stop now. Tell me more concerning the office and employment of these spirits, to whom *Plato* assigns the second rank and calls *Lunaries* <note>spirits associated with the moon</note>.

*Pollux.* What should I say?

*Castor.* First, explain why you previously called these spirits "cunning and highly knowledgeable accusers."

<margin>
Why the devil is said to have great knowledge.
</margin>
*Pollux.* *Saint Augustine* explains this difficulty. He says that the word "Devil" signifies a cunning and highly knowledgeable sharp-wittedness in deception. By understanding the harmonious and natural "seeds" <note>original: "seminal permixtures," referring to the fundamental components of nature</note> of the elements, the devil learns the secrets and hidden qualities of humanity. Things that would normally happen slowly and naturally over time, he can bring to pass much faster by speeding up or forcing the works of nature through his own skill. He gives the example of the magicians of *Pharaoh*, who immediately produced frogs and serpents at the King’s command—creatures that nature produces much more slowly and gradually.

*Castor.* You have answered the question excellently, *Pollux*. But add something about the origin of those spirits who resist and reject virtue. I have often doubted their source, having been convinced that such *Erinnyes* <note>original: "Erynnes"; these were the Furies of Greek mythology, deities of vengeance</note> sent by God do not simply emerge from the earth.

<margin>
Book 2, distinction 7.
</margin>
*Pollux.* Holy Scripture mentions their rise in many places, and I will clear up your doubt. There are many various opinions among writers, but most commonly, *Peter Lombard*—in his book *The Sentences* <note>original: "Sentences"; a major medieval theological textbook</note>—draws his arguments from *Saint Augustine’s* commentary on *Genesis*. Specifically, he states that before his fall, the Devil was an *Archangel* and had a delicate, refined body created by God from the clearest and purest matter of the sky and air. However, after his fall from being an Archangel, he became an *apostate* <note>one who abandons their faith or principles</note>, and his body was no longer refined or subtle. Instead, his body was changed so that it might experience the effects of a coarser substance, taking on the quality of something more obscure, dark, and...

<meta>catchword: dense (original: spissious)</meta>

<summary>This page discusses the superior knowledge of demons, which St. Augustine attributes to their understanding of natural elements, and describes the theological transition of the Devil from an airy Archangel to a coarse, fallen spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Saint Augustine, Peter Lombard, fallen angels, Lunaries, demonic knowledge, transformation of spirits, Pharaoh's magicians, Erinnyes</keywords>

--- Page 151 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the fall of angels, transitioning from their transformation into devils to the specific scriptural evidence for their fall and their current dwelling place in the atmosphere.</meta>

<page-num>113</page-num>
<header>Of the Nature of Spirits.</header>

*...thickened air <note>original: "spiffious air"; referring to the belief that fallen spirits took on "spissous" or dense, air-like bodies as a form of punishment</note>. This being was also struck and overwhelmed by the raging madness of pride, drawing away many who were then angels with him into his service and bondage so that they might become devils. These devils now carry out their servile duties for him in this troublesome world. They compel—or rather, entice—the inhabitants of the world, undertaking various efforts and attempting many different and horrible studies that are abominable to God. They serve in slavery and thraldom to Beelzebub, their Prince, and are held in a very strong captivity.*

*Castor.* Do we find the fall of this Archangel anywhere else in Holy Scripture, besides the writings of *Saint Augustine*?

*Pollux.* We also have the fall of other angels.

*Castor.* Where?

<margin>The fall of Lucifer in Scripture.</margin>
*Pollux.* In *Isaiah* <note>original: "Esaias"</note>, whom you should honor more as an Evangelist than a Prophet because he so fully and plainly foretold Christ and his kingdom. He mentions this in his 14th chapter. We also have them mentioned by the Apostle *Peter*, when he says, *"God did not spare the angels who sinned"* (2 Peter 2:4).

*Castor.* Do the devils have a specific place appointed by God where they live?

<margin>The place appointed for the devils.</margin>
*Pollux.* *Peter*, the head of the Church, in the passage quoted earlier, affirms they were cast headlong into hell and are kept in the chains of hell. From there (as *Cortesius* <note>likely Paolo Cortese (1465–1510), a Renaissance theologian</note> says) they never leave, unless it is to tempt, provoke, and deceive men. But *Saint Augustine*, the Champion of Christ, in his book *The Agony of a Christian*, teaches that these kinds of spirits live in the sublunary region <note>the region below the moon, specifically the earth's atmosphere</note>. And in his 49th *Letter*, he explains that the darkest and most obscure part of the air is predestined for them as a prison, so that they may more easily cast their nets to entice and capture us.

<margin>The torments of the devils are eternal.</margin>
*Castor.* *Origen* <note>an early Christian scholar whose views on universal salvation were later deemed heretical</note> taught that the punishments of the devils are only appointed for a limited time; what do you say to this?

*Pollux.* What can I say? I can only mourn and regret the opinion of such a great man.

*Castor.* Why should you do that?

<sig>Q</sig>
<meta>catchword: Pol-</meta>

<summary>Castor and Pollux discuss the scriptural evidence for the fall of Lucifer and his angels, debating whether their current prison is located in hell or the lower atmosphere (the sublunary region).</summary>
<keywords>Lucifer, Beelzebub, Saint Augustine, Isaiah, Apostle Peter, Origen, sublunary region, hell, fallen angels, demonology</keywords>

--- Page 152 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Castor and Pollux regarding the hierarchy and motivations of fallen angels, specifically focusing on how they mimic divine structures to satisfy their pride.</meta>

<page-num>114</page-num>
<header>A Discourse,</header>

**Pollux.** Truly, if they have hardened themselves in wickedness, time cannot purge nor cleanse them; or if they desire it ever so much, they can never be able to accomplish it; for no space for repentance, nor time to recall that which is past, is given unto them.

**Castor.** You have now declared that the Archangel who became an apostate <term>apostate: one who renounces their religious or moral allegiance</term> drew away very many other Angels with him in his fall, so that they might become Devils. Could he not, out of his own inherent malice after his fall, sufficiently rule over his own province without the Angels that fell with him?

<margin>Why the Devil has familiars <note>original: "familiars"; here meaning subordinate spirits or attendants</note>.</margin>
**Pollux.** He could; but being lured by that pride which made him so arrogantly aspire to the Majesty of God, he strove so far to be like God that he chose very many ministers for himself. To these, he does not generally entrust everything he wants to have done, but assigns different things to different ministers, as may be gathered from the Hebrew astronomers.

<margin>Demons of Jove or Morning Spirits <note>original: "Demons Jovii or Antemerid."</note>.</margin>
Those whom we call *Spirits of Jove* and *Morning Spirits* <note>original: "Jovii, & Antemeridiani"</note> are false Gods—that is, liars who desire to be esteemed and adored as Gods. They are appointed as servants and slaves to the Devil, their Prince, so that they might lure the people of the earth into a common love of themselves, which *Plato* says is the fountain of all wickedness. They do this so that they may aspire to authority and greatness, covet being gorgeously clothed, and seek to be called Monarchs of the earth with perpetual power, and "Gods upon earth." <margin>Matthew 4.</margin> It is said that it was one of these who spoke to our Savior, showing him all the kingdoms of the earth and saying, "*All these things I will give you, if you will fall down and worship me.*"

<margin>The Southern Spirits.</margin>
**Castor.** Certainly, I have almost declared these *Midday Spirits* <note>original: "Meridiani"</note> to be a form of madness appearing in Psaphon the Libyan <note>original: "Libicus"; referring to a man who taught birds to call him a god</note>, Sappho, and the Emperor Diocletian, who all believed that the highest degree of happiness was to be reputed as Gods.

<margin>Psaphon, Sappho, and Diocletian as Gods. The trick of Psaphon <note>original: "The Comment of Sapho"</note>.</margin>
**Pollux.** Truly, this is a certain natural foolishness of the mind and of human nature. He began by taking certain little birds and teaching them, little by little, to pronounce human words and say, "*Psaphon is a great God*" <note>original: "μέγας Θεὸς Ψάφων"</note>. When these birds could pronounce the words...

<meta>catchword: per-</meta>

<summary>Pollux explains that fallen angels are beyond repentance and describes how the Devil organizes them into a hierarchy of 'ministers' to mimic God's majesty. They focus on luring humans into pride and the desire for worldly power, citing historical and mythical figures who claimed divinity.</summary>
<keywords>fallen angels, demonic hierarchy, pride, Psaphon the Libyan, Spirits of Jove, Morning Spirits, temptation of Christ</keywords>

--- Page 153 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Castor and Pollux regarding the various hierarchies and roles of fallen spirits, specifically how they manipulate human behavior and the natural world.</meta>

<page-num>115</page-num>
<header>Of the Nature of Spirits.</header>

...perfectly, he sent them out for this end and purpose: that by flying everywhere, they might repeat those words. The people, who were ignorant of his deceitful invention, were led to believe that those words were spoken by divine instinct, and they consequently adored and worshiped him as a God. The other would force his subjects to do the same, requiring that they prostrate themselves and lift up their hands to worship him as the Almighty.

<margin>Psalm 55.</margin>
**Castor.** But are those who stir up wars—who are called "bloody men" in Scripture—not also the captives of the Devil?

<margin>The Spirits of the North.</margin>
**Pollux.** The "Martialists" <term>Martialists: those associated with Mars, the god of war; here meaning war-mongering spirits</term> of the Northern part of the world are called executioners of vengeance, authors of devastation, and sowers of evil. They work and carry out judgment alongside *Asmodeus* <note>original: "Asmodeus"; a king of demons often associated with lust or destruction</note>, under their King *Abaddon* or *Apollyon* <note>original: "Abaddon" (Hebrew) or "Apollyon" (Greek); both names meaning "The Destroyer"</note>. Saint John mentions in his Revelation that these kings were banished and expelled. For these 

<margin>Meridional Spirit <note>original: "Meridional"; meaning Southern</note>.</margin>
spirits have been assigned the tasks of robbery, hatred, envy, theft, wrath, anger, and the provocation of sin, war, and fury. Sometimes they use the *Meridional* <term>Meridional: Southern</term> spirits as their 

<margin>Ecclesiasticus 39:28.</margin>
messengers. There is also *Arioch*, the spirit of vengeance, whose work is to cause discord among brothers, to break marriages, and to dissolve married love so completely that it is impossible to renew. Mention is made of these in the 39th chapter of *Ecclesiasticus* <note>original: "Ecclesiasticus"; also known as the Wisdom of Sirach, a book of the Biblical Apocrypha</note>. Furthermore, Isaiah the heavenly prophet speaks of other spirits sent from God to the Egyptians to make them go astray. 

<margin>Spirits of darkness.</margin>
These were spirits of darkness—that is, spirits of lies—and this kind of spirit is called *Bolichim*.

**Castor.** Is sexual immorality <term>venery: the pursuit of sexual pleasure</term> and excessive gluttony also to be blamed on the Devils?

<margin>Occidental Spirits <note>original: "Occidental"; meaning Western</note>.</margin>
**Pollux.** Yes, primarily. For *Iamblichus* <note>original: "Iamblichus"; a Neoplatonist philosopher who wrote on theurgy and spirits</note> asserts that the spirits of the water, the spirits of the Western part of the world, and some Southern spirits are destined for this purpose. These include spirits such as *Nesrach* and *Kellen*, who devise and orchestrate unlawful loves that result in shame and dishonesty, wild parties, overeating, overindulgence in drink, lewd dances, gluttony, and vomiting. They wander about lakes, fishponds, and rivers; they are the worst, foulest, and most deceitful kind of spirits. And through *Alcinach*, a Western spirit, the Devil causes shipwrecks, storms,
