<margin>The spirits of the air infect the air.</margin>
...plagues, earthquakes, hail, rain, and frequently subverts and overturns ships. If he chooses to appear visibly, he appears and is seen in the shape of a woman. The *Hebrew Astronomers* mentioned before say that the spirits of the air cause thunder, lightning, and thunderbolts so that they might corrupt and infect the air, producing pestilence and destruction. *Saint John* mentions these kinds of spirits in the ninth chapter of the *Book of Revelation*, having *Meceris* as their guardian <term>tutelar: a guardian or protecting spirit</term>. Meceris is a spirit that causes heat during the noontime. *Saint Paul* calls him <margin>Ephesians 2</margin> "the prince of the power of the air, and the spirit that now works in the children of disobedience."

*Castor.* Are there so many monsters in *Phlegethon* <note>original: "Phlegeton"; in Greek mythology, one of the five rivers of the underworld, associated with fire</note>, *Pollux*?

<margin>Spirits of fire.</margin>
*Pollux.* And many more; for the same *Hebrew authorities* <note>original: "Assertors"</note> declare and maintain that there are spirits of the fiery element. They rage about like fierce panthers and are active within the regions below the moon <term>lunary regions: the area between the Earth and the Moon, traditionally thought to be the realm of change and corruption</term>, so that whatever task is assigned to them, they execute it immediately. <margin>Spirits of the earth.</margin> There are also spirits of the earth who inhabit groves, woods, and wildernesses; they are the plague and misfortune of hunters. Sometimes they frequent open fields, trying to lure travelers and passengers off their right path, or to deceive them with false and wicked illusions. Otherwise, they seek to afflict men with a harmful melancholy, making them furious or mad so that they may hurt themselves, and sometimes almost kill themselves. The leaders of these are *Sanyaab* and *Achimael*, who are Eastern spirits. They are a kind less prone to wickedness because of their steady dispositions.

<margin>Subterranean Spirits</margin>
There are also subterranean spirits who inhabit dens and caverns of the earth and the remote hollows of mountains so that they might occupy deep pits and the bowels of the earth. These spirits dig up metals and guard treasures, which they often move from one place to another so that no one can make use of them. They stir up winds with flashing flames of fire. They strike the foundations of buildings and perform frightening dances in the night, from which they suddenly vanish with a noise and the sound of bells, causing fear in those who see them. Sometimes they disguise themselves, pretending to be the souls of the dead; however, they are unsuccessful in carrying out their deceits against women. Among this company...

To these we will join the monk who invented the use of gunpowder <margin>Gunpowder <note>original Latin: *Pulvis pyrium*</note></margin> for his engines of war. *Jacob* makes mention of these in *Genesis* when he blesses his sons; he says, *Simeon and Levi are bloody <margin>*Genesis* 49.</margin> vessels of iniquity; Oh my soul, do not enter into their counsels.* The Psalmist calls these spirits "vessels of death"; *Isaiah* calls them "vessels of fury"; *Jeremiah*, "vessels of wrath"; and *Ezekiel* <margin>*Isaiah* 7.</margin> calls them "vessels of death and destruction." The necromancers call the aforementioned *Belial* by the name *Chodar*, an oriental spirit who also has under him the spirits of jugglers, who imitate and try to perform miracles so that they may seduce false magicians and wicked people. It is clearly evident that the serpent which deceived *Eve* was such a seducer, and *Satan* is his prince, of whom it is said in the *Book of Revelation* that he would deceive the whole world. And such a one was he who at Tübingen <note>original: "Tubinga," a prominent university city in Germany</note>, in the sight of many people, devoured an entire chariot and several horses.

<page-num>119</page-num>
### Of the Nature of Spirits

*...a spacious house in* Athens*, which nobody would inhabit because of the nightly disturbances of* spirits*. These were so terrifying to the residents that sometimes during the daytime, even while they were awake, the spirits would cast them into a dreamlike state—always ensuring that the shapes and forms they saw remained vivid in their memory. Finally, a certain philosopher named* Athenodorus <note>original: "Anthenodorus"</note> *happened to purchase that house and prepared and furnished it to live in himself. Because everyone had a bad suspicion of that house, he immediately commanded his servants to provide him with a bed and tables so that, after he had finished his studies, he might go to bed. He therefore (says* Pliny*) when he went in (in the evening) and applied himself to his studies, suddenly heard the locks rattling open and the sound of chains being moved. Nevertheless, he did not look up or move from his book, but stopped his ears with his fingers so that the frantic commotion would not cause him unnecessary fear. But as the noise still approached closer to him, he finally looked up and saw a figure* <note>original: "effigies"</note> *like a finger beckoning and calling to him. He paid it little attention until it had touched him three times and the noise drew near to the table. Then he looked up, took a light, and beheld the spirit: it looked like an old man, wasted away with withered thinness and deformity; his beard hung down long, with horrible and messy hair, and his legs and feet were weighed down with chains and fetters. The spirit went toward a gate which was bolted, and there it left the philosopher and vanished away.*

**Castor.** *What fearful things you relate,* Pollux*! But what was the outcome of this sad spectacle?*

**Pollux.** *The next day, he reported the whole matter to the* magistrates *in order, just as he had seen it, advising them that they should dig diligently around the threshold of the door. For there, it was probable they might find something which might cause the house to become quiet and habitable.*

**Castor.** *What did they find?*

**Pollux.** *Having dug up the earth,* Pliny *says,* they *found a corpse, bound and entangled in chains and fetters; its flesh had been consumed by time. Without delay, they had it buried according to* Christian <note>The original story by Pliny the Younger (1st century AD) refers to a proper public burial; the author of this text has added "Christian" ceremonies as a later religious interpretation.</note> *ceremonies.*

*Pol.* Yes, quite so.

<margin>
Those who destroy churchyards are possessed by madness. The spirits Zazalus and Eurynomus.
</margin>
*Castor.* What madness, therefore, possesses those who profane and destroy churchyards, where the sacred vessels <note>original: "Organs"</note> of the holy and blessed Spirit rest? They essentially give the bones of the dead as food to the spirit *Zazalus*, who is mentioned in the third book of Kings; <note>The "Third Book of Kings" refers to what modern Bibles call 1 Kings.</note> and we read in *Pausanias*, among the histories of *Delphi* <note>original: "Delphos"</note>, that he was called *Eurynomus*. <note>In Greek mythology, Eurynomus was a chthonic spirit or demon of the underworld who stripped the flesh off the bones of the dead.</note>

They allowed the youth to dance their mocking plays there to the frantic sound of the drum or taber, singing *Io Paean*; <note>A Greek choral song or chant of triumph or thanksgiving.</note> or they allowed poor, lower-class women to sell cheap trinkets or lupines <note>A type of legume, often sold as a common street food.</note> there. Yet God intended those places to be purified with holy prayers for the salvation of souls, or for the breaking of bread for the hungry.

*Castor.* But it is an impious and heathen thing to have interfered with the "anointed of God."

<margin>
The ceremony of burial was held in great esteem among the heathens.
</margin>
*Pol.* It is worse than heathen; for even the heathens highly esteemed the rites and ceremonies of burial. *Elpenor* is a witness to this in *Homer*, where he gives up his life; in *Homer*, he speaks to *Ulysses*: *I entreat you, O Ulysses, to be mindful of me, and not depart from here and leave me unburied, lest, by not being rightly buried, I become a cause of the Gods' wrath.* And *Archytas* the philosopher, in *Horace* <note>original: "Flaccus," referring to Quintus Horatius Flaccus.</note>, speaks thus to the mariner:

<margin>
Horace, Book 1 of his Odes.
</margin>
> The South wind, the rapid companion of setting Orion, has also overwhelmed me in the Illyrian waves. But you, sailor, do not be so stingy as to refuse to give a small portion of shifting sand to my unburied bones and head. If you do this, then whatever the East wind may threaten against the Hesperian waves, let the woods of Venusia be lashed while you remain safe, and may much profit flow to you from where it can, from fair Jupiter. <note>original: "Me quoque devexi Rapidus comes Orionis / Illyricis Notus obruit undis. / At tu Nauta vaga ne parce malignus harena, / Ossibus & capiti inhumato. / Particulam dare; sic quocunque minabitur Eurus / Fluctibus Hesperiis, Venusinæ / Plectantur siluæ, te sospite multaque merces / Unde potest tibi defluat æquo."</note>

Because this body was only covered with a light layer of turf, it greatly disquieted and troubled the owners of the garden with violent disturbances in the night, until it was taken up again by his sisters (who had returned from banishment) and was rightly and properly buried by them.

<margin>Not all spirits can take on multiple forms.</margin>

That sometimes they sleep or rest, and do change their places, and show themselves visible to the senses of men. **Socrates** asserts that a spirit did speak with him, which he also sometimes saw and felt; but their bodies cannot be discerned to have different sexes. But **Marcus Cherronesus** <note>original: "Marcus Cherronesus," likely referring to Michael Psellus, a Byzantine scholar known for his work on demons</note>, an excellent researcher into the nature of spirits, writes that they have simple bodies and that a difference of sex belongs only to complex <note>original: "compound"</note> bodies. Yet, he says their bodies are easily moved and flexible, and naturally suited to take on any shape. For, he says, *just as the clouds show the appearance and likeness sometimes of men, and sometimes of everything you can imagine; so likewise do the bodies of spirits take on various shapes as they please. By this means, they transform themselves into the forms sometimes of men, and sometimes of women.* 

Nevertheless, this is not possible for all of them, but only for the fiery and airy spirits. For he teaches that the spirits of the water have slower and less active bodies which, because of the slowness and softness of that element, most especially resemble birds and women. The **Naiads** and **Nereids** <note>types of water nymphs in Greek mythology</note>, celebrated by the poets, are of this kind. **Trithemius** <note>original: "Trimetius," referring to Johannes Trithemius, a German abbot and occultist</note> testifies that the demons <note>original: "Devils"</note> desire to assume the shapes of men rather than any other form; however, they often cannot find the substance of the air convenient and fitting for that purpose. He says that they frame such kinds of appearances for themselves as the available moisture or vapor will allow. Thus, they are seen sometimes in the form and shape of a lion, a wolf, a pig, a donkey, a centaur, or a horned man having feet like a goat. It is reported that such things were seen in the mountains of **Thuringia** <note>original: "Thrungia," a region in central Germany</note>, where a terrible roaring was heard.

**Castor**: **Porphyry** <note>a Neoplatonist philosopher</note>, as cited in **Eusebius's** fourth book of the *Gospel Preparation* <note>original Latin: "Evangelical Preparations" or *Praeparatio Evangelica*</note>, teaches that some of these are good spirits and some are bad; but I have counted them to be all evil, **Pollux**.

**Pollux**: Then it seems that you are not misled by the assertions of either **Porphyry**, **Apuleius**, **Proclus**, or other **Platonists** <note>original: "Platonicks"</note> who are mentioned in **Saint Augustine's** book, *The City of God* <note>original Latin: *De Civitate Dei*</note>, in Chapters 1, 2, and 3, who also...

<summary>This page discusses the physical nature of spirits, explaining that while they lack gender, they can manipulate their "simple bodies" to appear in various human or animal forms, with fiery and airy spirits being more adaptable than water spirits.</summary>

*Castor*. It would have been safer for everyone to have been without evil spirits; what, then, was the will of the heavenly Father regarding them?

*Pollux*. That with the help of the good spirits, we might courageously wage constant war against the evil spirits. *Castor*. If we engage <note>original: "condescend unto"</note> in this spiritual warfare, it seems appropriate to ask whether the devils have been granted the power to do harm by God, or whether they can cause as much damage as they wish on their own?

<margin>The devils are the princes of the earth.</margin>
*Pollux*. If the latter were true, who could imagine the extent of the harm they would do? But it is very clear that their authority from on high is of such great significance <note>original: "existency"</note> that *John* the Evangelist does not hesitate to call the devils the "princes of the earth."

*Castor*. In what way, then, do they cause harm?

*Pollux*. Although they are most mighty and powerful spirits, they can do no harm unless it is by permission—or, as *John of Damascus* <note>original: "Damascenus"</note> says, *by dispensation*. The Devil could not have deceived the prophets of *Ahab* if he had not received power from God; nor could he have caused any harm to *Job*, either to his body or his possessions, except by the power God had given him. In the 7th chapter of *Exodus*, the magicians created frogs and serpents through the power of the Devil by permission; but they could not produce lice because the greater power of God prevented them. Likewise, in the Gospel, the devils could not harm the swine until *Christ* had given them permission.

*Castor*. Therefore, the Devil is not to be feared so much as the Lord our God, so that He might either not allow the Devil to rage against us, or if at any time, by His own determined counsel—

# A Discourse

<margin>Why the devil sometimes tells the truth.</margin>
...referred to, *Chrysostom* <note>original: "Chrysostome"; John Chrysostom (c. 347–407 AD), an influential Early Church Father known for his preaching</note> most clearly teaches in these words:
> It is granted, he says, that sometimes the devil does speak the truth, so that he might recommend his own lying with rare truth <note>original: "verity"</note>: whereas, if he should never tell the truth, he could deceive no man, nor would his lying be enough for him to tempt with. Thus far *Chrysostom*.

<margin>The oracles of the devils are uncertain.</margin>
Nevertheless, if he understands that he does not have the grace granted to him by his own nature to foretell the truth, he foretells things anyway, but so obscurely, says *St. Augustine*, that he always places the blame for the things he foretold upon the interpreter. *Porphyry* <note>original: "Porphyrius"; a Neoplatonic philosopher (c. 234–305 AD) who was often cited by Christian authors for his knowledge of pagan rituals</note>, in his book of Oracles, although he is the greatest defender of demons and the most expert teacher of diabolical arts, nevertheless says with the aforementioned Doctors that the foreknowledge of things to come is not only complicated <note>original: "intricate"</note> for men, but also uncertain for the gods, and full of many obscurities.

*Pollux:* You have said that the predictions of the devils are done in this manner so that they may gain authority for themselves among gullible <note>original: "credulous"</note> people and be worshipped instead of Gods. For what purpose do the evil spirits work miracles?

*Castor:* What is a miracle, *Pollux*?

<margin>What a miracle is. The devils work miracles.</margin>
*Pollux:* A new and unusual event <note>original: "unwonted accident"</note>, which happens contrary to its natural course and custom, and leads men to wonder at it.

*Castor:* But do they work miracles?

*Pollux:* They do. For why do you believe that *Asclepius* <note>original: "Æsculapius"; the Greek god of medicine. Legend says he was brought to Rome in the form of a snake to end a plague</note> was honored in his consecration as a god, if not only by means of a miracle, when he conveyed a serpent from *Epidaurus* to *Rome*? What gave such great authority to *Juno*, but only the working of a miracle? This was when her wooden image was asked by *Furius Camillus* <note>Marcus Furius Camillus (c. 446–365 BC), a Roman general who famously captured the city of Veii</note> whether it wanted to be carried to Rome, and it answered with a human voice, *It would*. Also, from that event, *Fortune* was made a goddess because her statue <note>original: "Statua"</note>, on the way to *Latium*, was heard by many people—not just once, but often—speaking with a human voice. In the 8th Chapter of the *Acts* of the Apostles, we read of miracles done by *Simon* the son of *Rachel* <note>Likely referring to Simon Magus; the text uniquely identifies him as the "son of Rachel"</note>; and in *Exodus* 8, we read of the magicians—

# Of the Nature of Spirits.

<margin>The enchantments of the demons subvert nature.</margin>
The Magicians of Pharaoh, in the sight of many people, brought forth frogs and serpents and turned the waters into blood. Apuleius <note>original: "Apuleius"; a Roman philosopher and prose writer (c. 124–170 AD) whose works often explored magic and the supernatural</note> testifies that the power of men is so great in enchantments that the devils do not only work miracles by means of men, but they are also able to subvert Nature. With a demonic incantation, they can make violent streams stop their course, turn the winds, make the sun stand still, break the course of the moon, place obstacles upon the stars, prolong the day, and shorten the night, as Lucan <note>original: "Lucanus"; Marcus Annaeus Lucanus (39–65 AD), a Roman poet famous for his epic *Pharsalia*</note> excellently shows:

> *The natural cycles of things ceased, and the day, having been delayed, lingered in a long night; the heavens did not obey their laws, and the world was scorched headlong once the song was heard.*
> <note>original: "Cessavere vices rerum, dilatáq; longa, Hasit nocte dies, legi non paruit æther Torruit & præceps audito Carmine mundus."</note>

And Tibullus <note>original: "Tibullus"; Albius Tibullus (c. 55–19 BC), a Latin poet whose elegies occasionally mention magic and witchcraft</note> writes of a certain demonic charm:

> *I have seen her drawing stars down from the sky; she turns the course of a swift river with her song. By her chanting, she splits the ground, draws the spirits of the dead from their tombs, and snatches bones from the still-warm funeral pyre. When she chooses, she clears the clouds from the gloomy sky; when she chooses, she summons snow in the summer months.*
> <note>original: "Hanc ego de cælo ducentem sydera vidi, Fluminis ac rapidi Carmine vertitater, Hæc cantu funditq; solum manesq; sepulchris Elicit, & tepido devorat ossa rogo. Cum libet hæc tristi depellit lumina cælo, Cum libet æstivo convocat orbe nives."</note>

**Castor.** I no longer wonder that Moses called God *Wonderful*, considering that He overlooks this sink of wickedness and these most wicked seducers, granting them the power to act such things so freely.

For he says that *virtue is not virtue unless it has something similar to it <note>an opposing force</note>, in ruling over which it may show and exercise its power. For he says: just as victory cannot exist without an opponent, so neither can virtue subsist without an enemy. No sooner had the Almighty endowed man with virtue, than He immediately gave him an enemy, lest that virtue should lose its nature by becoming dulled through idleness.* He says that a man cannot...

->A Discourse,<-

...otherwise reach the highest step unless he always has an active hand; he must establish and build up his salvation through continual warfare and struggle. For God does not intend for mortal men to reach immortal blessedness through an easy journey. Instead, he must wrestle and strive with "sails and oars" <note>original: "with sayls and oars"; a metaphor meaning to use all of one's strength and resources, derived from the Latin idiom "velis remisque"</note> against the author and inventor of all evils and errors, who causes and performs detestable things and miracles.

<margin>Sometimes it happens that the devil cannot be resisted.</margin>
*Castor:* But sometimes it happens that because of the subtle snares and stratagems of the devil—which he so craftily prepares against us, and especially against simple people whom he entangles with vain religions—we cannot resist him. Or, if we think ourselves very able to withstand him, we may nonetheless be greatly deceived by him. We read that he often did this to the good, but rather foolish, pastor mentioned by Trithemius <note>original: "Tritemius"; Johannes Trithemius (1462–1516), a German Benedictine abbot and polymath often cited in works on magic and spirits</note>.

*Pollux:* But what happened to this good pastor, whom you call simple?

<margin>A remarkable story of a swineherd.</margin>
*Castor:* Trithemius <note>original: "Tritensius"</note> says that because the pastor was not strong in faith, he valued the name of Saint Blaise <note>original: "Saint Blaze"; a 4th-century bishop and martyr often invoked in folk religion for the protection of livestock</note> more highly and attributed more power and protection to it than to the name of God, the supreme good.

*Pollux:* In what way?

*Castor:* He had a small scroll <note>original: "Schedule"; a piece of paper or parchment containing writing</note> inscribed with the name of Saint Blaise inside his walking staff or pastoral crook. By the power and virtue of this staff, he believed his swine were safely defended from ravenous wolves. He attributed such great divinity to that scroll that he would leave his herd of swine to feed in the fields alone. However, a time came when the pastor was absent from his flock, and a man passing by saw the devil guarding them. He asked the devil—the worst persecutor of human salvation—what he was guarding there. He answered, "*I am guarding these swine.*" The other replied, "*By whose command?*" The devil said, "*By the foolish confidence of the pastor. **Pollux.** This is an amusing story, but I do not wonder that the devil should deceive such a simple pastor so much, since he often prevails over the wise if they expose themselves <note>original: "do at themselves"; likely a transcription of "do apt themselves," meaning to make oneself fit or ready for something</note> to his opportunities—something the Church warns against.

**Pollux.** But are all the things that men call miracles actually brought about by the devil?

<margin>Some miracles are done naturally.</margin>
**Castor.** No. We must credit Nature for what belongs to her, for she is said to be the greatest worker of miracles. Consider what we have experienced with the stone *asbestos*, which *Solinus* <note>Gaius Julius Solinus, a 3rd-century Roman writer who compiled a collection of world wonders</note> testifies cannot be quenched once it is set on fire. And consider the root *Baara*, described by *Josephus* <note>Flavius Josephus (c. 37–100 AD), a Romano-Jewish historian</note> in the history of *Jerusalem*. He testifies that it is the color of a flame of fire, glowing and shining in the night. However, it is so difficult to capture that it always retreats from the hand of anyone who tries to take it, deceiving his eyes until it is sprinkled with the urine of a menstruous woman. Even when it is held in place this way, it cannot be gathered or pulled up without danger. For immediate death follows the one who plucks it up, unless he is protected by a talisman <note>original: "Preservative"; here meaning a protective charm or amulet</note> around his neck made from the same root. For this reason, those who want the root dig around it; then, having tied the root with a cord, they fasten the other end to a dog and quickly walk away. When the dog tries to follow its master, it pulls up the root; as if the dog were taking its master's place in death, it dies immediately. Afterward, the root can be handled without danger to anyone. He advises us to be clothed with the silken garment of honesty, the purple robe of modesty and humility <note>original: "shamefac'dness"</note>, and the cloak of patience;

Saint <note>The "D." likely stands for *Divus* (Saint) or *Doctor*, referring to Maximus the Confessor</note> Maximus, in his book *On Charity* <note>original: "de charitate"</note>, commands us to bind and kill the demons. For he considers this the most perfect seal against the power of demons.

<margin>We are to fight against the devil with two sorts of armor.</margin>
Some advise us, as we go out to war against the devil, to use two kinds of weapons. One is pure prayer, which raises our affections toward heaven; the other is true and perfect knowledge, which fills our understanding with healthy teachings and suggests what we should pray for—so that we may pray fervently, as Saint James says, and not with doubt. In the prophecy of Isaiah and the epistles of Saint Paul, we may find the same things (Isaiah 59, Ephesians 6, and 1 Thessalonians 5), which serve as a remedy to drive away vain ghosts <note>original: "Ghosts"; here referring to spectral apparitions or terrifying visions</note>.

These doubters are ignorant of the fact that the Angel (who also has a body without hands and feet) carried Habakkuk <note>This refers to a story from the deuterocanonical book *Bel and the Dragon*, where an angel carries the prophet by his hair.</note>, along with his entire dinner, by the hair of his head into Babylon, and afterwards brought him back again and set him in his own place. I will not even speak of a certain incorporeal spirit that so disturbed the house of my grandfather that for nearly thirty years he caused it to be uninhabitable, except when a lamp was burning inside—and even then, it was not sufficiently quiet. For when they went out of the house, the spirit harassed them with stones thrown from above in the streets, even knocking the pine-wood torches <note>original: "hearts of Pine-trees"</note> out of their hands. 

Concerning the ghost that haunted the house of Athenodorus the Philosopher <note>A famous ghost story recorded by Pliny the Younger involving a shackled ghost in Athens.</note>, and the restless spirit of Gaius Caligula <note>The Roman Emperor Caligula, whose spirit was said to haunt the Lamian Gardens after his assassination.</note>, more could be said, but you have already heard the accounts of them in the previous discourse. # Of the Nature of Spirits.

*Cast.* So also some have declared that the soul of *Trajan Caesar* <note>Trajan (reigned 98–117 AD), a Roman Emperor often depicted in medieval legends as being released from hell through the prayers of Pope Gregory.</note> wandered about; but the soul of *St. George* was freed from such suffering <note>original: "suffrage," likely referring to the state of being subject to wandering or the prayers of the living.</note>.

*Poll.* You have just now spoken, and truly so, that the sea of various opinions concerning these spirits is vast; for indeed it is. But I desire you to tell me which port you are heading for <note>i.e., "what is your final conclusion?"</note>, for I am not yet satisfied of the certainty of this by our conversation.

*Cast.* What you desire, I believe, is this: I hold that these tumultuous spirits are mere images of *Satan*. They are not to be feared, nor is any credit to be given to their answers. They are in no way the souls of the dead, who either live with *Christ* if they have done well, or else are bound in chains with *Satan* if they have done evil.

*Poll.* It remains for us to sift this out, *Castor*. For it sometimes happens now that my father appears to me in my sleep; perhaps that may also seem to you to be a spirit.

*Cast.* It may seem so, but I will not contradict you in anything beyond reason. I will add nothing of my own; but at least I will dismantle your opinion with the assertions of *St. Augustine*.

*Poll.* What assertions are those?

*Cast.* In his second book, which he titles *On the Care of the Dead* <note>original: "De mortuorum cura"</note>, he offers this explanation: *“Human weakness is so convinced that when one sees a dead person in a dream, he believes he sees the actual soul of that dead person. But when he dreams of someone who is alive, he is then certain that it was neither the soul nor the body, but merely the likeness of the man that appeared to him. As if they could be unaware that the souls of dead men do not appear to them in dreams, but only the likenesses of the deceased.”* And he proves both of these points with two examples that occurred in *Milan* <note>original: "Mediolanus"</note>. The first he shows to have been the image of a certain father who was dead, who appeared to his son, warning him that he should not pay a debt a second time to an

...unjust creditor, whom the father had paid before. He says the case was this: the father had paid a debt to a certain creditor. The second example is one by which the same St. *Augustine* shows that the living can appear to the living in their sleep. He says that *Eulogius* <note>OCR: "Eurologius"</note> the *Rhetorician* <note>A teacher of rhetoric, the art of persuasive speaking and writing.</note>, while teaching the rhetoric of *Cicero* at *Carthage*, found a difficult and obscure passage that had not been explained to him. To further strengthen this judgment, he adds that if the souls of the dead had any influence or concern in the affairs of the living, he knew for certain that his own pious mother would not have abandoned him even for one night. While she was living, she followed him by both sea and land, and she never let him endure any anguish of heart without comforting his sorrows <note>Augustine’s mother, Saint Monica, was famous for her persistent devotion to her son.</note>.

*Castor.* We have already shown, from the writings of *St. Augustine*, that they perform many things in reality and many things only in appearance. For that great leader <note>original: "Prelate"</note> of the Christian Church writes in the 11th chapter of his book *On the Trinity* <note>original: "de Trinitate"</note> that it is a very easy thing for wicked spirits—because of the airy substance of their bodies—to perform many things that seem wonderful to souls who are weighed down <note>original: "oppressed"</note> by earthly bodies. He also says that earthly bodies can be so trained through skill and practice that they can perform such wonderful things in public theaters that those who have never seen them would not believe they were possible—unless they were done with the help of the devil and his servants, who could make their bodies into such an "airy element" that the physical flesh marvels at it. Or else—which is more significant—he also says that they devise hidden <note>original: "occult"</note> inspirations, forms, and imaginary images to delude the human senses, by which people may be deceived whether waking or sleeping. That is all from *Augustine*. But if you wish, *Pollux*, I will also provide another witness beyond reproach.

*Pollux.* I would like you to tell me who that is.

# A Discourse

<margin>Devils cannot truly raise the dead.</margin>
...to show miracles to curious men, so that they might, as it were, swallow them up with error instead of miracles; and they are altogether stubborn <note>original: "pertinacious"</note> and obstinate. However, they cannot truly and really raise the dead; instead, they deceive the senses of men in various ways by showing them false resemblances of the deceased. For it is clearly manifest that devils can do all things only as a false imitation <note>original: "similitude"</note> of true, holy miracles.

**Pollux.** Some say that devils are obedient to wicked men because of their shared malice. How does that seem to you?

**Castor.** It seems to me that they are obedient to evil men, but not to all men.

**Pollux.** But to whom?

**Castor.** Certainly to those with whom they have contracted and made compacts and covenants—such as those women called Pythonists <term>Pythonist: A term for a person possessed by a spirit or a medium, often used to describe witches or those who claimed to speak with spirits.</term>, who are considered to have vowed themselves by promise unto him.

**Pollux.** But although they are compelled to be so serviceable to them, is this service true or fake?

**Castor.** It is certainly fake. For they serve men of their own accord and through their own works so that they may deceive them and allure them to themselves. Although we do not deny that their service is sometimes true, this is only toward those men whose faith in the Lord Jesus Christ—by the merit of His holiness—has caused them to be acceptable and friends to Him. 

And *Lactantius* <note>Lactantius (c. 250 – c. 325) was an early Christian author and advisor to Constantine the Great.</note> also testifies to this in the 16th chapter of his second book, *On the Origin of Error* <note>original: "De origine Erroris"</note>: that the devils fear the righteous—that is, those who worship God—in whose Name they are commanded <note>original: "conjured"</note> to depart out of bodies, and by whose words they are beaten as if with scourges. They do not only confess that they are devils, but they even declare their names; neither can they lie to the righteous. 

The same *Lactantius*, in the 27th chapter of his fourth book, *On True Wisdom* <note>original: "De vera Sapientia"</note>, says that it is necessary that those who are of the true Christian religion should know the behavior and order of the devils, understand their craftiness <note>original: "subtilty"</note>, restrain their force, and conquer...

<meta>The text cuts off mid-sentence; the catchword "quer" indicates the next page begins with the completion of the word "conquer."</meta>

<summary>This page explores why devils appear to serve humans, clarifying that they cannot actually raise the dead and usually only serve wicked people to deceive them. It also cites Lactantius to argue that true Christians have the power to command and expose demons through the name of Jesus Christ.</summary>

**Castor.** Nor have I considered myself idle in answering you; let the use of this dialogue yield us each an equal reward <note>original: "recompence"</note>. Farewell, then.

**Pollux.** And you also.

<meta>This page introduces a treatise on "Astronomical Geomancy," attributed to Gerard of Cremona. It establishes a simplified method for astrological divination that does not require complex tables or astronomical calculations.</meta>

<image-desc>A wide ornamental headpiece featuring a central grotesque mask surrounded by elaborate scrolling foliage and acanthus leaves, with additional smaller faces integrated into the scrollwork at the sides.</image-desc>

# GERARD OF CREMONA <note>original: Gerard Cremonensis</note>
## ON
### ASTRONOMICAL GEOMANCY.

<image-desc>An ornate woodcut initial 'B' decorated with floral and leaf motifs.</image-desc>
Because Astronomy is such a transcendent and subtle art in itself, and because a person must consider so many factors before they can attain a true judgment by it, the eye of the understanding cannot pierce even halfway into its depths. Few scholars <note>original: "Doctors"</note> of our recent times have been found so experienced in it that they know sufficiently how to judge by it. Therefore, I have composed this work, which I wish to be named *Astronomical Geomancy*. In this work, I will sufficiently teach how to judge with less labor and study. For in this present science, it is not necessary to look up <note>original: "be hold"</note> the Ascendant <term>The sign of the Zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the inquiry.</term> or the hour in a table, as is required in Astrology.

It is necessary, therefore, to make four lines of unequal length by setting down points randomly <note>original: "casually"</note>. You must then join those points together in pairs. From the points that are not joined together—which remain at the ends of the lines (as is done in Geomancy <term>A method of divination that interprets markings on the ground or patterns formed by tossed handfuls of soil, rocks, or sand.</term>)—you extract one figure. Then, take the sign of the Zodiac that corresponds to that figure and use it as the Ascendant for the sake of the calculation. For example, if *Acquisitio* <note>Latin: "Gain" or "Acquisition," one of the 16 geomantic figures.</note> arises from the ends of those... <note>The text ends mid-sentence; the catchword is "four."</note>

<summary>Gerard of Cremona introduces Astronomical Geomancy as a more accessible alternative to complex traditional astrology, explaining how to generate a starting "Ascendant" using random points and geomantic figures.</summary>
<keywords>Gerard of Cremona, Astronomical Geomancy, divination, astrology vs. geomancy, Acquisitio, occult science, Ascendant</keywords>

# GERARD OF CREMONA <note>original: Gerard Cremonensis</note>

Based on the four lines, let **Aries** be placed in the Ascendant; if **Letitia** <note>original: *Letitia* (Joy)</note> or the **Lesser Fortune** <note>original: "leſſer Fortune" (*Fortuna Minor*)</note> is produced, put **Taurus** in the Ascendant; if **Puer** <note>original: *Puer* (The Boy)</note> or **Rubens** <note>original: *Rubens* (The Red, or *Rubeus*)</note>, place **Gemini**; if **Albus** <note>original: *Albus* (White)</note>, **Cancer**; if **Via** <note>original: *Via* (The Way)</note>, **Leo**; if **Conjunctio** <note>original: *Conjunctio* (Conjunction)</note> or the **Dragon’s Head** <note>The North Node of the Moon.</note>, **Virgo**; if **Puella** <note>original: *Puella* (The Girl)</note>, **Libra**; if **Amissio** <note>original: *Amiſſio* (Loss)</note> or **Tristitia** <note>original: *Triſtitia* (Sorrow)</note>, **Scorpio**; if the **Dragon’s Tail** <note>The South Node of the Moon.</note>, **Sagittarius**; if **Populus** <note>original: *Populus* (People)</note>, **Capricorn**; if **Fortuna Major** <note>original: *Fortuna major* (Greater Fortune)</note>, **Aquarius**; and if **Carcer** <note>original: *Carcer* (Prison)</note>, then put **Pisces** for the Ascendant. 

Afterward, in the second house, let that sign be placed which immediately follows the first. In the third house, place the third sign, and so continue placing the rest in order until you reach the end of the signs. Create a square figure divided into twelve equal parts and place the signs in order within it, as is done in Astrology and as you can find in this diagram. In this method, we are not to concern ourselves with the **Witnesses** or the **Judge** <term>In traditional geomancy, the "Witnesses" and "Judge" are specific figures generated at the end of a reading to provide a final answer. Here, they are ignored in favor of a purely astrological interpretation.</term>, or any other element belonging to standard geomancy. We use only the sixteen figures <note>There are sixteen possible four-line patterns in geomancy.</note> so that through them we may identify the twelve signs to which they correspond. Observe the manner of the figure as it is placed here.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for "Astronomical Geomancy," specifically detailing how to place the planets within the twelve astrological houses based on a mathematical calculation of points, followed by interpretations for questions regarding the first house (the house of life and the self).</meta>

# On Astronomical Geomancy

Afterward, it is necessary to make four lines of points for every planet in sequence, by marking points down randomly as you did for the **Ascendant** <note>The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the question.</note>. Divide the total of those points by twelve. Take the remainder—or twelve itself if there is no remainder—and place the planet for which the calculation was made into the house corresponding to that number. For example, if the remainder is twelve, the planet is placed in the twelfth house; if ten, in the tenth house; if one, in the first house; if two, in the second house; and so on for the rest.

You should always begin with the **Sun**, followed by the **Moon**, then **Venus**, **Mercury**, **Saturn**, **Jupiter**, **Mars**, the **Dragon's Head** <note>original: *Caput Draconis*; the North Node of the Moon</note>, and the **Dragon's Tail** <note>original: *Cauda Draconis*; the South Node of the Moon</note>. You must always take care not to ask a question during rainy, cloudy, or very windy weather, nor when you are angry or your mind is occupied with many affairs. Do not perform this for those who only wish to test or mock you, and do not repeat the same question again using the same figure or form, for that leads to error.

### *Questions of the First House*

If you wish to know whether a person's life will be long or short, look to the Lord of the **Ascendant**. If he is in strong **Angles** <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses; these are considered the most powerful positions in a chart.</note>, it signifies a long life. If he is in **succedent houses** <note>The 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses; these are of moderate strength.</note>, it signifies a middle age. If he is in **cadent houses** <note>The 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses; these are considered the weakest positions.</note>, it signifies a short life.

Furthermore, if he is in strong Angles, he signifies the "greater years"; if in succedent houses, the "mean years"; and if in cadent houses, the "lesser years."

*   The **lesser years** of **Saturn** are thirty; the **mean years** are forty-four; and the **greater** are fifty-eight.
*   The **lesser years** of **Jupiter** are twelve; the **mean years** are forty; and the **greater** are forty-seven.
*   The **lesser years** of **Mars** are fifteen; his **mean years** are forty; and the **greater years** are forty-seven.
*   The **lesser years** of the **Sun** are nineteen; his **mean years** are forty-five; and his **greater years** are eighty-two.
*   The **lesser years** of **Venus** are eight; her **mean years** are forty-five; and her **greater years** are eighty-two.
*   **Mercury's lesser years** are twenty; his

# Gerard of Cremona <note>original: Gerard Cremonensis; a prolific 12th-century translator of scientific and astrological works from Arabic into Latin.</note>

The mean years <note>In traditional astrology, each planet was assigned "greater," "mean," and "lesser" years to predict the length of a person's life or the duration of an event.</note> of the planet are forty-nine, and his greater years are eighty. The lesser years of the *Moon* are fifteen, her mean years are thirty-nine, and her greater years are one hundred and seven. 

Also, check if *Mars* or *Saturn* are in the first house, and if the Lord of the eighth house <note>The house traditionally associated with death.</note> is with them; if the *Sun* is also in the eighth house, the querent <note>The person asking the question.</note> shall not live. Likewise, if the Lord of the *Ascendant* <note>The planet that rules the sign rising on the eastern horizon.</note> happens to be void of course <note>original: "void of courſe"; a term for a planet that completes no further major aspects with other planets before leaving its current zodiac sign, often indicating a lack of power or result.</note> and *Mars* is in the eighth house, the querent shall not live. But if the *Sun* and the *Moon* are in conjunction <note>Occupying the same degree of the zodiac.</note> in the seventh house, and *Venus* is in the second house, he shall live well.

The traits and circumstances of the birth <note>original: "accidents of the nativity"</note> are likewise to be considered. If you find *Saturn* or *Mercury* in the first house, the person is foolish and talkative. If *Mars* and *Mercury* are there, he will not be a servant, but rather a wrangler <note>An argumentative person.</note> and a scoffer. If the *Sun* and *Mercury* are there, he will be a speaker of truth. If the *Sun* is in *Aries*, he will apply himself to learn whatever he hears. 

If *Venus* is in the seventh house, he will be luxurious <note>In this context, "luxurious" usually meant lustful or lecherous.</note>. If *Saturn*, *Mercury*, and *Venus* are in their fall <note>A position in the zodiac where a planet’s influence is weakened.</note>, he will be a Sodomite <note>A historical term used to describe male-male sexual activity, then considered a vice or crime.</note>. If the *Sun* and *Venus* are in the tenth house and the *Moon* is in the first, he will be very liberal <note>Generous or big-hearted.</note>. If *Venus*, *Mercury*, and the *Dragon's Head* <note>The North Node of the Moon.</note> are in the first, he will be covetous. If the *Moon* and *Mars* are in the first, he will be subject to great bondage <note>Slavery, imprisonment, or heavy servitude.</note>. 

If *Mars* is the Lord of the birth chart <note>original: "Lord of the nativity"</note>, the person will be rich, but also an evil speaker and litigious <note>Prone to lawsuits and arguing.</note>. If the *Sun* is in the first house, he will be envious, possessing a fair body that is neither very lean nor very fat. If *Venus* is in the first, he will be white and fair. If *Mercury* is in the first, he will not be stable, but always in motion. However, if the *Moon* is found there, it indicates he will have a graceful face, breast, and arms. If *Saturn* is there, the man will be black <note>Likely referring to a "melancholic" or dark, sallow complexion.</note> and filthy. If *Jupiter* is there, he will have a round face, a fair forehead, and a ruddy complexion mixed with a little white. 

If you wish to know his office or craft: if the *Moon* is in the seventh house with *Saturn*, or in the second, tenth, or first house, it is not good for him to build any house in a city, nor to build a ship. Neither is it good for him to be a tiller of the land, to prune vines, or to plant trees. Instead, he should be employed in some office belonging to the water, or concerning marriages, or

If the *Moon* is in the fifth or third house, it will be good for him; in the second, eighth, sixth, and twelfth houses, it is neither good nor evil.

*Jupiter* signifies bishops, prelates, nobles, potentates, judges, wise men, merchants, and usurers <note>original: "Usurers"; people who lend money at interest.</note>.

*Mars* signifies warriors, arsonists <note>original: "Incendiaries"</note>, murderers <note>original: "Homicides"</note>, physicians, barbers <note>In the early modern period, barbers often performed surgery and bloodletting, which were associated with Mars's rule over sharp tools and blood.</note>, hangmen, goldsmiths, cooks, furnaces, and all fireworks <note>Here referring to any work or craft involving fire.</note>. The *Sun* signifies emperors, kings, princes, nobles, lords, and judges.

*Venus* signifies queens and ladies, marriages, communications, friendship, apothecaries, tailors, and those who make ornaments for plays, cloth-sellers, jesters, vintners <note>Wine merchants.</note>, gamblers <note>original: "Players at dice"</note>, prostitutes, and robbers.

*Mercury* signifies clerks, philosophers, astrologers, geometricians, arithmeticians, Latin writers, and painters, as well as all subtle <note>Meaning highly skilled, intricate, or clever.</note> artists—both men and women—and their crafts.

Regarding the intention of the querent <note>The person asking the question of the astrologer.</note>, look to the ascending sign and its ruler <note>original: "Lord"; the planet that governs the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the question.</note>. Wherever you find the ruler of the Ascendant, the person has come to inquire about something pertaining to that house <note>The twelve divisions of an astrological chart, each representing a different area of life such as wealth, family, or health.</note>. 

If the *Sun* is the ruler of the Ascendant, his question concerns a fear he has of some man. If *Venus* is the ruler, he inquires about the arts, so that he may learn a specific craft, or he inquires about matters belonging to women. If *Mercury* is the ruler of the Ascendant, he seeks something that is lost, or inquires about some illness <note>original: "infirmity"</note>. If it is the *Moon*, he also seeks something lost, or inquires about sickness or a disease in his eyes. If *Saturn* is the ruler of the Ascendant, he inquires about some sickness or concerning a prince; he remains silent but has great grief or anguish in his heart. If *Jupiter* is the ruler of the Ascendant, his question concerns an illness, or restitution, or an office <note>A professional position or appointment.</note> which he desires to have. If it is *Mars*, he inquires out of fear, or about an enemy, death, sickness, riches, or possessions <note>original: "substance"</note>.

### Questions of the Second House

If you wish to be informed concerning the wealth <note>original: "substance"</note> of any man, and whether he shall be rich or not, look to the Lord <note>The planet that rules the zodiac sign located at the beginning (cusp) of a specific house.</note> of the Second House. If that planet is positioned with a beneficial <note>original: "good"</note> planet, and there is a beneficial planet in the Second House as well, he shall be rich. But if the Lord of the Second House is joined with harmful <note>original: "evil"</note> planets, and a harmful planet is located in the Second House, he shall be poor.

If you want to know whether you shall recover a lent item or not, look to see if there is a harmful planet in the Second House that is in discord with its Lord; if so, then the person who is keeping the lent item will not willingly return it. But if there is a beneficial planet in the Second House that agrees with its Lord, the item shall be easily recovered. If the Lord of the Second House is exalted <note>A planet is "exalted" when it is in a zodiac sign where it can exert its strongest and most positive influence.</note> and is a harmful planet, or if a harmful planet is with him in the Second House, or even if the Lord of the second is simply exalted, the person who keeps the deposited item will not willingly restore it, but he shall be forced to do so whether he wants to or not. If a harmful planet is in the Second House, the item is still to be recovered. But if *Mercury* is in the Second House as its Lord and brings opposition <note>original: "contrariety"</note>, then it shall be recovered; and if a beneficial planet is in the Second House, it signifies recovery, even if it is the Lord of that house.

Therefore, take note of the harmony <note>original: "concord"</note> and discord of the planets: the *Moon* and *Jupiter* are friends; the *Moon* and *Mars* are enemies; *Mercury* and the *Sun* are friends; *Mercury* and *Venus* are enemies; *Venus* and *Jupiter* are friends; *Jupiter* and the *Moon* are enemies. <note>The original text contains this contradiction regarding the relationship between the Moon and Jupiter, listing them as both friends and enemies.</note>

Planets are said to be friends when they agree in one nature and quality; for example, *Mars* and the *Sun* are friends because both their natures are hot and dry. *Venus* and the *Moon* agree in being cold and moist. Planets are also friends when they agree in substance and nature, as *Jupiter* and *Venus* are friends, or when the House of one is the sign of exaltation for another, or vice versa.

## Questions of the Third House

If you desire to know how many brothers <note>original: "brethren"</note> a man has, look to the Lord <note>The planet that rules the zodiac sign on the cusp (starting point) of a house.</note> of the Third House. It is held that for every planet to which the Lord is joined <note>original: "joyned," meaning in astrological aspect or conjunction</note>, that is how many brothers the seeker <note>original: "Querent"</note> has. The *Masculine* planets signify brothers, and the *feminine* <note>original: "female"</note> planets signify sisters. Note that *Saturn*, *Mars*, the *Sun*, *Jupiter*, and the *Dragon's Head* <note>The North Node of the Moon, considered a point of increase.</note> are masculine; but the *Moon*, *Venus*, and the *Dragon's Tail* <note>The South Node of the Moon, considered a point of decrease.</note> are feminine. *Mercury* is versatile <note>original: "promiscuous"</note>, being sometimes *masculine* and sometimes *feminine*: he is *masculine* when he is joined to *masculine* planets or when he is in a *masculine* quarter of the *Zodiac*; and he is *feminine* when he is joined to *feminine* planets or when he is in a *feminine* quarter of the *Zodiac*.

## Questions of the Fourth House

If you wish to know whether it is good for you to stay in any land, city, village, territory, or house, or not, examine the Lord of the *Ascendant* <note>The planet ruling the first house, representing the person asking the question.</note>, the Lord of the Fourth House, and the Lord of the Seventh House. If the Lord of the Fourth is in the Seventh House and is beneficial <note>original: "good"</note>, and the Lords of the First and the Tenth *Houses* are beneficial and positioned with beneficial planets, then it is good for you to continue in that place where you are. But if the Lord of the Seventh is with a beneficial *planet* and the Lord of the Fourth is with a harmful <note>original: "evil"</note> *planet*, then it is not good for you to remain there; because if you do stay, you shall suffer many losses and have bad rumors <note>original: "evil reports"</note> spread about you in 그 country.

If you wish to know when someone who is absent will return, look to the Lord of the *Ascendant*. If you find him in any of the four *Angles* <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which are the most powerful points in an astrological chart.</note>, he will return within that year. If he is not in an *Angle*, then see how far he is distant from the first *Angle*; he will stay away for as many years as there are *houses* between them.

If you wish to be informed about scarcity <note>original: "dearth"</note> or plenty of goods, examine the strong *houses*, the *Succedents* <note>The 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses, which follow the Angles.</note>, and the *Cadents* <note>The 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses, considered the weakest positions.</note>;

<meta>This page concludes the analysis of the Fourth House regarding agriculture and land, and begins the instructions for the Fifth House, which traditionally governs children, pregnancy, and pleasure.</meta>

->Gerard of Cremona<-

Strong houses <term>Also known as Angular houses (the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th); they represent the most powerful areas of a chart.</term> signify famine and scarcity. The Succedent houses <term>The 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses; they "succeed" or follow the Angular houses.</term> signify a moderate season that is neither too expensive nor too cheap. The Cadent houses <term>The 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses; they are considered the weakest positions in a chart.</term> signify plenty and the affordability <note>original: "profitableness"</note> of things. Consider also the planets and their locations; if they are in strong houses, the things signified by those planets will be rare. 

Note that **Saturn** signifies fields, vines, farming tools <note>original: "instruments to work in fields"</note>, leather, and fruits such as grain <note>original: "corn," referring to cereal crops in general</note>, acorns, oak-apples, and pomegranates. **Jupiter** governs oil, honey, silkworms, cloth, wine, grass, and fragrant things <note>original: "odoriferous"</note>. **Mars** signifies wine and meat <note>original: "flesh"</note>, especially pigs <note>original: "hogs"</note>, wars, armor and its related equipment, and red clothing. The **Sun** signifies wheat, wine, purple colors, cloth, and everything associated with gold, as well as horses and birds like hawks and falcons. **Venus** signifies fatness, grapes, figs, dates, fish, and recreation <note>original: "pastimes"</note>. **Mercury** governs barley, millet, grain, money, and quicksilver <note>Mercury</note>. The **Moon** signifies oats, milk, cheese, fire, salt, cows, rams, hens, and silver; the positions of these planets will determine the relative plenty or scarcity of these items.

### Questions of the Fifth House

**If** you want to know whether a woman is pregnant <note>original: "with Child"</note>, or whether she will ever have children, look to see if the Lord of the Ascendant <note>The planet ruling the first house, representing the person asking the question.</note> is in the Seventh House, or if the Lord of the Fifth is in the First, or the Lord of the First is in the Fifth. Check also if the Lord of the Fifth is in the Seventh, or if the Lord of the Seventh is in the Fifth, or if the **Moon** is with them. 

If beneficial planets <note>original: "good Planets," such as Jupiter or Venus</note> are in the First or Fifth houses, or are with the Lord of the Fifth, or if planets in retrograde <note>original: "R"</note> are in the Angles <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses.</note>, she is pregnant or is able to have children. However, if you find none of these, but instead find harmful planets <note>original: "evil Planets," such as Saturn or Mars</note> in those same places, she is not pregnant, nor will she have children. If there are both beneficial and harmful planets in those locations, then perhaps <note>original: "happily"</note> she may have children, but they will not survive. If the signs of **Cancer**, **Scorpio**, or **Pisces** <note>These are known as the "fruitful" or fertile signs of the Zodiac.</note> are in the First or Fifth house, she may have children; but if **Leo** or **Virgo** <note>These are traditionally known as "barren" signs.</note> are there, she is not pregnant, nor...

Consider also if more of the planets are in masculine signs, then it will be a male child; if many planets are in feminine signs, then it is a female child.

### Questions of the Seventh House

Regarding theft, look to the Ruler <term>Lord</term> of the seventh house. If that ruler is in the first house, the stolen property shall be restored again. But if the Ruler of the first house is in the seventh, it shall be sought after for a long time and will eventually be found. If the **Moon** is in the first house, or with its Ruler, the item shall be found. If the **Moon** is in the fifth house, or with the Ruler of the first, or is retrograde <note>original: "R"</note> in the first house, it may be found. 

Furthermore, if the **Sun** and the **Moon** are in the fifth house, and if the Ruler of the eighth house <note>the house of death and lost resources</note> is with the Ruler of the first house in the first house, it shall be found. However, if the Ruler of the second house <note>the house of personal possessions</note> is in the eighth house, it shall not be found. If **Saturn**, **Mars**, or the **Dragon's Tail** <term>The South Node of the Moon, traditionally considered an unlucky or draining influence.</term> is in the second house, the item shall not be found, yet it will not be entirely lost. 

If the Ruler of the second house is in the first, the thing that is lost shall be found, but it will not be known from where it came. If the Ruler of the first house is in the second, it may be found after much labor. If the Ruler of the second house is in his *fall* <term>A position in the zodiac where a planet's influence is at its weakest.</term>, it will never be found; but if he is *exalted* <term>A position in the zodiac where a planet's influence is at its strongest.</term>, it shall be found very easily. The seventh house itself indicates the identity of the thief.

If you would know what kind of object has been stolen, look to the Ruler of the second house:
*   If it is **Saturn**, the item is lead, iron, a kettle, a trivet <note>a three-legged stand for a pot</note>, a garment, some black object, or leather. 
*   If it is **Jupiter**, then it is something white, such as tin or silver, or something mixed with white and yellow veins. 
*   The **Sun** signifies gold and precious pearls. 
*   **Mars** signifies things belonging to the fire. 
*   **Venus** signifies things belonging to women, such as gloves, rings, and beautiful ornaments. 
*   The **Moon** signifies animals, such as horses or mules, as well as perfumes and wares <note>original: "wars"; likely a typo for "wares" meaning merchandise.</note>. 
*   **Mercury** signifies money, books, writings, pictures, or garments of many different colors.

<meta>This page continues Gerard of Cremona's astronomical rules for horary astrology—a method of answering specific questions based on a chart drawn for the moment the question is asked. This section focuses on predicting the outcome of conflicts and the safety of those going into battle.</meta>

# Gerard of Cremona <note>original: "Gerard Cremonensis"</note>

The person whose planet is in a strong House and does not "fall" <term>fall: a planet's position of weakness in a specific zodiac sign</term>, and does not have a malevolent <note>original: "evil"</note> planet with him, will be successful. If the person who is weak is not in his own House, nor in his exaltation <term>exaltation: a sign where a planet's influence is at its strongest</term>, nor with a benefic planet, then the person whose planet is in the strong House shall overcome. Similarly, the person whose significator <term>significator: the planet representing a specific person in an astrological chart</term> is in a "mean" <term>mean: intermediate or average</term> House will have great fear and doubt in his heart, because sometimes he will hope to conquer, and at other times fear being overcome. Note that in a question concerning war and kingdoms, it is said that there is more power and efficacy, or strength <note>original: "fortitude"</note>, in the exaltation of a planet than in its House; but in all other questions, the contrary is true.

If you want to be informed concerning anyone who has gone to a fight—whether he shall return safely—look to the Ruler <term>Lord</term> of the Ascendant <note>original: "Ascedent"</note>. If it is well-placed—that is, with benefic planets—and there is a benefic planet in the first house, he will return safely. But if the *Sun* is with the Ruler of the first house in any part of the chart <note>original: "Question"</note>, do not let him go, because the *Sun* "burns" him <term>combustion: when a planet is so close to the Sun that its influence is weakened or destroyed</term>. 

If the Ruler of the seventh house is with a benefic planet, and the Ruler of the first house is likewise with a benefic planet, he will encounter some obstacle on the way, but he will not die. If a malevolent planet is with the Ruler of the first house, but a benefic planet is in the first house itself, he will suffer great damage if he goes, but not death; nevertheless, he may be seriously wounded. 

If *Saturn* is in the first house, or with the Ruler of the first, do not let him go; because some obstacle will happen to him caused by a man he will meet. And if there is a malevolent planet with the Ruler of the first, or *Saturn* is in the first house or with the Ruler of the first, he will be wounded by wood or by a stone. If *Mars* and the *Dragon’s Tail* <term>Dragon's Tail: the South Node of the Moon, traditionally associated with misfortune</term> are in the first house, or with the Ruler of the first—or if there are malevolent planets in the first house or with its Ruler—he will suffer wounds or death. 

Also check if there is a malevolent planet in the eighth house <term>the eighth house traditionally rules death</term>, because then death is to be feared. If the *Sun* is with the Ruler of the seventh house, or in the eighth, it signifies that it is dangerous <note>original: "ill"</note> to go. The same judgment applies to the seventh and the tenth houses.

And if a question is proposed concerning the event of war...

# Of Astronomical Geomancy <note>original: "Geomancie"</note>

Regarding **war**, examine the seventh and the first Houses, and their Rulers <term>Lords: the planets that rule a specific zodiac sign</term>; for the first House and its Ruler signify the **Querent** <term>Querent: the person asking the question</term>, and the seventh House and its Ruler signify the adversary. So, if there are benefic <note>original: "good"</note> planets in the first House and malevolent <note>original: "evil"</note> ones in the seventh; and if the Rulers of both the first and seventh are malevolent, the Querent shall overcome. 

However, if there is a malevolent planet with the Ruler of the first, and a malevolent planet inside the first House, and the Ruler of the seventh is benefic—or is **Retrograde** <note>original: "R."</note> in the seventh—the Querent shall be overcome, captured, or slain. 

If both Rulers are in the first House, and there are benefic planets from the beginning of the first House until the end of the House which marks the middle of the chart <note>original: "Question"</note>, but malevolent planets occupy the other half of the chart (that is to say, from the seventh until the end of the twelfth House), the adversary shall overcome. 

But if both Rulers are in the **Ascendant** <term>Ascendant: the first house, or the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>, and they are benefic from the perspective of the first House but malevolent from the perspective of the seventh, both parties shall suffer great loss, but the Querent shall have the better outcome in the end. 

If the Ruler of the Ascendant is in the seventh House (or in that part of the chart), it signifies the strength <note>original: "fortitude"</note> of the adversary. If the Ruler of the seventh is in the first (or in that part of the chart), it signifies the strength of the actor <note>the person taking action/the Querent</note>. 

Furthermore, if the Ruler of the Ascendant is in the eighth House, or positioned with the Ruler of the eighth; or if the Ruler of the eighth is in the first, or with the Ruler of the first, it signifies the death of the Querent <note>the 8th House traditionally rules death</note>. If the Ruler of the seventh is in the second House, or with its Ruler; or if the Ruler of the second is in the seventh, or with the Ruler of the seventh, it signifies the death of the enemy <note>the 2nd House is the 8th House—the house of death—when counting from the 7th House of the enemy</note>.

If you would know whether a war shall continue for a long time or not, or if it will be moderate: if the Rulers of the first and the seventh houses agree <note>are in a favorable aspect</note>, the parties shall be reconciled after the war.

If you wish to depart from the place where you are and move yourself to some other place, and you want to know whether it is better for you to stay or go—or concerning two business options, if you desire to know which of them is most profitable for you to undertake—consider...

->Gerard of Cremona<- <note>original: "Gerard Cremonenfis"</note>

...examine the Rulers <term>Lords: the planets that rule a specific zodiac sign</term> of the first and the second houses to understand the places to which you would go, the place where you currently are, and the profit <note>original: "gain"</note> you might obtain there. Also, look at the seventh and eighth houses and their Rulers for the destination you seek and the profit you might find there; choose those locations whose Rulers are more favorable <note>original: "better"</note> or are joined with beneficial planets.

Alternatively: observe the Ruler of the **Ascendant** <term>Ascendant: the first house, or the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> and the **Moon**. If they are separated from malevolent <note>original: "evil"</note> planets and joined with good and fortunate planets, it is better for you to leave your current location than to stay, and you should proceed with whatever business you have in mind. However, if the Ruler of the Ascendant and the Moon are separated from the **Fortunes** <term>Fortunes: beneficial planets, usually Venus and Jupiter</term> and joined with malevolent planets, then it is not good for you to move or to conduct your business.

Or do this: observe the Moon. If the planet from which she is moving away <note>original: "separated"</note> is better than the one she is approaching <note>original: "joined to"</note>, do not move. If the planet she is approaching is better than the one she is moving away from, then go.

### Questions of the Eighth House

Regarding any man or woman, if you wish to know what kind of death they shall die, see if **Leo**, **Scorpio**, or **Mars** (especially if it is **Retrograde** <note>original: "†R.†"</note>) are in the eighth house; if so, the person shall die by a beast. If **Saturn** is in the eighth house, or is with the Ruler of the eighth house in **Scorpio**, **Cancer**, or **Pisces**, they shall die in water. 

If a malevolent planet is in the eighth house or is with the Ruler of the eighth house—or if **Mars** or the **Dragon's Tail** <term>Dragon's Tail: Cauda Draconis, the South Node of the Moon, traditionally associated with misfortune</term> is there—they shall die by fire, iron, or fever. And if there is a beneficial planet in the eighth house, or with the Ruler of the eighth house, they shall die a peaceful death <note>original: "a good death"</note>.

### Questions of the Ninth House

Regarding long journeys, see if the Ruler <term>Lord: the planet that rules the zodiac sign on the house's threshold</term> of the eighth house is accompanied by beneficial planets. If *Saturn* is in the ninth house and exalted in the tenth—provided he is not in his own sign <note>original: "Houſe"</note>—do not go; for you will encounter many obstacles and war. 

If a malevolent <note>original: "evil"</note> planet is in the ninth house, or with the Ruler of the ninth, and the Ruler of the ninth is in his "fall" <term>Fall: a sign where a planet's influence is weakened or debased</term>, the traveler shall suffer great damage on the way. For if he travels by water, he shall suffer shipwreck; and if by land, he shall have misfortunes, be captured, or die. However, if *Saturn* is in the ninth house, or with its Ruler, you may go safely. 

If a beneficial planet is in the ninth house, or with the Ruler of the ninth, the journey is good and secure. If *Mars* is in the ninth house, you must not go, for you will meet mortal enemies on the way. If the Ruler of the ninth is with a malevolent planet or the *Sun*, it signifies a bad outcome, but the traveler shall not be captured. If the Ruler of the ninth has a beneficial planet near him, he shall escape; but if a malevolent one is near, he shall be captured. 

If *Venus* is in the ninth house, or with the Ruler of the ninth, the journey will be good because he shall find comfort from women. If *Mercury* is in the ninth and the Ruler of the ninth is with beneficial planets, the journey will be very good; but if he is with malevolent planets, it will be bad. The same is said of the *Moon* as of *Mercury*. 

If the *Dragon's Tail* <term>Dragon's Tail: the South Node of the Moon, usually representing loss or negative influence</term> is in the ninth house, he will meet with thieves or some other wicked people. If the *Dragon's Head* <term>Dragon's Head: the North Node of the Moon, representing gain or protection</term> is in the ninth, the journey will be good because he shall be accompanied by noblemen. In this same manner, you may judge questions in the third house concerning short journeys.

If you wish to know when the journey shall be completed, look to the Ruler of the ninth house and judge according to his strength <term>fortitude</term> or weakness <term>debility</term>. This is because the place where the planet is positioned signifies years, months, or days. In this way, you shall judge concerning his stay and approximately what time he will return <note>original: "come"</note>.

[The time of return] comes by turning the years of the Ruler of the ninth house <term>Lord of the ninth: the planet that rules the zodiac sign located on the threshold of the ninth house</term> into days; because he shall stay as many days as the planet signifies in the place where he is. Or otherwise: weak Angular houses <term>Angles: the four most powerful houses in a chart (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th), representing the cardinal directions</term> signify a speedy journey; mean Angular houses signify a journey of average length. And this I say concerning his return.

If you would know whether he shall return from his journey with an unsuccessful trip <note>original: "imperfect voyage"</note> or not, see if the Moon is joined with the Ruler of the first, the third, or the ninth houses, and if that planet is in its "fall" <term>Fall: a sign in the zodiac where a planet's influence is significantly weakened</term>; because if it is so, he shall return with an unsuccessful trip. And if the Moon is in her "exaltation" <term>Exaltation: a sign in the zodiac where a planet's influence is most powerful</term>, the journey shall be quickly completed. And if there are two strong planets and one "cadent" <term>Cadent: houses that "fall away" from the powerful angles (3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th), often signifying weakness or travel</term>, the journey shall be made; and if one is strong and another is in its fall, he shall turn back.

### Questions of the Tenth House

If you would know whether you shall have any honor or benefit from a King, Bishop, or Lord, look to the first house and the ninth house and their Rulers. If the Ruler of the first house is in the ninth, or with the Ruler of the ninth, or with any other beneficial planet; or if the Ruler of the ninth is in the first, or with the Ruler of the first, or with any other beneficial planet—such as *Venus*, *Jupiter*, or the *Dragon's Head* <term>Dragon's Head: the North Node of the Moon, traditionally associated with good fortune and growth</term>; or if any of them are the Ruler of the ninth or the Ruler <note>original: "R."</note> of the first, he shall receive honor and benefits from them.

And if you would know whether he shall have it in his own country or in a foreign country, look to see if the Ruler of the ninth is in the Angular houses; if so, it shall be in his own country. If he is in "succedent" houses <term>Succedent: houses that follow the Angles (2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th), representing stability and resources</term>, it shall be near; but if he is in cadent houses, it will be very far away.

# Of Astronomical Geomancy.

### Questions of the eleventh House.

If you would know when it is good to launch a ship to sea, look at the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Ascendant: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the inquiry</note>. If it is in a "stable" sign <term>stable: a Fixed sign (Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, or Aquarius), suggesting stability or slowness</term>, the ship will be heavy or slow; but if the Lord of that sign is with a good planet, she will carry a great weight. If the <term>Ascendant</term> is in an "instable" sign <term>instable: a Moveable or Cardinal sign (Aries, Cancer, Libra, or Capricorn), suggesting movement</term> and with a good planet, the ship will be swift and carry a good load. And the results will be moderate if the <term>Ascendant</term> is in a "mean" sign <term>mean: a Mutable sign (Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces), which represents a middle ground</term>. You may judge a question concerning a horse in this same manner.

If any "instable" sign is ascending and its Lord is in its "exaltation" <term>Exaltation: a specific sign where a planet’s influence is most powerful</term> or is otherwise fortunate, and the Moon views it with a lowering aspect <note>original: "lowring Aspect"; likely a square or opposition aspect</note> or a <term>Sextile</term> <note>Sextile: an auspicious astrological angle of 60 degrees</note>, then let the ship be launched <note>original: "applied to the water"</note>, because she will be very swift. If any misfortune <note>original: "evil"</note> is predicted for her, or if she is likely to be pulled into trouble, then launch her when a "stable" sign is ascending, or when the Moon is in the third, fifth, eighth, ninth, or tenth house or "mansion" <term>mansion: one of the 28 Lunar Mansions, segments of the zodiac the Moon passes through daily</term>.

If you would know what wind she shall have, look at the <term>Ascendant</term> and its Lord—whether he is with good or evil planets and in what place—and judge accordingly.

If you desire a strong wind, spread your sails at the rising of <term>Aquarius</term> <note>original: "Aquary"</note>. If you want a light wind, spread your sails when <term>Libra</term> is ascending. If you want a moderate wind, then set your sails under the sign of <term>Gemini</term>.

## Of the MAGIC of the Ancients,
### The Greatest Study of Wisdom.

> *In all things, ask counsel of the Lord;*
> *and do not think, speak, or do*
> *anything in which God is not your*
> *counselor.*

->Proverbs 11:13<-

> *He who walks deceitfully reveals*
> *secrets; but he who is of a faithful spirit*
