# ARBATEL of MAGIC:

->OR,<-

## The Spiritual Wisdom of the Ancients,

->both the wise men of the people of God,<-
->and the Magi <term>Magi: ancient practitioners of sacred arts and wisdom</term> of the Gentiles: for the illustration<-
->of the glory of God and his<-
->love to Mankind.<-

Now for the first time produced out of darkness into the light, against all evil magicians <note>original: "caco-Magicians," from the Greek *kakos* meaning "bad" or "evil"</note> and those who despise the gifts of God; for the benefit and delight of all those who truly and piously love the creatures of God and use them with thanksgiving, for the honor of God and the profit of themselves and their neighbors.

<summary>This is the title page of the "Arbatel of Magic," a influential Renaissance grimoire that frames the study of magic as a spiritual pursuit aligned with Christian piety and divine wisdom. It emphasizes the importance of seeking God's counsel and distinguishes its "spiritual wisdom" from the practices of "evil magicians."</summary>

<keywords>Arbatel, Magic, Magi, Spiritual Wisdom, Proverbs 11, Caco-Magicians, Renaissance Grimoire, Occult Philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 206 ---
<meta>This page continues from the title page, providing a table of contents for the nine "Tomes" (books) that comprise the Arbatel of Magic. It outlines the specific types of magical wisdom that the author intends to cover.</meta>

# Arbatel of Magic.

->Containing nine volumes <note>original: "Tomes"</note> and seven sets of seven <note>original: "Septenaries"</note> of<-
->APHORISMS.<-

The first is called *Isagoge*, or A Book of the Instructions of Magic: or, *Introductory* <note>original Greek: ἰσαγωγικῆς (*isagogikes*)</note>, which in forty-nine aphorisms contains the most general precepts of the entire art.

The second is Microcosmic Magic: what the Microcosm <term>Microcosm: literally "the little world," referring to the human being as a reflection of the entire universe</term> has achieved magically through his spirit and the personal guardian <note>original: "Genius"</note> assigned to him from his birth <note>original: "Nativity"</note>, which is spiritual wisdom; and how this is achieved.

The third is Olympic Magic: in what manner a person may act or be acted upon <note>original: "do and suffer," reflecting the philosophical concept of *agere et pati*</note> by the spirits of Olympus.

The fourth is Hesiodic and Homeric Magic, which teaches operations involving the spirits called "evil demons" <note>original: "Cacodaemones," from the Greek for bad or evil spirits</note>, treating them as if they were not enemies to mankind.

The fifth is Roman or Sibylline Magic, which acts and operates with guardian spirits and lords to whom the whole globe of the earth is distributed. This is a very distinguished Magic <note>original Latin: *valde insignis Magia*</note>. The doctrine of the Druids is also referred to this category.

The sixth is Pythagorean Magic, which only interacts with spirits to whom the teaching of the arts is given, such as medicine <note>original: "Physick"</note>, mathematics, alchemy, and similar types of arts.

The seventh is the magic of Apollonius and the like, which agrees with Roman and Microcosmic Magic. It is unique only in that it has power over the spirits that are hostile to mankind.

The eighth is Hermetic—that is, Egyptian—Magic; it does not differ much from Divine Magic.

The ninth is that wisdom which depends solely upon the Word of God; and this is called Prophetic Magic.

<summary>This page outlines the nine books of the Arbatel, categorizing magic into different schools including Microcosmic, Olympic, Pythagorean, and Prophetic systems. It defines the scope of the work, from the instruction of the individual to the governance of planetary and elemental spirits.</summary>

<keywords>Tomes, Aphorisms, Isagoge, Microcosm, Olympic Magic, Cacodaemones, Sibylline Magic, Druids, Pythagorean Magic, Apollonius, Hermetic Magic, Prophetic Magic</keywords>

# The First Volume <note>original: "Tome"</note> of the Book of
# Arbatel of Magic,
->CALLED<-
# ISAGOGE <note>Greek: εἰσαγωγή (*isagoge*), meaning "Introduction" or "Instructional Manual"</note>.

In the name of the Creator of all things, both visible and invisible, who reveals His mysteries from His treasures to those who call upon Him, and who fatherly and mercifully bestows His secrets upon us without measure: May He grant to us, through His only-begotten Son Jesus Christ our Lord, His ministering spirits—the revealers of His secrets—so that we may write this Book of *Arbatel*, concerning the greatest secrets that are lawful for humans to know, and to use them without offense to God. *Amen.*

## The First Septenary <term>Septenary: a group or set of seven</term> of Aphorisms.

### *The First Aphorism.*

Whoever would know secrets, let him know how to keep secret things secretly; to reveal those things that are to be revealed, and to seal those things which are to be sealed; and not to give holy things to dogs, nor cast pearls before swine <note>This is a reference to a well-known proverb from the New Testament (Matthew 7:6), advising against sharing sacred or valuable knowledge with those who cannot appreciate or respect it.</note>.

...swine. Observe this Law, and the eyes of your understanding shall be opened to understand secret things; and you shall have whatever your mind desires to be divinely revealed to you. You shall also have the angels and spirits of God prompt and ready in their nature to minister to you, as much as any human mind can desire.

### *Aphorism 2.*

In all things, call upon the name of the Lord. Without prayer to God through His only-begotten Son, do not undertake to do or think anything. Use the spirits given and assigned to you as ministers—without rashness or presumption—as the messengers of God, maintaining a due reverence toward the Lord of Spirits. For the remainder of your life, fulfill your days by conducting <note>original: "demeaning"</note> yourself peaceably, for the honor of God and the benefit of yourself and your neighbor.

### *Aphorism 3.*

Live to yourself and the Muses <note>The Muses were the ancient goddesses of inspiration in the arts and sciences. Here, the author encourages a life of study and intellectual reflection.</note>. Avoid the friendship of the common crowd <note>original: "Multitude"</note>. Value your time highly and be beneficial to all people. Use your gifts and be vigilant in your calling; let the Word of God never depart from your mouth.

### *Aphorism 4.*

Be obedient to good advice. Avoid all procrastination. Accustom yourself to constancy and seriousness <note>original: "Gravity"</note> in both your words and deeds. Resist the temptations of the Tempter <note>A traditional term for the Devil.</note> by the Word of God. Flee from earthly things; seek after heavenly things. Put no confidence in your own wisdom, but look to God in all things, according to that passage of Scripture: *When we do not know what we should do, we lift up our eyes to You, O God, and from You we expect our help.* <note>original: "When we know not what we shall do, unto thee, O God, do we lift up our eyes, and from thee we expect our help." This is a paraphrase of 2 Chronicles 20:12.</note> For where all human help <note>original: "refuges"</note> fails us, there...

### *Aphorism 5.*

> You shall love the Lord your God with all your heart, and with all your strength, and your neighbor as yourself. ### *Aphorism 6.*

Whatever you have learned, repeat it frequently and fix it in your mind. Learn much, but not many things <note>This is a translation of the Latin proverb *multum, non multa*, suggesting it is better to master a few deep subjects than to have a superficial knowledge of many.</note>, because a human understanding cannot be equally capable in all things, unless it is one that is divinely regenerated <note>spiritually reborn or enlightened by God</note>. to things or persons, and has also distributed certain powers and offices to them from His treasures. Thus, the characters and names of stars do not have any power because of their shape or pronunciation, but because of the virtue <note>original: "vertue" — here meaning inherent power or efficacy</note> or office which God has ordained by nature for such a name or character. For there is no power in heaven, on earth, or in hell that does not descend from God; and without His permission, they can neither give nor carry out any action with anything they possess.

### *Aphorism 9.*

The highest wisdom is that which comes from God. Next is the wisdom found in spiritual creatures. Third is the wisdom in physical <note>original: "corporal"</note> creatures. Fourth is the wisdom in nature and natural things. The spirits that are fallen <note>original: "apostate" — spirits that have rebelled against God</note> and reserved for the Last Judgment follow these after a long interval. Sixth, there are the ministers of punishment in hell who are obedient to God. Seventh, the Pigmies <note>original: "Pigmies" — in the context of 16th-century occult philosophy, these are gnomes or elemental spirits associated with the earth</note> do not occupy the lowest place, nor do those who inhabit the elements and elemental things. 

It is therefore necessary to know and distinguish the different types of wisdom belonging to the Creator and His creatures. This ensures it is clearly revealed to us what we should take for our use from everything, and that we may truly know how and in what manner that should be done. For truly, every creature is ordained for some profitable purpose for human nature and for its service, as the Holy Scriptures, reason, and experience testify.

### *Aphorism 10.*

God the Father Almighty, Creator of heaven and earth and of all things visible and invisible, declares in the Holy Scriptures that He watches over us. Like a tender father who loves his children, He teaches us what is profitable and what is not; what we should avoid and what we should embrace. Then, He draws us toward obedience with great 

<meta>catchword: promises</meta>

<summary>This page establishes that all magical or natural power stems from God rather than the symbols themselves and outlines a hierarchy of wisdom ranging from the divine down to elemental spirits.</summary>
<keywords>divine authority, hierarchy of wisdom, elemental spirits, Pigmies, spiritual creatures, providence, Arbatel</keywords>

promises of physical <note>original: "corporal"</note> and eternal benefits, and deters us (with the threat of punishments) from those things which are not profitable for us. Turn over, therefore, with your hand, both night and day, those holy Writings, so that you may be happy in the present, and blessed for all eternity. Do this, and you shall live, as the holy Books have taught you.

### Aphorism 11.

The number four is *Pythagorean* <note>original: "Pythagorical"</note>, and the first square <note>original: "Quadrate"</note>; therefore, let us place here the foundation of all wisdom, following the wisdom of God revealed in the holy Scriptures and the considerations proposed in Nature.

Grant, therefore, to him who solely depends upon God, the wisdom of every creature to serve and obey him, *willingly or unwillingly* <note>original: "nolens volens"</note>. And in this, the omnipotence of God shines forth. It consists, therefore, in this: that we discern the creatures which serve us from those that are unwilling, and that we may learn how to apply <note>original: "accommodate"</note> the wisdom and duties <note>original: "offices"</note> of every creature to ourselves. This Art is not handed down except by divine means. To whom God wills, he reveals his secrets; but to whom he will not bestow anything out of his treasuries, that person shall attain to nothing without the will of God.

Therefore we ought truly to desire *the all-powerful wisdom* <note>original: "τὴν παναλκῆ δὴ σοφίαν"</note> from God alone, who will mercifully impart these things to us. For he who has given us his Son, and commanded us to pray for his Holy Spirit—how much more will he subject the whole of creation to us, both things visible and invisible? *Whatever you ask, you shall receive.* Beware that you do not abuse the gifts of God, and all things shall work together for your salvation. Above all things, be watchful in this: that your names are written in heaven. It is a lesser matter <note>original: "more light" — meaning less significant compared to salvation</note> that the spirits be obedient to you, as Christ warns.

### Aphorism 12.

In the *Acts of the Apostles*, the Spirit says to *Peter* after the Vision, *Go down, and do not doubt, for I have sent them*, when he was summoned by *Cornelius* the Centurion. In this manner, through spoken words, all teachings <note>original: "disciplines"</note> are delivered by the holy Angels of God, as it appears from the records <note>original: "monuments"</note> of the Egyptians. These things were afterwards corrupted <note>original: "vitiated"</note> and distorted by human opinions, and by the prompting of evil spirits who sow weeds <note>original: "tares"</note> among the children of disobedience, as is evident from *St. Paul* and *Hermes Trismegistus*. There is no other way of restoring these Arts than by the doctrine of the holy Spirits of God, because *true faith comes by hearing*. But so that you may be certain of the truth and may not doubt whether the spirits that speak with you declare things true or false, let it depend only upon your faith in God, so that you may say with *Paul*, *I know whom I have believed* <note>original: "I know on whom I trust"; a reference to 2 Timothy 1:12</note>. If no sparrow can fall to the ground without the will of the Father who is in heaven, how much more will God not allow you to be deceived, O you of little faith, if you depend wholly upon God and cling <note>original: "adherest"</note> only to him?

### Aphorism 13.

The Lord lives; and all things which live, live in him. And he is truly Yahweh <note>original: יהוה</note>, who has given to all things the nature that they possess. By his word alone, through his Son, he has produced all things out of nothing which are in existence. He calls all the stars and all the host of heaven by their names. Therefore, he to whom God has revealed the names of His creatures knows the true strength and nature of things, and the order and governance <note>original: "policie"</note> of every creature, visible and invisible. It also follows that he receives power from God to extract the virtues <note>original: "vertues"</note> in nature and the hidden secrets of the creature, and to produce their

### Aphorism 14.

Your soul lives forever through him who created you; therefore, call upon the Lord your God, and serve him alone. You will do this if you fulfill the purpose <note>original: "end"</note> for which you were ordained by God, and if you do what you owe to God and to your neighbor. God requires of you a mind that honors his Son and keeps his Son’s words in your heart. If you honor him, you have done the will of your Father who is in heaven. To your neighbor, you owe acts <note>original: "offices"</note> of humanity, and you should draw all people who come to you to honor the Son. This is the Law and the Prophets <note>A biblical summary of the core teachings of the Old Testament, found in Matthew 7:12.</note>. In earthly <note>original: "temporal"</note> matters, you ought to call upon God as a father, so that he may give you all the necessities of this life; and you ought to help your neighbor with the gifts that God bestows on you, whether they are spiritual or physical <note>original: "corporal"</note>.

Give me also a capable and teachable heart, so that I may easily understand those things which you shall teach me, and may store them in my understanding, that I may bring them forth as if out of your inexhaustible treasures for all necessary uses. And give me grace, that I may use these gifts of yours humbly, with fear and trembling, through our Lord* Jesus Christ, *with your Holy Spirit.* Amen.

# The Third Septenary <note>A "septenary" is a group or set of seven; the *Arbatel* is organized into seven sets of seven aphorisms.</note>.

## Aphorism 15.

They are called **Olympic spirits**, who inhabit the firmament <note>the heavens or the sky, traditionally thought of as a solid dome</note> and the stars of the firmament. The duty <note>original: "office"</note> of these spirits is to declare destinies and to administer fatal charms <note>original: "fatal Charms"; influences or spells related to fate and the stars</note>, as far as God is pleased to permit them. For nothing—neither evil spirit nor evil destiny—shall be able to hurt someone who has the Most High for their refuge. Therefore, if any of the **Olympic** spirits should reveal or declare what the star to which he is appointed portends <note>foreshadows or predicts</note>, he can still bring nothing into action unless he is permitted by the Divine power. It is God alone who gives them power to carry it out. Unto God, the maker of all things...

### Aphorism 16.

There are seven different governments of the Spirits of **Olympus**, by whom God has appointed the whole frame and universe of this world to be governed: and their visible stars are <term>ARATRON</term>, <term>BETHOR</term>, <term>PHALEG</term>, <term>OCH</term>, <term>HAGITH</term>, <term>OPHIEL</term>, and <term>PHUL</term>, according to the Olympic language. Every one of these has under him a mighty military host <note>original: "Militia"</note> in the firmament <note>the heavens or the sky</note>.

-><term>ARATRON</term> rules 49 visible provinces.<-
-><term>BETHOR</term> rules 32.<-
-><term>PHALEG</term> rules 35.<-
-><term>OCH</term> rules 28.<-
-><term>HAGITH</term> rules 21.<-
-><term>OPHIEL</term> rules 14.<-
-><term>PHUL</term> rules 7.<-

So that there are 186 Olympic provinces in the whole universe, wherein the seven governors exercise their power: all of which are elegantly set forth in astronomy. But in this place it is to be explained in what manner these princes and powers may be drawn into communication. **Aratron** appears in the first hour of Saturday, and very truly gives answers concerning his provinces and their inhabitants <note>original: "Provincials"</note>. So likewise do the rest appear in order on their days and hours. Also, every one of them rules for 490 years. The beginning of their simple cycle <note>original: "Anomaly"; here referring to a chronological period or astronomical cycle</note>, in the 60th year before the Nativity of Christ, was the beginning of the administration of **Bethor**; and it lasted until the year of our Lord 430. He was succeeded by **Phaleg**, until the year 920. Then

## Aphorism 17.

Magically, the princes of the seven governors are called simply by their names and offices, which God has given to them, in whatever time, day, and hour they rule—whether visibly or invisibly. This is done by presenting their character <note>original: "Character"; a mystical sigil or symbol used to represent and summon a spirit</note>, which they have given or confirmed.

The governor **Aratron** has power over those things that occur naturally—that is, in the same way and regarding the same subjects that astronomy ascribes to the power of **Saturn**.

->*The things which he does of his own free will are:*<-

1. He can instantly convert anything into stone, whether animal or plant, while it retains its original appearance.
2. He converts treasures into coals, and coals into treasure.
3. He gives familiar spirits <note>original: "familiars"; supernatural entities believed to assist a practitioner</note> with a specific, limited power.
4. He teaches *alchemy*, *magic*, and *medicine* <note>original: "Phyfick"; the study and practice of healing</note>.
5. He reconciles subterranean <note>underground</note> spirits to humans; he makes hairy men <note>likely referring to the summoning of wild forest spirits or gnomes</note>.
6. He can make a person invisible.
7. He makes the barren fruitful and grants long life.

->*His character.*<-

Another horizontal bar starts from the center vertical line and extends to the right, passing through the rightmost vertical line.</image-desc>

4. In all the elements, there are the seven governors with their hosts, who move in harmony with the motion of the firmament <note>the heavens or the sky</note>; and lower beings always depend upon the higher ones, as is taught in philosophy.

### Aphorism 18.

There are other names for the Olympic spirits provided by others, but only those are effective which are revealed to someone by the spirit-revealer, whether visibly or invisibly. These names are given to each person according to what is predestined for them; therefore, they are called constellations, and they rarely remain effective for more than 40 years. Therefore, it is safest for young practitioners of the Art to work through the offices <note>original: "offices"; the specific duties or functions assigned to a spirit</note> of the spirits alone, without using their names. If they are pre-ordained to master the art of magic, the other parts of the art will present themselves to them of their own accord.

### *Aphorism 19.*

*Olympus* and its inhabitants offer themselves to people of their own accord in the form of spirits; and they are ready to perform their duties <note>original: "Offices"</note> for them, whether they desire it or not. How much more, then, will they attend to you if they are invited? However, evil spirits and destroyers also appear, which is caused by the envy and malice of the devil; it also happens because people lure and draw them to themselves through their sins, as a punishment due to sinners. Therefore, whoever desires to communicate familiarly with spirits should keep themselves from all great <note>original: "enormous"</note> sins and diligently pray to the Most High to be their protector; then he shall break through all the snares and obstacles of the devil. Let him dedicate himself to the service of God, and He will grant him an increase in wisdom.

### *Aphorism 20.*

All things are possible for those who believe and are willing to receive them; but for the unbelieving and unwilling, all things are impossible. There is no greater hindrance than a wavering mind, frivolity <note>original: "levity"</note>, inconsistency, foolish chatter, drunkenness, lust, and disobedience to the word of God. A magician, therefore, ought to be a person who is godly, honest, and consistent in their words and deeds, having a firm faith in God, being prudent, and desiring <note>original: "covetous"</note> nothing except wisdom regarding divine things.

### *Aphorism 21.*

When you wish to call any of the Olympic spirits, observe the rising of the Sun on that day and consider the nature of the spirit you desire; by saying the following prayer, your desires shall be fulfilled.

# The third <term>Septenary</term> <note>Septenary: A group or set of seven; here it refers to the third cycle of seven aphorisms in the text</note>.

## Aphorism 22.

We call that a secret which no person can achieve through human effort <note>original: "humane industry"</note> without divine revelation. This knowledge <note>original: "Science"</note> lies obscured, hidden by God within the creation; nevertheless, He permits it to be revealed by Spirits for the proper use of the thing itself. These secrets concern things that are either divine, natural, or human. However, you may examine a few of the most select secrets, which you will then be able to compare with many more.

### Aphorism 23.

Begin to learn the nature of the secret, either through a Spirit in human form, or by separate powers <note>original: "vertues separate"; refers to spiritual influences or planetary forces not embodied in a specific entity</note>, whether through human organs [senses] or by whatever means it can be achieved. Once this is understood, ask a spirit who knows that art to briefly reveal to you exactly what that secret is. Pray to God that He would inspire you with His grace, so that you may bring the secret to the result you desire, for the praise and glory of God and the benefit of your neighbor.

### Aphorism 24.

->The greatest secrets are seven in number.<-

1.  The first is the curing of all diseases within seven days, whether by symbols <note>original: "characters"; magical sigils or written charms</note>, by natural substances, or by higher spirits with divine assistance.
2.  The second is the ability to prolong life to any age we wish; I mean a physical and natural life.
<margin>* Spirits of the four elements. Paracelsus <note>Paracelsus (c. 1493–1541) was a famous Swiss physician and occultist who classified elemental spirits</note>.</margin>
3.  The third is to have the obedience of the creatures in the elements which appear as individual spirits; including Pygmies, *Sagani* <note>original: "Sagani"; a term used by Paracelsus for elemental spirits</note>, Nymphs, Dryads, and spirits of the woods.
4.  The fourth is to be able to speak with knowledge and understanding of all things visible and invisible, and to understand the power of every thing and its purpose <note>original: "to what it belongeth"</note>.
5.  The fifth is for a person to be able to govern themselves according to the purpose for which God has appointed them.
6.  The sixth is to know God, Christ, and His Holy Spirit; this is the perfection of the Microcosm <note>original: "Microcosmus"; the "little world," referring to the belief that the human being is a miniature reflection of the entire universe</note>.
7.  The seventh is to be reborn <note>original: "regenerate"</note>, like Henochius <note>original: "Henochius"; a reference to the biblical Enoch, who was said to have been taken by God and was often associated with secret wisdom</note>, the king of the lower world.

These seven secrets a person of an honest and steadfast mind...

->*The intermediate <note>original: "mean"; refers to the middle rank of secrets</note> secrets are likewise seven in number.*<-

1. The first is the transmutation of metals, which is commonly called *Alchemy*; this is certainly granted to very few, and only by special grace.

3. The third is to be able to perform astronomical and mathematical miracles, such as *hydraulic machines* <note>original: "Hydraulick-engines"; water-powered mechanical devices</note>, to manage business by the influence of Heaven, and other things of that sort.
4. The fourth is to perform the works of natural magic, of whatever kind they may be.

6. The sixth is to know the foundation of all arts which are practiced with the hands and the functions of the body.
7. The seventh is to know the foundation of all arts which are practiced by the *angelic nature* <note>original: "angelical nature"; referring to the higher, spiritual, or intellectual faculties of humans</note> of man.

->*The lesser secrets are seven.*<-

1. The first is to do things diligently and to accumulate much money.
2. The second is to rise from a humble <note>original: "mean"</note> state to positions of dignity and honor, and to establish a new family lineage which may be illustrious and achieve great things.
3. The third is to excel in military affairs, to successfully achieve great things, and to be a leader among the leaders <note>original: "head of the head"</note> of kings and princes.
4. To be a good household manager both in the country and the city.
5. The fifth is to be an industrious and fortunate merchant.

### *Aphorism 25.*

We have already explained <note>original: "declared"</note> what a secret is, as well as its various types and categories. It now remains to show how we may attain the knowledge of those things which we desire.

The true and only way to all secrets is to turn to God, the Author of all good; and as *Christ* teaches:

> 1. *First, seek the kingdom of God and his righteousness, and all these things shall be added to you.*
> 2. *Also, see that your hearts are not burdened with overindulgence <note>original: "surfeting"; excessive eating or drinking</note>, drunkenness, and the cares of this life.*
> 3. *Also, commit your cares to the Lord, and he will do it.*
> 4. *Also, I the Lord your God teach you what things are profitable for you, and guide you in the way you should walk.*
> 5. *And I will give you understanding, and will teach you the way you shall go, and I will guide you with my eye.*
> 6. *Also, if you who are evil know how to give good things to your children, how much more shall your Father in heaven give his Holy Spirit to those who ask him?*
> 7. *If you do the will of my Father in heaven, you are truly my disciples; and we will come to you and make our home <note>original: "abode"</note> with you.*

If you apply these seven passages of Scripture—moving from the literal word to the Spirit, or into practice—you cannot fail <note>original: "erre"</note>, but will reach your desired goal. You will not miss the mark, and God himself, through his Holy Spirit, will teach you true and beneficial things. He will also grant you his ministering Angels to be your companions, helpers, and teachers of all the secrets of the world; and he will command every creature to be obedient to you, so that you may cheerfully...

### Aphorism 26.

There is another way, which is more common, through which secrets may also be revealed to you even when you are unaware of it. This may happen either by God, or by spirits who have power over secrets; or through dreams, or by strong imaginations and impressions, or by the configuration of a birth chart <note>original: "constellation of a nativity"</note> through celestial knowledge. In this manner, heroic men are made, of whom there are very many, including all the learned men in the world: *Plato*, *Aristotle*, *Hippocrates*, *Galen*, *Euclid* <note>original: "Euclides"</note>, *Archimedes*, and *Hermes Trismegistus* (the father of secrets), along with *Theophrastus* and *Paracelsus* <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim, known as Paracelsus, was a famous Swiss physician and alchemist.</note>. All of these men possessed within themselves all the powers <note>original: "vertues"</note> of secrets. To this category also belong *Homer*, *Hesiod*, *Orpheus*, and *Pythagoras*; however, these did not have such great gifts of secrets as the previously mentioned men. To this are also referred the nymphs, the sons of *Melusina* <note>A figure of European folklore, often depicted as a woman who is a serpent or fish from the waist down; in alchemical traditions, her "sons" were sometimes thought to be elemental beings.</note>, and the gods of the pagans <note>original: "Gentiles"</note>: *Achilles*, *Aeneas*, and *Hercules*. Also included are *Cyrus*, *Alexander the Great*, *Julius Caesar*, *Lucullus*, *Sulla* <note>original: "Sylla"</note>, and *Marius*.

It is an established rule <note>original: "canon"</note> that everyone should know their own Angel and obey them according to the Word of God. Let them also beware of the snares of the evil Angel, lest they become involved in calamities like those of *Brutus* <note>original: "Brute"</note> and *Mark Antony* <note>original: "Marcus Antonius"</note>. For more on this, refer to the book *On Fortune* and the dialogue *Eutichus* by *Giovanni Gioviano Pontano* <note>original: "Jovianus Pontanus," an Italian humanist and poet.</note>.

The third way is through diligent and hard labor, without which no great thing worthy of admiration can be obtained from the Divine Deity, as it is said:

->*You shall say or do nothing against the will of Minerva.* <note>original Latin: "Tu nihil invita dices faciesve Minerva." Minerva was the Roman goddess of wisdom; this proverb suggests that one cannot succeed in intellectual or artistic pursuits without natural talent or divine favor.</note><-

->Nothing can you do or say against *Minerva’s* will.<-

We detest all evil magicians who associate themselves with devils through their unlawful superstitions, and who obtain and bring about certain things which God permits to be done as a punishment inflicted by the devils. They also perform other evil acts with the devil as their author, just as the Scriptures testify concerning *Judas*. To this category belong all the idolaters of the past and of our own time, as well as those who abuse Fortune—of whom the heathens are full. This also includes all *Charontic* <note>original: "Charontick"; referring to Charon, the ferryman of the Greek underworld; here it refers to necromancy or the summoning of the dead</note> conjuring <note>original: "evocation"</note> of spirits, such as *Saul's* dealing with the woman <note>A reference to the Witch of Endor (1 Samuel 28)</note>, and *Lucan's* <note>original: "Lucanus"; a Roman poet who wrote the epic *Pharsalia*</note> prophecy of the deceased soldier concerning the outcome of the *Pharsalian* War, and similar instances.

### Aphorism 27.

Draw a circle with a center point, A, and a circumference marked by B, C, D, and E. To the East, let B and C form a square. To the North, C and D. To the West, D and E. And to the South, E and D. Divide these various quadrants into seven parts each, so that there are 28 parts in total; and let these be divided again into four parts each, so that there are 112 parts of the circle. These represent the total number of true secrets to be revealed. 

This circle, divided in this manner, is the "seal of the secrets of the world," which are drawn from the single center, A—that is, from the invisible God—out toward every created thing. The Prince of the Eastern <note>original: "Oriental"</note> secrets resides in the middle and has three Nobles on each side; each of these Nobles has four subordinates, and the Prince himself has four attendants. In this same way, the other Princes and Nobles have their own quadrants of secrets, each with their own four secrets. 

The Eastern secret involves the study of all wisdom; the Western, of strength; the Southern <note>original: "Meridian"</note>, of agriculture <note>original: "tillage"</note>; and the Northern, of a more disciplined <note>original: "rigid"</note> life. Thus, the Eastern secrets are recommended as the best; the Southern <note>original: "Meridian"</note> as middling; and the Western <note>Note: The original text repeats "East" here, likely a printing error for "West," as it ranks the directions in descending order of importance.</note> and Northern as the least. The purpose of this seal of secrets is so that you may know the origin of the spirits or angels who can teach the secrets entrusted to them by God. These spirits have names derived from their offices and powers, according to the

Each direction is governed by a hierarchy of spirits specializing in wisdom, strength, agriculture, or discipline.</summary>

...gift which God has separately distributed to each of them. One has the power of the sword; another, the power of pestilence; and another, the power of inflicting famine upon the people, as ordained by God. Some are destroyers of cities, like the two who were sent to overthrow *Sodom* and *Gomorrah* and the surrounding areas, as witnessed in the Holy Scriptures. Some are the watchmen over kingdoms, while others are the keepers of private individuals. From this, anyone can easily derive their names in his own language. 

Thus, anyone who wishes may ask for a physical angel <note>original: "physical Angel"; an angel governing the laws of nature and the physical world</note>, a mathematical or philosophical one, or an angel of civil wisdom, or of supernatural or natural wisdom, or for anything whatsoever. Let him ask seriously, with a deep desire of mind, and with faith and constancy; and without doubt, he shall receive what he asks for from the Father and God of all spirits. This faith overcomes all seals and brings them into subjection to the human will. 

The systematic method of calling angels follows this faith, which depends entirely on divine revelation; but without this preceding faith, the method remains in obscurity. Nevertheless, if anyone uses these names or characters as a memorial—and for no other reason—and views them simply as things created by God for the purpose of binding a spiritual power or essence, he may use them without offending God. But let him beware lest he fall into idolatry and the snares of the devil, who easily deceives the unwary with his cunning sorceries. 

The devil is not overcome except by the finger of God <note>a biblical metaphor for God's direct power, as seen in Exodus 8:19 and Luke 11:20</note>, and he is compelled into the service of man. Thus, these spirits unwillingly serve the godly, though not without bringing temptations and trials, because the commandment states that he [the serpent] shall bruise the heel of *Christ*, the "seed of the woman" <note>A reference to Genesis 3:15, traditionally interpreted by Christians as a prophecy of the conflict between Satan and Jesus</note>. We should therefore occupy ourselves with spiritual matters with fear and trembling and with great reverence toward God, interacting with spiritual essences with seriousness and justice. And whoever meddles with such things, let him beware of all levity, pride, greed, vanity, envy, and ungodliness, unless he wishes to perish miserably.

### Aphorism 28.

Because all good comes from God, who alone is good, those things which we wish to obtain from him we ought to seek through prayer in Spirit and Truth, and with a simple heart. The conclusion of the "secret of secrets" is this: that everyone should practice prayer for the things they desire, and they shall not suffer a rejection. Let no one despise prayer; for to whoever prays to God, he both can and will give. 

Now let us acknowledge him as the Author, from whom we should humbly seek our desires. A merciful and good Father loves "men of desires" <note>original: "sons of desires"; a biblical reference to Daniel 10:11, where the prophet is called a "man greatly beloved" or "man of desires" (Latin: *vir desideriorum*)</note>, as Daniel was; and he hears us sooner than we are able to overcome the hardness of our hearts to pray. But he does not want us to "give holy things to dogs," nor to despise and hold in contempt the gifts of his treasury. Therefore, diligently and often read over the first <term>Septenary</term> <note>a set or group of seven (in this context, the first seven Aphorisms or secrets)</note> of secrets, and guide and direct your life and all your thoughts according to those precepts; and all things shall yield to the desires of your mind in the Lord, in whom you trust.

## The Fifth Septenary.

### Aphorism 29.

As our study of magic <note>original: "Magick"</note> proceeds in order from the general rules stated previously, let us now come to a specific explanation of them. Spirits are either divine ministers of the word and of the Church and its members; or else they serve creatures in physical matters, partly for the salvation of the soul and body, and partly for its destruction. And nothing is done, whether good or evil, without a certain and fixed order and government. He who seeks after a good end, let him follow it; and he who desires an evil end pursues that also—and does so earnestly—resulting in divine punishment and a turning away from the divine will.

<summary>This page emphasizes the necessity of sincere prayer and moral alignment as the foundation for magic, while introducing a classification of spirits as either divine ministers or servants in physical affairs.</summary>
<keywords>prayer, Daniel, secret of secrets, Septenary, spirits, divine ministers, physical magic, spiritual government</keywords>

Therefore, let everyone compare their goals with the word of God, using it as a touchstone that will judge between good and evil. Let them determine for themselves what is to be avoided and what is to be sought after; and whatever they decide and establish for themselves, let them follow it diligently—neither procrastinating nor delaying—until they reach their appointed goal.

->Aphorism 30.<-

Those who desire riches, worldly glory, public office <note>original: "Magistracy"</note>, honors, status, or absolute power <note>original: "tyrannies"</note>—and seek them through magic—will obtain them if they strive for them diligently. Every person will succeed according to their destiny, industry, and knowledge of the magical sciences. This is shown in the history of *Melesina* <note>likely referring to Melusine, a legendary figure of folklore often associated with the magical origins of noble houses</note> and its magicians. They decreed that no one of the Italian nation should ever again rule the Kingdom of *Naples*, and they brought it to pass that the man who reigned in that age was thrown down from his throne. So great is the power of the guardian or protecting angels <note>original: "tutelar Angels"</note> of the kingdoms of the world.

->Aphorism 31.<-

Call upon the Prince of the Kingdom and issue a command to him; command whatever you wish, and it shall be done, provided that Prince is not released from his obedience by a later magician. Therefore, the Kingdom of *Naples* may be restored to the Italians once more if any magician calls upon the one who established this order and compels him to undo his act. He may also be compelled to return the secret powers taken from the treasury of magic: a Book, a Gem, and a magical Horn. If a person possessed these, they could easily make themselves the Monarch of the world if they wished. But *Judæus* <note>Latin for "The Jew"; this likely refers to a specific legendary figure or a symbolic archetype of a wise magician common in the occult literature of the time</note> chose instead to live among the Gods until the final judgment, preferring that over the temporary goods of this world. His heart is so blind that he understands nothing of the God of heaven and earth, nor thinks of him further, but instead enjoys the delights of immortal things to his

He can be summoned more easily than the Angel of *Plotinus* in the Temple of *Isis*. <note>original: "Angel of Plotinus"; referring to a famous account where an Egyptian priest summoned the guardian spirit (daimon) of the philosopher Plotinus in Rome's Temple of Isis.</note>

### Aphorism 32.

In the same way, the Romans were taught by the Sibylline books <note>The Sibyls were ancient prophetesses; the Roman state consulted a collection of their prophecies, the Sibylline Books, during times of crisis.</note>; by those means, they made themselves the lords of the world, as history testifies. However, it is the lords serving under the Prince of a Kingdom who grant the lesser government offices <note>term: "Magistracies" — public offices or positions of civil authority.</note>. Therefore, whoever desires to hold a lesser office or position of honor should magically summon a Noble serving under that Prince, and his desire will be fulfilled.

### Aphorism 33.

But he who covets lowly honors <note>original: "contemptible dignities"</note>, such as wealth alone, should call upon the Prince of Riches or one of his lords. He shall obtain his desire in whatever way he wishes to grow rich—whether through earthly goods, trade, the gifts of princes, the study of metals, or chemistry <note>original: "Chymistry," here referring to alchemy and the transmutation of metals.</note>. As he follows any established example <note>original: "president," meaning precedent or method.</note> of growing rich by these means, he shall achieve his goal.

### Aphorism 34.

All methods of summoning <note>term: "evocation" — the act of calling upon or summoning a spirit or deity.</note> are of the same kind and form. This method was well-known in ancient times to the Sibyls and high priests. In our own time, through ignorance and a lack of piety, this knowledge is completely lost. What remains has been corrupted by endless lies and superstitions.

### Aphorism 35.

Human understanding is the only thing that brings about all wonderful works, provided it is joined to a spirit. Once joined, the mind produces whatever she desires. Therefore, we must proceed carefully in magic, lest Sirens <note>In mythology, Sirens were deceptive creatures who lured men to ruin; here, the author warns of deceptive spirits.</note> and other...

## The Sixth Septenary

### Aphorism 36

Care must be taken that experiments are not mixed together, but that every one remains simple and distinct; for God and Nature have ordained all things to a certain and appointed end. For example, those who perform cures using only simple herbs and roots are the most successful of all. In this same way, the greatest influences or virtues lie hidden within constellations, words, characters, stones, and similar things, which act as miracles.

There are also words which, when spoken, immediately cause both visible and invisible creatures to obey—including creatures of our world, as well as those of the water, air, underground, the heavens <note>original: "Olympick," referring to the Olympic or celestial spheres</note>, the realms above the heavens, the underworld, and even the divine.

Therefore, simplicity is the most important thing to study, and the knowledge of such "simples" <note>term: "simples" — in medicine, a "simple" is a single herb used as a remedy; here, it refers to singular, unmixed magical components or ingredients.</note> must be sought from God; they cannot be discovered by any other means or experience.

### Aphorism 37

And let all lots <note>term: "lots" — likely referring here to the distribution of duties or the use of divination to determine the proper order of things.</note> have their proper place: Order, Reason, and Means are the three things that make all learning—of both visible and invisible creatures—easily accessible. This is the course of Order: that some creatures are creatures of the

<meta>This page continues the description of different types of spiritual creatures and transitions into Aphorism 38, which defines the primary divisions of magic and how it operates through visible and invisible means.</meta>

...the light; others, of darkness. These are subject to vanity <note>original: "vanity"; in a biblical context, this often refers to futility, emptiness, or things that are temporary and without true value.</note> because they run headlong into darkness and enthrall themselves in eternal punishment for their rebellion. 

Their kingdom is, on one hand, very beautiful regarding transitory and corruptible things because it cannot exist without some virtue and great gifts from God. On the other hand, it is most filthy and too horrid to mention because it abounds with all wickedness and sin: idolatry, contempt for God, blasphemies against the true God and His works, the worship of devils, disobedience toward magistrates <note>term: "magistrates" — civil authorities and government officials</note>, sedition, homicide, robbery, tyranny, adultery, wicked lusts, rape, theft, lies, perjury, pride, and a covetous desire for power. The kingdom of darkness consists of this mixture. 

But the creatures of light are filled with eternal truth and the grace of God; they are lords of the whole world and reign over the lords of darkness as members of Christ. Between these two groups, there is a continual war until God puts an end to their strife at His Last Judgment.

### Aphorism 38

Therefore, magic is divided into two primary categories. The first is from God, which He bestows upon the creatures of light. The other is also from God, but it is the gift He gives to the creatures of darkness. This second type is also twofold: one is for a good end, as when the princes of darkness are compelled to do good for creatures because God forces them; the other is for an evil end, as when God permits such beings to punish evil people so that they are magically deceived into destruction, or when He commands them to be cast out into destruction.

The second division of magic is that it accomplishes some works using visible instruments through visible things; it effects other works using invisible instruments through invisible things; and it performs other things using a mixture of means, both in instruments and effects.

<meta>This page continues the categorization of different types of magic according to their source and the nature of the spirits involved, following the discussion of the kingdoms of light and darkness.</meta>

<page-num>207</page-num>
<header>Arbatel of Magic.</header>

The third division is: there are some things which are brought to pass by the invocation of God alone. This is partly prophetic and philosophical, and partly what might be called "Theophrastical" <note>original: "Theophraſtical"; likely referring to the divinely inspired philosophy of Paracelsus (Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim), which emphasizes direct spiritual insight granted by God.</note>.

Other things exist which, because of an ignorance of the true God, are performed through the "Princes of Spirits" so that a person's desires may be fulfilled; such is the work of the "Mercurialists" <note>term: "Mercurialists" — practitioners of magic associated with the planet or spirit of Mercury, often linked to intelligence, communication, and commerce.</note>.

The fourth division is that some practice their magic with good angels instead of God, as if they were descending down from the most high God; such was the magic of the "Baalim" <note>original: "Baalim"; the plural of the Hebrew word "Baal" (meaning "lord" or "master"). Here it refers to a system of magic that addresses various spiritual intermediaries or planetary "lords" rather than God Himself.</note>.

Another kind of magic is that which performs its actions through the leaders of evil spirits; such were those who worked by the "minor gods" of the pagans.

The fifth division is that some interact with spirits openly and face to face, which is granted to very few. Others work through dreams and other signs, which the ancients derived from their "auguries" <note>term: "auguries" — the practice of interpreting omens, such as the flight of birds or sacrificial remains, to predict the future.</note> and sacrifices.

The sixth division is that some work through immortal creatures, while others work through mortal creatures such as nymphs, satyrs, and similar inhabitants of other elements, like "pygmies" <note>term: "pygmies" — in the context of Renaissance elemental magic, these were often considered "gnomes" or spirits belonging to the element of Earth.</note>, etc.

The seventh division is that spirits serve some people of their own accord without any special skill; others they will hardly attend to, even when called by "art" <note>term: "art" — refers here to the learned rituals, symbols, and technical skills of ceremonial magic.</note>.

Among these types of magic, the most excellent of all is that which depends upon God alone. The second most excellent is that where the spirits serve faithfully of their own accord. The third is the magic that is the property of Christians, which depends on the power that Christ has in heaven and on earth.

### Aphorism 39.

## *There is a seven-fold preparation for learning the Magic Art.*

The first is to meditate day and night on how to attain the true knowledge of God. This is achieved both by His word, revealed since the foundation of the world, and by the "seal of creation" <note>term: "seal of creation" — the philosophical concept that God’s signature or divine laws are imprinted upon the physical world.</note> and His creatures <note>term: "creatures" — in this context, "creatures" refers to all created things, whether human, animal, or spiritual.</note>; and also by the wonderful effects which the visible and invisible creatures of God demonstrate.

Secondly, it is necessary that a man descend deep into himself and primarily study to know himself: what mortal part he has in him and what is immortal; and what part is properly his own, and what is different <note>original: "diverse"; referring to the distinction between the soul's own essence and external influences.</note>.

Thirdly, he must learn to use the immortal part of himself to worship, love, and fear the eternal God, and to adore Him in Spirit and Truth. With his mortal part, he should do those things which he knows to be acceptable to God and profitable to his neighbors.

These are the three first and greatest precepts of Magic. Let everyone who desires to obtain true Magic or divine wisdom prepare himself with these, so that he may be accounted worthy of them, and become one whom the Angelic beings willingly serve—not only in secret, but also openly and, as it were, face to face.

Fourthly, every man must be vigilant to see what kind of life he shall be called to from his mother’s womb. Everyone should know whether he is born to Magic and to what species <note>term: "species" — a specific branch or type of magical practice.</note> of it. This is something that anyone who reads these writings may easily perceive and may find success in through experience; for such things and such gifts are not given to any but the lowly and humble.

In the fifth place, we must take care to understand when the spirits are assisting us in undertaking the greatest business; and for he who understands this, it is mani— <meta>The text cuts off mid-word ("manifest"); the catchword for the next page is "fest."</meta>

Here, for the most part, they sin, either through negligence, ignorance, or contempt, or by too much superstition; they also offend by ingratitude toward God, by which many famous men have afterward drawn destruction upon themselves. They sin also by rashness and obstinacy, and also when they do not use their gifts for that honor of God which is required, and instead prefer secondary tasks over the essential work <note>original: πάρεργα ἔργοις (parerga ergois) — a Greek phrase meaning "side-issues over the main business."</note>.

Sixthly, the Magician has need of faith and secrecy <note>original: "taciturnity"</note>, especially so that he discloses no secret which the Spirit has forbidden him, just as he commanded *Daniel* to seal some things—that is, not to declare them in public. Similarly, it was not lawful for *Paul* to speak openly of all things which he saw in a vision. No one will believe how much is contained in this one precept.

Seventhly, in one who would be a Magician, the greatest justice is required, so that he undertakes nothing that is ungodly, wicked, or unjust, nor lets such a thing even enter his mind; and so he shall be divinely defended from all evil.

### Aphorism 40.

When the Magician determines within himself to do any incorporeal <note>term: incorporeal — spiritual or non-physical, such as interacting with spirits or using mental powers.</note> thing, whether with any exterior or interior sense, then let him govern himself according to these seven subsequent laws to accomplish his magical end.

The first Law is this: that he knows that such a Spirit is appointed to him by God; and let him meditate that God is the beholder of all his thoughts and actions; therefore, let him direct the entire course of his life according to the rule prescribed in the word of God.

Secondly, always pray with *David*:
> *Do not take your holy Spirit from me; and strengthen me with your free Spirit; and lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil: I beseech you, O heavenly Father, do not give power to any lying spirit, as you did*

Thirdly, let him accustom himself to test the spirits, as the Scripture warns; for grapes cannot be gathered from thorns. Let us test all things and hold fast to that which is good and praiseworthy, so that we may avoid everything that is opposed to the divine power.

The fourth is to be remote and clear from all forms of superstition. For superstition consists of attributing divinity to things that possess nothing divine, or choosing and framing for ourselves a way to worship God that he has not commanded. Such are the magical ceremonies of Satan, by which he impudently offers himself to be worshipped as God.

The fifth thing to be avoided <note>original: "eschewed"</note> is all worship of idols, which attempts to bind divine power to idols or other things by their own initiative, where they were not placed by the Creator or by the order of Nature. Many false and wicked magicians pretend <note>original: "faign"</note> to have such power.

Sixthly, all the deceitful imitations and pretenses of the devil are also to be avoided, by which he imitates the power of creation and the Creator. He does this so that he might produce things with a word that are not truly what they seem to be. Such power belongs only to the omnipotence of God and cannot be shared with any created being.

Seventhly, let us cling fast to the gifts of God and of his Holy Spirit, so that we may know them and diligently embrace them with our whole heart and all our strength.

### Aphorism 41.

We come now to the nine final aphorisms of this entire volume, with which we will—with the assistance of divine mercy—conclude this whole magical introduction <note>original: "Isagoge" — a Greek term (εἰσαγωγή) meaning an introductory treatise or manual.</note>.

An evil magician is one whom evil spirits serve—by divine permission—to his temporal and eternal destruction and ruin <note>original: "perdition"</note>, in order to deceive people and draw them away from God. Such a person was *Simon Magus*, who is mentioned in the *Acts of the Apostles* and in the writings of *Clement*; Saint *Peter* commanded him to be thrown down to the earth after he had ordered himself to be raised into the air by unclean spirits as if he were a god. <note>Simon Magus is a biblical figure often cited in early Christian tradition as a sorcerer who attempted to buy spiritual power.</note>

Nevertheless, I will not deny that some people may obtain certain types of both kinds of magic through study and diligence, if such is permitted. ## The Seventh Septenary <note>A "septenary" refers to a group of seven; here, the seventh section containing seven aphorisms.</note>

### Aphorism 43.

The Lord lives, and the works of God live in him by his appointment, by which he wills them to be; for he will have them use their liberty in obedience to his commands, or in disobedience to them. To the obedient, he has proposed their rewards; to the disobedient, he has set forth their deserved punishment. Therefore, these spirits of their own free will—through their pride and contempt for the Son of God—have revolted from God their Creator and are reserved for the day of wrath. A very great power remains in them within the creation, but it is nonetheless limited, and they are confined to their bounds by the bridle of God.

Therefore, the magician of God—which signifies a wise man of God, or one informed by God—is led forth by the hand of God toward all everlasting good, including both ordinary <note>original: "mean"</note> things and also the most important physical <note>original: "corporal"</note> things.

Great is the power of Satan because of the great sins of humanity. Therefore, the magicians of Satan also perform great things, greater than anyone would believe; although they exist within their own limits, they are nevertheless beyond all human apprehension regarding the physical and transitory things of this life, as many ancient histories and daily examples testify. Both kinds of magic are different from one another in their goals: the one leads to eternal good and uses temporal things with <meta>catchword: thankfulness</meta>

### Aphorism 44.

The transition from the common life of man to a magical life is nothing other than a sleep from that life and an awakening to this life. For those things which happen to ignorant and unwise men in their common life, the same things happen to the willing and knowledgeable magician.

The magician understands when the mind meditates within itself; he deliberates, reasons, establishes, and determines what is to be done. He observes when his thoughts <note>original: "cogitations"</note> proceed from a divine separate essence <note>A spiritual being or intelligence that exists independently of the human mind.</note>, and he proves <note>meaning: tests or validates</note> what order that divine separate essence belongs to.

But the man who is ignorant of magic is tossed to and fro, as if at war with his own emotions <note>original: "affections"</note>. He does not know when they originate in his own mind or are impressed upon him by the assisting spirit <note>original: "essence"</note>, and he does not know how to defeat the plans <note>original: "counsels"</note> of his enemies by the word of God, or how to keep himself from the snares and deceits of the tempter.

### Aphorism 45.

The greatest rule of magic is to know what every man ought to receive for his use from the assisting spirit and what to refuse. He may learn this from the Psalmist <note>Refers to King David; the following quote is from Psalm 119:9.</note>, who says, *"How can a young man keep his way pure? By living according to your word, O Lord."* To keep the word of God so that the evil one does not snatch it out of the heart is the chief rule of wisdom. It is permissible to accept and act upon other suggestions which are not contrary to the glory of God and charity toward our neighbors, without inquiring from what spirit such suggestions proceed. But we must take care that we

### Aphorism 46.

There is nothing that so much suits a person as constancy <note>meaning steadfastness or consistency</note> in their words and deeds, and when like rejoices in its like; there are none happier than such people, because the holy Angels are familiar with them and take them into their custody. On the contrary, men who are inconstant are lighter than nothing, like rotten leaves. From these principles, we choose the 46th Aphorism: just as everyone governs themselves, so they attract spirits of their own nature and condition to themselves. 

However, one truly advises that no man should carry himself beyond his own calling <note>his proper station or God-given purpose</note>, lest he draw to himself some malignant spirit from the furthest parts of the earth, by whom he shall either be deluded <note>original: "infatuated"</note> and deceived, or brought to final destruction. This precept appears most plainly in the story of *Midas*: when he wanted to convert all things into gold, he drew a spirit to himself that was able to perform this. But being deceived by that spirit, he would have been brought to death by famine if his foolishness had not been corrected by the mercy of God. 

The same thing happened in our times to a certain woman near Frankfurt an der Oder <note>original: "Fanckford at Odera"</note>, who would scrape together and devour money from anything. I wish that men would diligently weigh this precept and not consider the histories of *Midas* and similar stories to be mere fables; they would be much more diligent in moderating their thoughts and desires <note>original: "affections"</note>, and they would not be so perpetually tormented by the spirits of the golden mountains of *Utopia* <note>original: "Utopia" – a reference to Sir Thomas More’s fictional island, here meaning unattainable or imaginary wealth.</note>. Therefore, we ought most diligently to observe that

### Aphorism 47.

Anyone who is faithfully engaged <note>original: "conversant"</note> in their calling <note>original: "vocation"</note> shall also have spirits as constant companions to their desires, who will continually supply them in all things. But if they have any knowledge in magic, these spirits will not be unwilling to show themselves, to converse familiarly with them, and to serve them in those various ministries to which they are assigned <note>original: "addicted" – used here in the archaic sense of being bound or dedicated to a specific task</note>. Good spirits serve in good things leading toward salvation; evil spirits serve in every evil thing leading toward destruction. Examples of this are not lacking in the histories of the entire world, and they happen daily in the world. **Theodosius** <note>Theodosius I, the Roman Emperor who reportedly received divine aid during the Battle of the Frigidus.</note> before his victory over **Arbogastus** <note>A Frankish general who rebelled against the empire.</note> is an example of the good spirits. **Brutus** <note>original: "Brute" – Marcus Junius Brutus, the assassin of Julius Caesar.</note> before he was killed was an example of evil spirits; he was persecuted by the spirit of **Caesar** and so driven to punishment that he killed himself—he who had slain his own father <note>Brutus was a protégé and rumored biological son of Caesar</note> and the "Father of his Country" <note>original: "Father of his Country" – a title held by Julius Caesar.</note>.

### Aphorism 48.

All magic is a revelation from spirits of the same nature as the magic itself. For example, in the works of **Hesiod**, the nine Muses are called the "ninth magic," as he clearly testifies of himself in the *Theogony* <note>Hesiod’s epic poem regarding the origins and genealogies of the Greek gods.</note>. In **Homer**, it is the guardian spirit <note>original: "genius"</note> of **Ulysses** in the *Psychagogia* <note>original: "Psigiogagia" – likely referring to the scene in the Odyssey where spirits are summoned from the underworld.</note>. In **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary author of Hermetic philosophy.</note>, it refers to the spirits of the higher parts of the mind. God revealed himself to **Moses** in the burning bush. For the three wise men who came to seek **Christ** at Jerusalem, the Angel of the Lord was their leader. The Angels of the Lord directed **Daniel**. Therefore, there is nothing that anyone should boast about; *for it does not depend on human will or effort, but on God, who shows mercy* <note>original: "For it is not unto him that willeth, nor unto him that runneth; but to whom God will have mercy" – a quote from Romans 9:16.</note>, or on some other spiritual destiny. From this source all magic springs, and to that source it will return, whether it be good or evil. In

--- Page 244 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from Aphorism 48 regarding the origins of magic and the influence of spirits on human history, leading into the conclusion of the first book (the Isagoge).</meta>

<page-num>216</page-num>
<header>Arbatel of Magic.</header>

In this manner **Tages**, the first teacher of the magic of the **Romans**, emerged <note>original: "gushed out" – refers to the myth of Tages, a prophetic child who was plowed up from a furrow in the earth</note> from the earth. **Diana of the Ephesians** demonstrated her worship as if it had been sent from heaven. So also did **Apollo**. All the religion of the heathens is derived from these same spirits; nor are the opinions of the **Sadducees** <note>A Jewish sect at the time of Jesus that denied the existence of spirits and the resurrection</note> merely human inventions.

### Aphorism 49.

The conclusion, therefore, of this introductory treatise <note>original: *Isagoge* – a Greek term for an introductory work or foundational text</note> is the same as what we have already stated: that just as there is one God from whom all good comes, there is also one sin—namely, disobedience against the will of the commanding God—from which all evil originates. Thus, *the fear of God is the beginning of all wisdom and the benefit of all magic.* For obedience to the will of God follows the fear of God; and after this follows the presence of God and of the Holy Spirit, the ministry of the holy angels, and all good things from the inexhaustible treasures of God.

However, useless and damnable magic arises from this: when we lose the fear of God in our hearts and allow sin to rule over us, the Prince of this world—the god of this world—begins to act. He establishes his kingdom in place of holy things within those whom he finds useful for his domain. There, just as the spider catches the fly that falls into its web, so Satan spreads his nets and captures men with the snares of greed <note>original: "covetousness"</note> until he drains them and drags them into eternal fire. These individuals he nurtures and raises to high positions, so that their eventual fall may be even greater.

Kind reader, apply your eyes and mind to both sacred and secular <note>original: "profane" – here meaning non-religious or worldly history</note> histories, and to the things you see happening daily in the world. You shall find that all things are full of magic, according to a two-fold science of good and evil. So that these may be better distinguished, we will provide <unclear>here th</unclear>eir division and subdivision for the conclusion of these introductory treatises <note>original: *Isagoges*</note>. In this, everyone may contemplate what should be followed and what should be avoided, and how much effort each person should put forth to reach a proper and fulfilling end to their life.

<summary>This page concludes the introductory section (Isagoge) by contrasting divine magic, rooted in the fear of God, with damnable magic, which stems from sin and the influence of Satan. It references mythological figures like Tages and Diana to argue that ancient pagan religions were also driven by spiritual forces.</summary>

<keywords>Tages, Diana of the Ephesians, Sadducees, Isagoge, fear of God, Satan, dualism, good vs evil magic, Prince of this world</keywords>

--- Page 245 ---
<meta>This page serves as the concluding summary of the Arbatel of Magic, presenting a taxonomic chart that categorizes all forms of "Science" or knowledge into Good and Evil branches, contrasting divine and human wisdom with diabolical arts.</meta>

<page-num>217</page-num>
<header>Arbatel of Magic.</header>

<image-desc>A taxonomic branching diagram categorizing "Sciences" through a series of curly braces. The chart divides Sciences into "Good" and "Evil." "Good" is subdivided into "Theosophy" and "Anthrosophy given to man," while "Evil" is subdivided into "Cakosophy" and "Cacodemony." Each sub-category is accompanied by detailed definitions or characteristics.</image-desc>

### Sciences

*   **Good**
    *   **Theosophy** <note>original: "Theoſophy" — Divine wisdom; from the Greek *Theos* (God) and *Sophia* (Wisdom)</note>
        *   Knowledge of the Word of God, and ruling one's life according to the Word of God.
        *   Knowledge of the government of God by Angels, which the Scripture calls watchmen <note>A biblical term, particularly from the Book of Daniel, referring to a specific class of angels who observe and execute God's decrees</note>; and to understand the mystery of Angels.
    *   **Anthrosophy** <note>original: "Anthro-ſophy" — Human wisdom; from the Greek *Anthropos* (Man) and *Sophia* (Wisdom)</note> **given to man**
        *   Knowledge of natural things.
        *   Wisdom in human affairs.
*   **Evil**
    *   **Cakosophy** <note>original: "Cakoſo-phy" — Evil wisdom; from the Greek *Kakos* (Bad/Evil) and *Sophia* (Wisdom)</note>
        *   Contempt for the Word of God, and living according to the will of the devil.
        *   Ignorance of the government of God by Angels.
        *   To despise the protection <note>original: "custody"</note> of the Angels, and to take companions from the devil.
        *   Idolatry.
        *   Atheism.
    *   **Cacodemony** <note>original: "Cacodæ-mony" — The influence or study of evil spirits; from the Greek *Kakos* (Evil) and *Daimon* (Spirit/Spirit-power)</note>
        *   The knowledge of poisons in nature, and how to use them.
        *   Wisdom in all evil arts for the destruction of mankind, using them in contempt of God and for the ruin and destruction of men.
